# Changelog

All notable changes to loom-core will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **spawn: wire Mills spawn pods to the Plan Store over MCP** (`cmd/loom/{proxy_wsbackend,cmd_proxy}.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`): Mills-spawned agent pods had **zero MCP tools** — the generated `~/.codex/config.toml` carried no `[mcp_servers]` block and no `loom` binary shipped in the pod — so a spawned codex agent could not call `agent_plan_get`, blocking plan-store ↔ Mills unification (plan-store S7). The store **is** reachable from spawn pods, but it speaks **MCP over WebSocket at `/ws`** (`mcp-agent-context` deploys with `MCP_TRANSPORT=websocket`; svc port named `websocket`, health `/health`, ready `/ready`) — **not** HTTP/streamable, so a plain codex `url=` transport can't reach it; and the in-cluster gateway's `agent_context` `always_allow` filters out **every** `agent_plan_*` tool (the gateway analog of the !785 proxy-profile fix). Fix: a new `loom proxy --ws-backend <ws-url>` mode (`wsBackendPump`, also `LOOM_WS_BACKEND`) — a thin **single-backend** MCP pass-through bridging the agent's stdio MCP client straight to one upstream MCP server over WebSocket, with **no daemon, no aggregation, and no tool-name namespacing** (so `agent_plan_get` stays un-prefixed and the gateway's plan-tool filter is bypassed). The default proxy handlers speak daemon-only `loom/tools`/`loom/call` and would not work against a raw MCP server, hence a dedicated pump. The generated spawn Dockerfile now bundles the `loom` binary (`COPY --from=<loom-core image> /usr/local/bin/loom`, `SPAWN_LOOM_IMAGE` override) before the `USER agent` switch, and `injectAgentConfig` writes an `[mcp_servers.loom]` stanza for codex (`config.toml`), claude-code (project `.mcp.json` + `enableAllProjectMcpServers`), and gemini (`settings.json` `mcpServers`), each launching `loom proxy --ws-backend ws://mcp-agent-context.loom-hub.svc.cluster.local:8080/ws`. WS URL via `SPAWN_PLAN_STORE_WS_URL` (`disabled`/`off` opts out **and** drops the loom COPY layer). Closes the long-standing TODO in `injectAgentConfig`. Kill-test (load-bearing transport assumption) **PASSED** live against the deployed `mcp-agent-context` (port-forwarded): both the raw mcp-go WS transport and the built `loom proxy --ws-backend` mode — driven through a real stdio MCP handshake (`initialize` → `notifications/initialized` → `tools/call agent_plan_get`) — return the byte-identical kill-test plan (marker `KILLTEST-20260624`, id `plan-plan-store-live-kill-test-2026-06-24-c5d64f`). Deploy verification (a real Mills pod's codex calling `agent_plan_get`) follows the loom-core image build + operator redeploy. Tests: `TestResolvePlanStoreWSURL`, `TestResolveSpawnLoomImage`, `TestLoomBinaryCopyLines`, `TestGenerateDockerfile_BundlesLoom`, `TestLoomMCPServer{TOML,JSON}` (internal/hud); `TestWSBackendPump_ForwardsRequestsAndMatchesIDs`, `TestJSONRPCIDEqual` (cmd/loom).

### Changed
- **HUD: IBM Plex typography — reserve monospace for data** (`internal/hud/frontend/{index.html, src/lib/styles/{theme,layout}.css, src/lib/components/fleet/{EconomicsPanel,FleetStatsGrid,FleetTable}.svelte, src/lib/widgets/AgentCard.svelte}`): swapped the UI typeface from Outfit to **IBM Plex Sans** and JetBrains Mono to **IBM Plex Mono** — a cohesive instrument-panel pairing — loaded via an `index.html` `preconnect` + stylesheet instead of a render-blocking `@import`, with `kern/liga/calt` enabled on `body`. Pulled prose out of monospace so mono now signals data only (numbers, IDs, timestamps, code): economics captions/ratio-hints/legend, the fleet-table grouping toggle + evidence/ungrouped labels, stat sub-captions, and agent-card status/evidence/telemetry chips move to IBM Plex Sans (with `tabular-nums` where they carry figures); ratio values, agent IDs, and counts stay mono. Rebuilt the `go:embed` dist via `pnpm build` (CI does not rebuild it). Verified: `document.fonts` confirms both Plex faces load + apply, `pnpm build` compiles clean, and the built `dist/index.html` references `IBM+Plex+Sans`.

### Fixed
- **mills: deflake `pkg/mills/pipeline` under `-race` + stop the reconciler same-tick double-count (DEBT-079, #176)** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/{runner,adapter}.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, tests, `.gitlab-ci.yml`): the package failed ~75–100% of `go test -race` runs and was quarantined from CI `test:race`. Two root causes. (1) **`Started:2`** — within one `Reconciler.Tick`, the queued-item loop started a run, then `pickupInFlightRuns` (same tick) re-found that still-non-terminal run and re-invoked `Starter.Start`, double-counting `res.Started`. `Runner.Start`'s `active`-guard always prevented an actual second drive, so it was a **count/re-entrancy artifact, not double agent-spend** — but flaky and wrong. Fixed by threading a `startedThisTick` set so the in-flight re-driver skips runs launched earlier in the same tick (they re-drive on the next tick if their goroutine has since exited). (2) **DATA RACE / leaked goroutines** — `Runner.Start` and `RunnerStarter.driveFanOut` run detached goroutines; `Integrator.Run` does `*run = parentRun` on the shared run pointer while tests polled it, and `t.Cleanup` closed the store while drive loops still ran ("database is closed" noise). Fixed by adding deterministic `Runner.Wait()` (`sync.WaitGroup`; fan-out goroutines register on the same group) + `RunnerStarter.Wait()`; tests now join before reading shared state / closing the store, replacing fragile poll loops. The `pkg/mills/pipeline` quarantine is removed from `.gitlab-ci.yml` `test:race`. Verified: `go test -race -short -count=10 ./pkg/mills/pipeline` 10/10 (was 0/4). Regression test: `TestReconciler_TickDoesNotDoubleStartFreshlyQueuedItem`.
- **mills: a stalled-but-alive spawn re-spawns and escalates instead of looping pending forever** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/{runner,dispatcher,error_class}.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`): when a spawn pod stayed alive but never reached a terminal status, `HUDSpawnClient.pollSpawn` returned a non-terminal error with `SpawnID` set; `runStage` parked the stage **pending** and `Drive` treated `errStagePending` as a no-op, never burning the retry budget. Every reconciler tick (`pickupInFlightRuns`) re-attached to the same dead spawn (`resumeSpawnID`), re-polled to the 30-minute `PollDeadline`, and re-parked — so the run looped pending forever (observed 2026-06-25: a run sat `pending@implement` ~13h on `spawn-2ab680e9275a` until the orphan pod was deleted by hand). The HUD-side `livenessStallTimeout` didn't catch it: `runLivenessWatcher` is process-local to the exec stream, only trips on output *silence*, and dies with its goroutine on HUD restart — it never re-attaches to an orphaned pod, so the durable backstop belongs operator-side. Fix: a new sentinel `pipeline.ErrSpawnPollTimeout` is wrapped on every `pollSpawn` deadline-exceeded return; `Classify` maps it to `ClassTransient` (free retry, but every attempt still counts toward the `maxAttempts+transientRetryCap` hard cap so a persistently hung substrate escalates rather than looping); and `runStage` counts consecutive poll-timeouts per attempt (persisted in the pending row's artifacts, JSON round-trip safe) — below `maxConsecutiveSpawnPollTimeouts=2` it keeps the resume-safe "check again next tick" behavior, and at/above it falls through to the error path so a fresh spawn re-dispatches and the run ultimately escalates. Non-timeout interruptions (operator restart mid-poll, transient GET error) still park pending unchanged. Tests: `TestRunner_StalledSpawnEscalatesInsteadOfLoopingPending` (drives the reconciler loop against a perpetually-stalling spawn; asserts escalation + fresh re-spawns rather than unbounded pending), `TestRunner_SinglePollTimeoutStillParksPending` (legit slow-but-progressing spawn still parks, no budget burn), and `TestRun_PollDeadlineExceeded` extended to pin `errors.Is(err, ErrSpawnPollTimeout)`.
- **proxy: expose the `agent_plan_*` tools in the `llm-core`/`antigravity-core` profiles so codex/antigravity can resolve plans** (`cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go`): the loom proxy shapes tools/list per client — codex and antigravity get a curated `selectCoreDeveloperTools` set (`--tool-profile llm-core --max-tools 140`, antigravity 100). The `requiredPatterns` allowlist had the full agent_context session/recall/task/handoff/worktree/presence family but **none of the plan-store tools**, so `agent_plan_get` and the slice tools were filtered out for every profile-limited vendor. A live cross-vendor kill-test (2026-06-24) caught it: a plan created from Claude was byte-identical-resolvable from a Claude subagent, but a `codex exec` call **could not see `agent_plan_get`** and silently fabricated an approximate answer from fuzzy `agent_context_recall` instead of erroring — exactly the worktree-resilient, cross-agent resolution the plan store exists to provide, broken for codex/Mills. Added `agent_plan_get/list/create/update/lifecycle_advance` + `agent_plan_slice_get/list/update/claim` to `requiredPatterns` (placed right after the agent_context block so they land within even antigravity's 100-tool window). Re-ran the codex leg after rebuilding the binary: `loom/agent_context__agent_plan_get` now resolves byte-identical (`TITLE=Plan Store live kill-test 2026-06-24 CREATEDBY=claude-code`). Deploy: takes effect for new codex sessions after the `loom` binary refresh (`~/go/bin` + `~/.local/bin`). Tests: `TestFilterProxyTools_{AntigravityCore,LLMCore}Defaults` extended to assert the plan tools survive both profiles.
- **mills: scope the devbox `tests`-stage quality gate to `fmt` for all items so real (non-canary) work isn't false-failed** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`): the hub-spawned devbox sandbox clones only `services/loom-core`, but loom-core's `go.work` `use`s sibling repos (`../../libs/fi-accel/go/fiaccel`, `fi-mcp-kit`, `mcp-go`) and fi-accel is cgo — so whole-module `go vet ./...` / `go test ./...` fail immediately (~79ms, `directory ../../libs/... does not exist`). Canary items already scoped to `fmt` for this reason; non-canary items used the gate default (`fmt+lint+test`), so the **first real autonomous-merge attempt** (`MILLS-DEBT-TICKLABEL-20260624`, a unit-test addition) escalated at the `tests` stage on `PASS fmt / FAIL lint (79ms)` despite the change being correct (GitLab `test:unit` passed, all 6 post-implement gates passed). `devboxScopeFor` now returns the sandbox-safe `[fmt]` scope for every item; the authoritative lint/vet/test/build runs in GitLab CI, which the `ci_watch` stage already gates before merge, so no merge safety is lost. A richer pre-flight that runs the changed packages with `GOWORK=off` is a follow-up. Test: `TestDevboxWorker_NonCanaryScopesToFmt` (with `TestDevboxWorker_CanaryScopesChecks` still green).
- **HUD: codex worktree-canonicalization also covers the hooks.json mint path (completes the prior fix)** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`): the earlier fix canonicalized codex's `WS_HASH` in the `config.toml` notify mint and the Go proxy, but codex's `hooks.json` events mint a *scoped* `codex-<WS_HASH>-<SESSION_SCOPE>` id through the shared `hookAgentIDBootstrap`, which still hashed the raw `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree) path. In a worktree that left the scoped hooks.json id keyed on the worktree hash while the scopeless notify/proxy ids used the canonical-repo hash — so codex would **split into two conversation rows within a single worktree** (`conversationId` keeps the `WS_HASH` for codex). `hookAgentIDBootstrap` now injects `worktreeCanonRootShell` before the `WS_HASH` cksum **only for `agentID == "codex"`**, so all three codex mint paths agree on the canonical hash and fold across (and within) worktrees; conversation-scoped vendors (claude/gemini) are byte-for-byte unchanged and never receive the canon snippet (it is unsafe for Gemini's `settings.json` `${...}` interpolator). Test: `TestHookAgentIDBootstrap_CodexCanonicalizesWorktreeRoot` (string + ordering + `sh -n` parse, asserts claude/gemini untouched).
- **HUD: codex agent identity is stable across worktrees of one repo (no more per-worktree orphan rows)** (`pkg/generator/{configs_hooks,configs_codex}.go`, `cmd/loom/proxy_heartbeat.go`): codex is workspace-anchored — its lifecycle hook mints `codex-<WS_HASH>` with no `SESSION_SCOPE`, and `conversationId`/`ConversationID` keep the `WS_HASH` for codex (`WORKSPACE_ANCHORED_BASES`). `WS_HASH` was the `cksum` of `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, i.e. the **worktree** path, so one codex app working across a repo's main checkout and its linked worktrees (`<repo>/.worktrees/<b>`, `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/<b>`) fragmented into a separate, unjoinable conversation row per worktree — and the proxy/session base-match couldn't reconcile across the divergent hashes (the open "worktree divergence → different `WS_HASH`" limitation noted in the `IsBaseOf` fix below). The namespace derivation (`hookNamespaceVars`) already collapsed those two worktree layouts to the main repo, but the identity hash did not. Fix: a shared `worktreeCanonRootShell` snippet rewrites `$WS_ROOT` to the main repo root **before** the `WS_HASH` cksum in the codex notify mint, and the Go proxy mirror (`workspaceProxyAgentIDFor`, gated on `agentType == "codex"`) applies the existing `stripWorktreeFromRepoRoot` to the same effect — so codex folds all worktrees of one repo to a single id while conversation-scoped vendors (claude/gemini) stay worktree-specific to keep matching their unchanged shell bootstrap. Namespace output is unchanged (nsVars reads the now-canonical `$WS_ROOT` and yields the same `NS_PROJECT`). Deploy: takes effect after `loom sync codex --regen` + a new `loom` binary; existing worktree-hashed codex sessions age out. Tests: `TestWorktreeCanonRootShell_StableHashAcrossWorktrees` and `TestCodexNotifyCommand_CanonicalizesWorktreeRoot` (shell-exec proof + wiring/ordering guard), `TestWorkspaceProxyAgentIDFor_CodexFoldsWorktree` (codex folds, claude/gemini do not).
- **mills: reconciler increments the pipeline_run attempt on re-queue so escalation auto-retry isn't wedged** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`): `tryStart` hardcoded `Attempts: 1` when creating a `pipeline_run`. The Slice-3d escalation auto-retry path (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go` `maybeAutoRetry`) marks a transiently-escalated run `escalated` but leaves the backlog item `queued` so the reconciler spins up a fresh run on the next tick — that fresh insert collided with the prior run on the `pipeline_runs` `UNIQUE(backlog_id, attempts)` constraint (migration `001_initial.sql:74`), so `PutRun` failed **every tick** and the item wedged `queued` forever. Observed live 2026-06-24: `mills_reconciler_ticks_total{outcome="errored"}` was the only label emitted (climbing ~1.4/min) and two stale canary backlog items (`MILLS-CANARY-20260619-135827`/`-134843`) had been erroring on every tick for 5 days across operator restarts (state persisted on the PVC) — silently disabling the entire auto-retry resilience feature. `tryStart` now computes the next attempt = `max(existing attempts) + 1` via `PipelineDAO.ListByBacklog`; the retry ceiling stays enforced upstream in `maybeAutoRetry` (`EscalationAutoRetryCap`), so attempt numbers remain bounded. Test: `TestReconciler_RequeuedItemStartsNextAttempt` (seeds an escalated `attempts=1` run on a queued item, asserts the next tick starts a fresh `attempts=2` run instead of erroring).
- **HUD: reconcile background proxy/mirror identity to the real session; unstick the trace drawer** (`internal/hud/fleetview/{identity,fleetview}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_session.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/SessionDetail.svelte`): the session-detail drawer's "Recent Traces" stayed pinned on "Loading recent traces…" forever because the loading state read the **global** `traceStore.loading` poll flag, which is decoupled from the per-session trace fetch — a session with zero traces never fell through to "No recent traces" while the global poll was reloading; now gated on the per-session fetch (`loadingEntries`). Separately, the background proxy heartbeat (`cmd/loom/proxy_heartbeat.go`) and federation mirror (`internal/hud/mirror/mirror.go`) register a **scopeless workspace-base** identity `<type>-<WS_HASH>` while the CLI hooks own a **scoped** session `<type>-<WS_HASH>-<SCOPE>`. Exact-only matching (`fleetview.Join`, `GetActiveSession`) surfaced the base id as a bogus "NO ACTIVE SESSION MATCH" orphan and, with `ensure_session=true`, forked a phantom `agents/<id>` session — a background wakeup of the wrong session. New directional `fleetview.IsBaseOf` (base must be the shorter, scopeless id, so two distinct scoped conversations in one workspace never merge) lets `Join` reconcile the base presence to the most-recent active scoped session before flagging an orphan, and `ensureHeartbeatSession` base-matches and reuses an existing active session instead of bootstrapping a twin (truly hook-less agents still bootstrap). Known limitation: when proxy and hooks resolve different git toplevels (worktree divergence → different `WS_HASH`) there is no shared sub-id to link them, so reconciliation can't fire. Rebuilt the `go:embed` dist via `pnpm build`. Tests: `TestIsBaseOf`, `TestJoin_ProxyBaseReconcilesToScopedSession`, `TestJoin_DifferentWSHashStaysOrphan`.
- **mills/spawn: liveness watchdog fails a zombie spawn pod instead of waiting out the operator's poll deadline** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/spawn_telemetry.go`): a spawned agent (codex) sometimes dies on its first `implement` attempt while its pod stays in `Phase=Running` — a "zombie pod" (container PID 1 alive, agent process dead). The spawn controller maps `Running → status=running` indefinitely (`internal/spawn/controller.go`), so the Mills operator's `pollSpawn` (`pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`) never saw a terminal status: it blocked up to its 30-minute `PollDeadline` holding the run's `active` lock (so `pickupInFlightRuns` no-op'd it), and only an **operator restart** (`ResumeInFlightRuns`) re-spawned — the manual nudge every autonomous run needed ~2 of 3 times, gating the `autonomous_merges_24h` north-star. Root cause: nothing checked agent **liveness**, only pod **existence**. Fix: the `SpawnTelemetryAccumulator` now tracks `lastActivity`, stamped via `Touch()` from the streaming exec's per-line callback (every flight-deck JSONL line proves the process is alive); a new `runLivenessWatcher` (modeled on the existing budget watcher) cancels the exec context with `errSpawnStalled` when no output arrives for `LivenessStallTimeout` (default **15m**, env override `LOOM_SPAWN_LIVENESS_STALL_TIMEOUT`). The finalize path reads the cancellation cause (`context.WithCancelCause`) and calls `failSpawn`, which already cleans the pod → the operator's poll sees a terminal failure and auto-retries instead of wedging. Streaming (K8s) path only — the buffered harvester-vm path has no mid-flight telemetry and keeps its own `Exec` `TimeoutSec`. Tests: `TestRunLivenessWatcher_{TripsOnStall,StaysAliveWhileActive,ExitsOnDone}`, `TestSpawnTelemetryAccumulator_{LastActivityInitialised,TouchAdvancesLastActivity}`.
- **mills: `scope` gate exempts slice-less canaries before the empty-slices check** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): the `post_implement_gate` `scope` check failed with `backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce` and escalated deterministic canaries whose backlog item carried **zero slices** (e.g. when `plan_slice` fails to persist one) — observed on `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260623-235142`, where the implement work was correct (branch pushed, diff = only `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md`) yet the run looped to max attempts and escalated because retrying `implement` can never add a slice. Root cause: the `CanaryLabel` carve-out that seeds `canaryAllowedPaths` (added for escalations #151/#163) ran **after** the `allowed.empty()` guard, so a slice-less canary failed before the carve-out could exempt the fixture path. The carve-out now runs **before** the empty check, so a slice-less canary enforces the narrow `canaryAllowedPaths` allowlist instead of escalating; non-canary slice-less items still fail (blast radius unchanged). Test: `TestScope_CanaryNoSlicesHeartbeatAllowed` (with `TestScope_NoSlicesFails` and `TestScope_CanaryHeartbeatStillFailsForNonCanaryItems` still green).
- **mills: merge stage recovers the detached `merge_request_event` head pipeline (autonomous-merge `405`)** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): the `405` survives the no-MWPS-at-create and branch-poll fixes below. GitLab attaches a spurious 0-job `failed` `merge_request_event` pipeline as the MR `head_pipeline` on MR-open **even with** `workflow.rules: merge_request_event → never` and no `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds` — the rule suppresses the jobs but GitLab still records the failed pipeline and pins it as head, so under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` the merge `405`s forever (`detailed_merge_status=ci_must_pass`). Verified live on canary MR !770 (2026-06-23): the run reached a green `ci_watch` yet the merge stage still escalated. `Merge()` now probes once, and on a `405` inspects `head_pipeline.source`: a persistent **detached-head** `405` triggers `recoverDetachedHead` (delete the `merge_request_event` pipeline for the head SHA, then close+reopen the MR so head re-points to the green push pipeline), then retries; a **benign timing** `405` (normal head pipeline still settling) is polled away without close/reopen. The delete is the load-bearing precondition — close+reopen alone re-attaches the same failed pipeline. Tests: `TestMerge_RecoversDetachedHeadOn405`, `TestMerge_BenignTiming405DoesNotCloseReopen`.
- **mills: `ci_watch` polls the branch pipeline for the MR SHA, ignoring the spurious `merge_request_event` placeholder** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): `PollPipeline` previously polled `mr.head_pipeline`. When an MR is opened on a repo whose `workflow.rules` block `merge_request_event` pipelines (loom-core runs branch pipelines), GitLab briefly attaches a 0-job `merge_request_event` pipeline as the head and immediately fails it (then deletes it) — the `ci_watch` poll raced that transient and reported `failed`, escalating the run (the long-standing autonomous-merge blocker #147/#148/#150). `PollPipeline` now resolves the most recent **non-`merge_request_event`** pipeline for the MR's head SHA (`GET /pipelines?sha=`) and polls that, so the placeholder is ignored and a still-only-placeholder state keeps waiting rather than failing. Pairs with disabling the project's Auto DevOps (which made the placeholder transient instead of persistent). Tests: `TestPollPipeline_TerminatesOnSuccess` (ignores a listed placeholder), `…_IgnoresMergeRequestEventPlaceholder` (placeholder-only → wait, not fail), `…_FailedTerminal`, `…_TimeoutWhenNoSHA`.
- **mills: don't arm `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds` at MR-create so the autonomous merge isn't blocked by an empty MR pipeline** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): the operator's `mr` stage created the MR with `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds: req.AutoMerge`. Opening an MR with MWPS armed makes GitLab spin up a detached merge-request pipeline; on a repo whose `workflow.rules` block `merge_request_event` pipelines (loom-core runs branch pipelines), GitLab empties it into a **`failed`, 0-job** placeholder that becomes the MR `head_pipeline` and blocks merge with **HTTP 405** under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` — the long-standing autonomous-merge `405` blocker (#147/#148/#150). Operator-created (bot) MRs hit this; human MRs created plain (then armed separately) never did. `CreateMR` now omits MWPS entirely, so the MR `head_pipeline` is the green branch pipeline; the operator's explicit `merge` stage (`runMerge → Merge`, already a 405-retry immediate merge) performs the autonomous merge after `ci_watch` confirms green. Test `TestCreateMR_AutoMergeDoesNotArmMWPSOnCreate` (was `…SetsMergeWhenPipelineSucceeds`) now asserts the create body omits the flag even when `AutoMerge=true`. (The earlier `.gitlab-ci.yml` `test:race` rule removal was necessary cleanup but not the root cause — the empty pipeline is created by the MWPS-on-create, not job rules.)
- **ci: drop the dead `merge_request_event` rule from `test:race` so MR merges aren't blocked by an empty MR pipeline** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`): the top-level `workflow.rules` blocks all `merge_request_event` pipelines (this repo runs branch pipelines), but `test:race` still carried `rules: - if: $CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE == "merge_request_event"`. That made it the one MR-pipeline-eligible job, so opening an MR spawned a *merge request pipeline* that the workflow then emptied into a **`failed`, 0-job pipeline** — which became the MR's `head_pipeline` and blocked merge with a **HTTP 405** under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` (the green branch pipeline was ignored). This is the long-standing Mills autonomous-merge `405` blocker (#147/#148/#150): the canary reached `mr` then `ci_watch`/merge always failed on the spurious pipeline. The rule was vestigial — the job only ever ran on `main` because the workflow blocks MR pipelines — so removing it is a no-op for when `test:race` runs, but stops the empty MR pipeline from being created. MR head pipelines are now the branch pipeline, so `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` gates on real CI.
- **mcp-devbox: quality gate awaits the async sandbox build instead of failing instantly** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate.go`): on the K8s backend a cold/stale sandbox triggers an asynchronous image build that returns a `buildInProgressError` immediately (so quick `devbox_exec` calls don't hang). `handleQualityGate` surfaced that as `ensure sandbox: ...`, which the Mills operator's `tests` stage recorded as an opaque `devbox quality gate failed: 0 checks` and retried within **~0.1s** — exhausting its 3 attempts long before a cold build (`go mod download`, apk installs, image push; minutes) could finish. The Mills A2 canary consequently passed `plan_slice → research → implement` and every post-implement gate but always died at `tests`, blocking the `autonomous_merges_24h` north-star. The gate now calls `ensureRunningAwaitBuild`, which polls `ensureRunning` with bounded exponential backoff (up to 8m) past the build-in-progress signal — so the first gate call waits for the build, then runs real checks; a genuine build/start failure is surfaced (no longer masked as `0 checks`). Wait/backoff/timeout logic extracted to `awaitSandboxBuild` with tests (`TestAwaitSandboxBuild`: completes-after-building, passthrough of non-building errors, timeout-while-building, context cancellation).
- **codebase-memory: revert workspace volume to `emptyDir` to unblock the `loom-hub-servers` Flux reconciliation** (`k8s/base/servers/codebase-memory/deployment.yaml`): a prior incomplete fix pointed codebase-memory's workspace volume at the `loom-hub-workspace-nfs` PVC, but that NFS export (`storage-vm-linux-nas-pilot`, `192.168.50.211`) was halted in the 2026-06-21 storage-fault incident. Worse, the live pod still had an `emptyDir` volume of the same name co-owned by a stray `kubectl-patch` field manager, so Flux's server-side apply could not replace it with the PVC (`volumes[0]: may not specify more than 1 volume type`) — the dry-run failed and **aborted the entire `loom-hub-servers` Kustomization**, freezing it at revision `8463b1fe` and blocking *every* loom-hub server deploy (including the mobile-hud workspace fix above). Reverted the volume to `emptyDir` (matching the live pod) so the same-type apply succeeds and reconciliation resumes. codebase-memory keeps indexing an empty workspace exactly as it does today; repointing it at a healthy Longhorn workspace volume is a follow-up.
- **mobile-hud: mount a workspace volume so Mills spawns can resolve projects** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): the spawn orchestrator's project resolver (`internal/hud/spawn.go` → `resolveProjectPath`, `wsRoot = $HOME/workspace = /home/mcp/workspace`) reads a repo on disk to fingerprint + generate the Dockerfile **before** the spawn pod git-clones, but the mobile-hud Deployment never mounted a workspace volume — the live pod had a read-only empty `emptyDir` at `/home/mcp/workspace`. Every Mills spawn stage (`plan_slice`/`implement`) then failed with `project "services/loom-core" not found under /home/mcp/workspace (searched services, libs, platform, private, labs)`. Now mounts the `loom-hub-workspace` PVC (read-only) at `/home/mcp/workspace`. This is the **healthy Longhorn replacement** (storageClass `loom-ws-1r`) for the prior `loom-hub-workspace-nfs` export on `storage-vm-linux-nas-pilot` (`192.168.50.211`), which was halted during the 2026-06-21 storage-fault incident (two dying SAS disks) — a fresh pod doing a `hard` NFS mount against the down server would hang on mount. Because the Longhorn volume is `ReadWriteOnce`, the Deployment also switches to the `Recreate` strategy so the old pod releases the volume before the new one attaches (a `RollingUpdate` surge pod would deadlock on the RWO mount). Volume-only change (no image rebuild); Flux rolls the pod.
- **weaver: record metrics in the embedded daemon + instrument tool-response compression so the F8 economics card shows real ratios** (`internal/daemon/weaver_embed.go`, `pkg/weaver/{metrics,telemetry,router,tokens}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/economics.go`): two root causes left the Token Economics card full of `STUB`/`INSUFFICIENT DATA`/degenerate values even after the unreachable fix. (1) `startEmbeddedWeaver` never called `router.SetMetrics()`, so the daemon's weaver Router had `metrics == nil` — `loom/weaver/metrics` always returned all-zeros (so "Local util." stuck at 0 and lifetime counters never accumulated). Now wired with `weaver.NewMetrics(nil)`, mirroring the standalone `cmd/mcp-weaver`. (2) The `token_savings`, `compression`, and `context_waste` ratios depended on `ToolResponseTokens`/`WeaverResponseTokens` that **nothing ever populated**. Weaver now counts raw tool-response tokens in the subagent telemetry sink (`RecordToolCall`, previously discarded the result) and compressed-answer tokens per successful query, exposes them via `raw_tool_response_tokens`/`weaver_response_tokens` in the metrics summary, and the HUD threads them into the economics inputs. `context_waste` now reports `insufficient_data` when no tool tokens have accrued instead of a misleading `0.00`. Token estimate uses a local chars/4 heuristic (matches `pkg/openairesponses` fallback). Tests: metrics summary + record nil/zero-safety, telemetry raw-token counting (incl. error path), economics input wiring + context-waste honesty.
- **HUD: wire weaver metrics into the F8 token-economics card** (`internal/hud/domain_adapters_fleet.go`): `fleetDepsAdapter.WeaverMetrics()` was a hard stub returning `(zero, false)` ("Threading a weaver fetcher through here is a follow-up slice"), so the Operations → Fleet **Token Economics** card permanently showed `WEAVER METRICS UNREACHABLE` on the "Local util." ratio even though weaver was healthy. The adapter now fetches `loom/weaver/metrics` over the same daemon IPC bridge (`app.client`) the weaver HUD domain already uses, mapping `total_queries`/`total_tokens` into the economics view; a parse failure or nil bridge still yields `weaver_metrics_unreachable`, while a well-formed zero-counter payload is treated as reachable (`insufficient_data` = on-but-quiet). Parse extracted into `weaverMetricsViewFromJSON` with tests (`domain_adapters_fleet_test.go`) against the real `pkg/weaver.Metrics.Summary()` shape.
- **ci: stop the manual `ios:archive-export` job from blocking every main pipeline** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`): the iOS archive job is `when: manual` on the default branch but lacked `allow_failure: true`, making it a *blocking* manual job — so main pipelines never reached `success`, sitting in `blocked/manual` indefinitely. The server `build:image:*` jobs run `on_success` regardless (deploys were never actually gated), but the blocked status confused operators and any pipeline-success automation. Added `allow_failure: true` to both manual rules so the pipeline goes green on its own while the iOS archive stays manually triggerable for real App Store builds.
- **HUD: render em-dash/warn glyphs as text + mute malformed namespaces in the Dispatch roster** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{DispatchPanel,MemoryPanel,CatalogPanel}.svelte`): the Operations → Dispatch agent roster rendered the literal string `—` in the **BLOCKERS** and **MERGE** columns (visible on the live HUD). Those `\uXXXX` escapes were written directly in Svelte **markup text nodes**, where Svelte does not interpret JS string escapes — only the `{'—'}` expression form (used correctly elsewhere in the same table) does. The identical latent defect existed in `MemoryPanel`'s error-banner warning icon (`⚠` text node) and `CatalogPanel`'s FilterBar placeholder (`…` in a **static attribute**, also not escape-decoded). Each is now wrapped in an expression/attribute binding so the glyph renders. Additionally, the Dispatch roster rendered `agent.namespace` **raw**, so the malformed shapes the minting fix targets (`////main`, `agents/<id>`, `/` — see the hooks `NS_PROJECT` guard below) showed as garbage rows instead of being muted; it now uses the shared `parseNamespace()` (`utils/namespace.ts`) like `FleetTable`, rendering synthetic/malformed namespaces as a muted `—` with a tooltip and splitting real ones into repo + branch. Display-side only; the namespace-minting root cause is fixed at the lifecycle hooks. Verified: `pnpm build` compiles clean (validates the `{@const}` placement), `make hud-dist-check` reports dist up-to-date, `tsx namespace.fixture.ts` passes (covers the reused `parseNamespace` logic). Rebuilt the `go:embed` dist (CI does not rebuild it).
- **devbox: fix a data race in the async build tracker** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/builds.go`): `buildTracker.startOrJoin` copied the build entry after releasing the lock and after launching the build goroutine (which writes `done`/`err` under the lock), an unsynchronized read/write the race detector flagged. This reddened `test:race` on `main`, which gates the deploy stage — so post-merge `build:image:*` jobs were skipped, blocking cluster image rollouts. The snapshot is now taken while still holding the lock. Tests: `TestBuildTracker_DoneTransition`/`RestartsAfterDone` pass under `-race`.
- **weaver: forward `parent_session_id` through the `loom/weaver/query` RPC** (`internal/daemon/daemon_dispatch_weaver.go`): the daemon handler decoded query params into a local struct that omitted `parent_session_id` and never set `weaver.QueryRequest.ParentSessionID`, silently breaking weaver session stitching. The Mills weaver delegator POSTs `parent_session_id` (= pipeline run ID) to `/api/weaver/query` and the HUD forwards it, but the daemon dropped it — so the agent-context recorder recorded an empty parent (queries could not be joined to the originating run) and the spawn bridge never propagated `LOOM_PARENT_SESSION_ID` to domain pods (no HUD grouping). The wire→`QueryRequest` mapping is extracted into `weaverQueryRequestFromParams` as the single source of truth (now including `parent_session_id`), with a regression test (`daemon_dispatch_weaver_test.go`) asserting every field round-trips. `session_id` was already forwarded, so the break was partial and easy to miss.
- **agentcontext/hooks: stop minting phantom `project` keys from non-workspace namespaces** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`, `pkg/projectmeta/projectmeta.go`): the lifecycle-hook namespace minting (`hookNamespaceVars`, shared by Claude + Codex) derived `NS_PROJECT` purely from the filesystem path (`basename(dirname(WS_ROOT))/basename(WS_ROOT)`) and never validated the result. A degenerate `WS_ROOT` — a detached keepalive where `git rev-parse` failed and PWD is `/`, or an agent running OUTSIDE a workspace repo (`~`, `~/workspace`) — minted garbage 2-level values (`Users/cblevins`, `5f1b/fi-fhir`, `cblevins/workspace`) that canonicalize to phantom project keys (`Users`, `5f1b`, `cblevins`), and the synthetic `agents/<id>` heartbeat fallback canonicalized to `agents`. A live 40-session sweep (2026-06-20) showed a large fraction of the fleet carrying these — so the flexdeck `/projects` federation (flexdeck!197) would scatter real work across phantom buckets. The server can't reject them (`FromNamespace` intentionally supports the bare-repo form `loom-core` → `loom-core`, structurally identical to `Users` → `Users`), so the fix is at minting: a `case` guard keeps `NS_PROJECT` only when rooted at a real workspace bucket (`apps|labs|libs|platform|private|services`), else empties it so the namespace is an honest project-less `/<branch>` (the HUD's `parseNamespace` already mutes the leading-empty form to "—", and the PM view excludes rather than mis-buckets it). Healthy workspace-rooted namespaces are untouched. `projectmeta.workspaceNamespaceRoots` gains `labs` + `private` (the set was stale vs AGENTS.md) so the hook allowlist and the server agree and legitimate `labs/*`/`private/*` work buckets correctly. Takes effect after `loom sync {claude,codex} --regen` + a new `loom` binary; old malformed sessions age out. Diagnosis in `.loom/plan-task-integration-pm-2026-06-20.md` (Slice 2). Tests: `projectmeta_test.go` (labs/private 2-level, `////main` → empty); `configs_hooks_test.go` `TestHookNamespaceVars_WorkspaceRootGuard` (executes the snippet across 9 WS_ROOT shapes incl. worktree layouts + degenerate roots).
- **embed: send explicit `encoding_format: "float"` so the flexinfer gte-Qwen2 fallback stops 500ing** (`pkg/codebase/embed/morph.go`, `pkg/codebase/embed/flexinfer_test.go`): the OpenAI-compatible embeddings client (shared by the Morph primary and the flexinfer `gte-qwen2-1.5b` write-path fallback) omitted `encoding_format`. litellm then forwards `encoding_format: null` to the backend, and llama.cpp's OpenAI-compat server (b8173) rejects a null with HTTP 500 `[json.exception.type_error.302] type must be string, but is null` — so once flexinfer!673 fixed the `--pooling last` 400, the agent-context write-path fallback hit a 500 instead. Sending the OpenAI default (`"float"`) explicitly makes the path return a valid 1536-dim vector (verified live end-to-end via `litellm.flexinfer.ai`); harmless to Morph, which already defaults to float. Pairs with flexinfer!651 (`--pooling last` operator fix) and flexinfer!673 (gfx906 runtime rebuild carrying it). Tests: `flexinfer_test.go` asserts `encoding_format=float` on both the query and document request paths.
- **agentcontext: decouple `agent_task_add` from embedding so an embedder outage never blanks the PM view's task lane** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_tasks.go`): task creation called `embedr.EmbedDocuments` and **hard-returned on any error** (`return mcp.ErrorResult(fmt.Errorf("embedding tasks: %w", err))`), unlike the `pkg/pm` risk store which persists a deterministic fallback vector when embedding fails. With the shared embedder down (live 2026-06-20: `gte-qwen2-1.5b` HTTP 500, `type must be string, but is null` — an OAI pooling incompat), **no agent could create any task**, the reconciler GC drained `agent_tasks_v1` to `points_count: 0`, and flexdeck's `/projects` federation (flexdeck!197) showed an empty task lane for every project. Embedding is now best-effort: on embedder error or a count mismatch the write logs a warning and proceeds, sizing the collection from a returned vector → the shared discovered size → the live collection (`GetCollectionVectorSize`) → the 1536 default, and any task lacking a usable, correctly-sized embedding gets a deterministic non-zero `fallbackEmbedVector` (Qdrant rejects all-zero vectors under cosine distance) so it still persists and stays payload-filterable by `project`/`session_id`/`status`. Semantic search over un-embedded tasks is degraded until the embedder recovers, but persistence and the PM-view federation never are. Discovered by the Slice-1 kill-test of `.loom/plan-task-integration-pm-2026-06-20.md` (the stamping logic itself is sound — sessions already carry a canonical `project`). Pairs with the gte-qwen2 fallback-embedder lane and the upstream `--pooling last` operator fix (flexinfer!651). Tests: `TestTaskAdd_EmbedFailureStillPersists` (failing embedder → task still upserts with a non-zero fallback vector and a stamped `project`).
- **devbox: drain stdout in tar-pipe sync to prevent an SPDY flow-control deadlock** (`internal/devbox/backend/k8s_sync.go`): `pipeTarIntoPod` requested a stdout stream (`PodExecOptions.Stdout=true`) but passed no `Stdout` reader in `StreamOptions`. stdin and stdout share the SPDY connection's flow-control window, so with stdout never drained, a payload larger than the initial window back-pressures the stdin send — `tar xzf` never receives the full archive, never exits, and `StreamWithContext` blocks until the context timeout. Small/empty syncs fit the initial window and slipped through, so the bug only hit large workspaces (e.g. loom-core, ~79 MB / 4k files), presenting as a ~timeout-long `devbox_exec` hang on otherwise-healthy nodes. Root-caused via a `SIGQUIT` goroutine dump (`spdyStreamExecutor.StreamWithContext` ← `pipeTarIntoPod`). Fix: pass `Stdout: io.Discard` so the stdout stream is drained. Verified live — the hang is eliminated and the sync completes.
- **devbox: exclude agent/editor state dirs from the tar-pipe sync** (`internal/devbox/backend/k8s_sync.go`, `scripts/devbox-sync.sh`): the workspace sync did not exclude agent/editor tooling state (`.claude`, `.codex`, `.gemini`, `.antigravity`, `.kilocode`, `.cursor`). In an active multi-agent checkout these accumulate large worktree roots — notably `.claude/worktrees`, each a full source checkout — which pushed the tar payload past the 200 MB cap and made `devbox_exec` fail with `sync payload exceeds 200 MB limit` on the repos agents work in most (observed: loom-core at a 9.8 GB working tree, ~600 MB of `.claude` worktree source alone). These dirs are never a build input; excluding them keeps the sync bounded regardless of how much agent state accumulates. The Go `defaultSyncExcludes` and the `scripts/devbox-sync.sh` rsync list are kept aligned. Tests: `TestAddDirToTar_ExcludesAgentState`.
- **devbox: build sandbox images asynchronously so the first `devbox_exec` never hangs** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/{builds.go (new),manager.go,handlers.go}`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`): the first `devbox_exec` on a project (cold start or changed fingerprint) called `backend.Build` synchronously inside `ensureRunning`. K8s sandbox builds run `RUN go mod download`, apk installs, and an image push that routinely take **5–15+ min** (observed live) — far past the 2m exec / proxy recv timeout — so the call hung and surfaced a cryptic `tools/call timeout`. The build finished in the background, so a later retry "magically" worked: exactly what trips agents up. For the K8s backend the build now runs in a detached goroutine tracked by a `buildTracker` (dedupes concurrent callers per image tag) and `ensureRunning` returns `{"status":"building", ...}` immediately so the agent re-runs shortly; subsequent calls report progress or fall through to `Start` once the image is ready. Docker / harvester-vm keep the synchronous path (fast / no-op builds); gate via `LOOM_DEVBOX_ASYNC_BUILD` (default on for k8s). Builds use a dedicated `buildWg` so a multi-minute build never stalls daemon shutdown (the K8s build is detached cluster-side). Also enables a warm pool for `loom-core` (`DEVBOX_WARM_PROJECTS`) so the common project's image is pre-built and kept fresh off the agent's critical path. Tests: `builds_test.go` (tracker dedup, done/error transitions, restart-after-done, sentinel detection, building-result rendering).
- **daemon: tear down a stalled local stdio transport after repeated recv timeouts** (`internal/daemon/{callpipeline_errors.go,callpipeline_recv_breaker.go (new),daemon.go}`): on a local recv timeout the pipeline keeps the subprocess + shared `muxstdio` transport alive (so one slow tool call doesn't drop every concurrent caller). But when the *shared transport itself* desyncs or goes stale (subprocess idle on `read`, or loomd holding a transport to a replaced process), every subsequent request hangs the full timeout and never recovers — the server appears permanently "unavailable" (observed live on devbox: the subprocess was idle on `read(stdin)` while loomd's `prompts/list` priming timed out at 30s). A new per-server consecutive-recv-timeout circuit breaker keeps the subprocess alive for the first N-1 timeouts, then on the Nth (default 3, `LOOM_LOCAL_RECV_TIMEOUT_BREAKER`) escalates to the existing full teardown (`ClearServer` + `stopServerProc` + `runningServers.Delete`) so the next request dials a fresh channel. A successful local recv resets the streak. Tests: `callpipeline_recv_breaker_test.go` (counter helpers, keep-alive for N-1 then teardown on Nth, success resets streak); existing hub recv/send-timeout tests unchanged (breaker is local-gated).

### Added
- **daemon: auto-route LAN-pinned MCP servers through the hub when off-LAN** (`pkg/netmode/{netmode,netmode_test}.go`, `internal/daemon/{routing,daemon_new}.go`, `internal/daemon/routing_offlan_test.go`): off the home LAN, local MCP backends that depend on LAN-only services (qdrant `192.168.50.176`, `*.lan`, k8s) are unreachable — so `agent_context` (pinned `local-only`) and `gitlab` (`prefer-local`) silently break, taking "standard agent work" memory/sessions with them. New `pkg/netmode` resolves on/off-LAN (`LOOM_NETWORK_MODE=lan|tunnel|auto`, default auto = a fast TCP dial of `LOOM_LAN_SENTINEL`, default the home ingress VIP `192.168.50.227:443`). At daemon start, when a hub is configured and the host is off-LAN, `applyOffLANRouting` upgrades explicit `local-only`/`prefer-local` pins to `prefer-hub` for hub-capable servers (registry local-only servers like godot/browserkit are never upgraded; servers with no explicit pin keep their health-based default, whose local→hub fallback already covers off-LAN). The hub (`wss://mcp.flexinfer.ai/ws`) runs those backends cluster-side and is reachable through Cloudflare Access from anywhere. Re-detected at each daemon start (a restart adapts after the laptop moves networks). Tests: `netmode` env-override + reachable/unreachable auto-probe; `applyOffLANRouting` flip (agent_context/gitlab → prefer-hub) leaving hub-only/health-based/non-hub-capable untouched. Note: inherits the `.loom/149` hub transport-storm risk while off-LAN.
- **mills: operate the cluster operator off-LAN via Cloudflare Access** (`cmd/loom/cmd_mills.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_mills_cfaccess_test.go`): the `loom-mills-operator` Service is ClusterIP-only, so `loom mills` could only reach it through a `kubectl port-forward`. With the operator now exposed at `mills.flexinfer.ai` behind the cloudflared `home-k3s` tunnel + Cloudflare Access (gitops!281), the CLI defaults `--operator-url` to the public ingress (reachable on- and off-LAN) and injects a CF Access service token (`CF-Access-Client-Id`/`Secret`) on non-loopback requests. Credentials resolve `LOOM_MILLS_CF_ACCESS_*` > `CF_ACCESS_CLIENT_*` env > `~/.config/loom/config.yaml` `hub.cf_access_*` (the same Access application gates every `*.flexinfer.ai` host). Loopback targets (`--operator-url http://localhost:8090` for port-forward) skip the token so it never leaks to a local endpoint. Tests: `millsCFAccessHeaders` precedence, loopback-skip (no token leak), remote-vs-loopback injection predicate.
- **agent-context + mills: plan store S7 — task + Mills-backlog convergence** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema,svc_tasks}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_tasks.go`, `pkg/mills/store/{types,dao_backlog}.go` + migration `005_backlog_plan_id.sql`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): makes the Plan the shared work unit. (1) `agent_task` gains `plan_id`/`slice_id` (round-tripped on the Qdrant payload + keyword-indexed), so a task is a granular TODO under a plan slice (`plan → slice → task`). (2) Mills `BacklogItem` gains a `plan_id` column (migration 005, appended last to preserve positional scans); when set, `backlogPromptContext` instructs the spawned Mills agent to resolve the **live** plan + slices via `agent_plan_get{plan_id}` rather than re-reading a stale `.loom` SpecDoc — so the factory and interactive sessions share one plan. Council/importer authoring plans + backfilling existing backlog is a documented follow-up (S7b); this slice establishes the links + read-through. Tests: task plan-linkage round-trip; backlog `plan_id` round-trip; migration set 1–5. Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **hud: plan store — plans management panel (visible + user-manageable)** (`internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`, `internal/hud/domain/plans/*`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/{App.svelte,lib/stores/router.svelte.ts}`): adds management on top of the read API — `POST /api/plans` (create) + `POST /api/plans/{id}/advance` (lifecycle) with `AgentBridge.PlanCreate`/`PlanAdvance` (illegal transition → 422, undeployed daemon → 503), and a **Work → Plans** Svelte lifecycle board grouping plans by phase with a create form, per-plan advance control, and a slice detail drawer (degrades to a "deploy pending" state). Verified live after a loomd rebuild+restart (create → draft; advance draft→in_progress; in_progress→merged → 422; served bundle contains the panel). Tests: `plans_test.go` (create-requires-title, advance success + illegal→422). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`.
- **hud: plan store S6 — lifecycle read API (`/api/plans`)** (`internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`, `internal/hud/domain/plans/*`, `internal/hud/domain_adapters{,_ops}.go`): new `plans` HUD domain + `AgentBridge.Plans`/`Plan` read methods expose the agent-context Plan store so the lifecycle (`plan→implement→review→merge→deploy`) is reviewable: `GET /api/plans?project=&namespace=&phase=` and `GET /api/plans/{id}` (plan + slices, with phase/MR/pipeline/deploy refs + `phase_history`). Read path is deliberately NOT agent-scoped. **Graceful degradation**: if the running daemon predates the plan store (`agent_plan_*` tools unknown), the endpoints return `{available:false}` + empty list at HTTP 200 instead of erroring, so the view shows a clean "deploy pending" state. Tests: `internal/hud/domain/plans/plans_test.go` (filter passthrough, undeployed-daemon degradation vs genuine 502, single-plan get + missing-id 400). The Svelte lifecycle card is paired with the daemon deploy so it's verified against live data. Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **agent-context: plan store S5 — plan-aware handoffs** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema,service_handoffs}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_handoffs.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): `agent_handoff_create` accepts optional `plan_id`/`slice_id`; they round-trip on the handoff payload and are surfaced by `agent_handoff_accept` (with a `resume_hint`) and `agent_handoff_inbox`. The receiver resumes a known plan scope by id (`agent_plan_get`/`agent_plan_slice_get`) — a durable, cross-vendor handoff (Claude ↔ Codex ↔ Mills) instead of loose `entry_ids` that may be compacted away. Plain handoffs are unchanged (empty plan fields, backward compatible). Tests: `svc_handoff_plan_test.go` (plan-linkage round-trip + no-linkage backward compat). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **agent-context: plan store S4 — enforced slice file-claims + store-driven parallel-slice-ship** (`pkg/agentcontext/{svc_claims,svc_plan_slices,svc_plans,service}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_fileclaims.go`, `.claude/agents/slice-implementer.md`, `mcp/context/skills-registry.yaml`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): made parallel slice file-boundaries real instead of advisory. `agent_file_claim_acquire` gains `enforce` (default false = unchanged advisory): when true and another active agent holds the file, it REFUSES (`ok:false, rejected`) instead of acquiring. New `ClaimSvc.AcquireEnforced` claims a whole file set all-or-nothing. `agent_plan_slice_claim` now hard-claims the slice's `files` via that path (wired through `PlanSvc.claimFiles` in `service.go`), so two slices sharing a file cannot both be claimed — the second is refused with `conflicting_files` and the slice is left unassigned (no half-claim). The `slice-implementer` agent and `parallel-slice-ship` skill are rewritten store-first: the orchestrator persists the decomposition (`agent_plan_create`/`slice_add`), spawns `slice-implementer` (not `general-purpose`) with only `plan_id`+`slice_id`, each implementer resolves its scope via `agent_plan_slice_get`, claims it, and records status/decisions via `agent_plan_slice_update`; the orchestrator reconstructs state via `agent_plan_slice_list` and drives `agent_plan_lifecycle_advance` through review→merging→merged — so a fresh sub-agent never depends on the spawn prompt or a stale `.loom/` checkout. Tests: `svc_plan_claim_enforce_test.go` (advisory-vs-enforce, same-agent idempotent re-claim, slice file-boundary refusal + no half-claim). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **agent-context: plan store S3 — markdown mirror renderer + store-aware plan-loom-core skill** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_plan_render.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `mcp/context/skills-registry.yaml`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): the Plan in Qdrant is canonical; `agent_plan_render{plan_id, path}` now projects it to a human/MR-reviewable `.loom/*.md` mirror, written **atomically** (same-dir tempfile + rename) so codex/gemini/fs watchers never observe a partial file, and records the written path as the plan's `mirror_path`. The render is a pure `renderPlanMarkdown` function (header/metadata, riskiest-assumption+kill-test, success criteria, dependencies, lifecycle refs, phase-history table, spec body, per-slice sections) so it is directly unit-tested. The `plan-loom-core` skill is rewritten to be store-first: create the plan via `agent_plan_create`/`agent_plan_slice_add`, render the mirror, edit via `agent_plan_update`/`agent_plan_lifecycle_advance`/`agent_plan_slice_update` then re-render, and recall by `plan_id` from any worktree/agent — never from a stale `.loom/` checkout. Tests: `svc_plan_render_test.go` (section rendering, minimal plan, atomic write + mirror_path persistence + no leftover temp files). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **agent-context: plan store S2 — full schema, slice records, lifecycle, search** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema_plan,svc_plans,svc_plan_slices}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): builds on the S1 `Plan` entity. (1) Rich schema aligned with the Mills `BacklogItem` (success criteria, budget, `riskiest_assumption`/`kill_test`, dependencies, `mr`/`pipeline`/`deploy` refs, `mirror_path`, `mills_backlog_id`, `gitlab_issue_iid`, `phase_history`) so the two converge in S7. (2) Slices promoted to their own collection `agent_plan_slices_v1` so parallel `slice-implementer`s update status/decisions independently without racing on the plan; a fresh implementer resolves its scope with `agent_plan_slice_get(slice_id)` and anchors blockers/decisions to the slice record instead of losing them to a context window. (3) Validated lifecycle transitions (`draft→…→done`, `abandoned` from any) recorded in `phase_history` via `agent_plan_lifecycle_advance`. (4) Best-effort semantic search (`agent_plan_search`) over title+spec — a failed embedder never blocks a write (deterministic fallback vector), avoiding the embed-coupling outage class. New tools: `agent_plan_update`/`search`/`lifecycle_advance` + `agent_plan_slice_add`/`get`/`list`/`update`/`claim`; `QdrantClient.SearchRaw` added. Live cross-process kill-test re-run PASS with the slice collection. Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **agent-context: first-class, worktree-resilient Plan store (plan store S1)** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema_plan,svc_plans}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): plans lived only as git-tracked `.loom/*.md`, so `git worktree add` froze a copy at the branch HEAD and a fresh sub-agent in another worktree (or Codex, or a Mills pod) could not find the plan it was implementing. A new `Plan` entity now lives in the shared global Qdrant (the same store backing sessions/tasks/handoffs), addressable by a stable `plan_id` (`plan-<slug>-<short>`). Reads are scoped by `plan_id`/`project`/`namespace` and **never** by `agent_id` — the explicit fix vs the agent-scoped context-recall path that would otherwise hide a plan from the parallel + Mills agents that need it. New collection `agent_plans_v1` (+ keyword indexes, `AGENT_CONTEXT_PLANS_COLLECTION`) and tools `agent_plan_create` / `agent_plan_get` / `agent_plan_list`. Kill-test: `svc_plans_killtest_test.go` (`RUN_PLAN_STORE_IT=1`) creates a plan via one `PlanSvc` and retrieves it byte-identical from a *separate* `PlanSvc` (fresh cache) against the real shared Qdrant using only `plan_id` — proving cross-process, non-agent-scoped retrieval (the cross-worktree + cross-vendor legs). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **mcp-pm: `pm_project_status` — agent-facing unified project rollup** (`pkg/pm/{project.go,service.go,config.go}`, `cmd/mcp-pm/tools.go`): the flexdeck `/projects` view federates a project's tasks + risks + decisions, but agents had no equivalent — they could write risks (`pm_risk_*`) and list session-scoped tasks (`agent_task_list`), but nothing returned "the state of project X" in one call. New `pm_project_status{project}` mirrors flexdeck's `GET /api/projects/<id>` for agents: it correlates the shared `project` key (GitLab `path_with_namespace`) across **risks** (`pm_risks`, owned by mcp-pm) and the read-only federated **tasks** (`agent_tasks_v1`) and **decisions** (`agent_context_v1`, `entry_type=decision`) — returning `{open_tasks, in_progress, blocked, open_risks, tasks[], risks[], decisions[]}`. A new `ProjectReader` (interface + `QdrantProjectReader`) scrolls the two agent-context collections by `project` using the same `QdrantClient`/shared config as the risk store (new `Config.{TasksCollection,ContextCollection}`, env-aligned with agent-context so both point at the same data). Each source is **error-isolated** — a dead collection yields an empty slice + `partial=true` (with `partial_reasons`), never a failed call — mirroring the flexdeck federation's per-source isolation; a nil reader degrades the same way. Open-task counting excludes terminal states (completed/cancelled); open-risk counting excludes `closed`. This closes the author→see loop: an agent records a risk/task and can immediately read the unified project state. Read-only; not yet in the registry `always_allow` (follow-up). Tests: `pkg/pm` 86% (hermetic) — `TestProjectStatus_Rollup` (counts, project filter, terminal exclusion), `_NilReaderIsPartial`, `_SourceErrorIsolated`, `_RequiresProject`, `TestQdrantProjectReader_ScrollsByCollection` (collection-aware fake Qdrant). Plan: `.loom/plan-task-integration-pm-2026-06-20.md` (Slice 4).
- **codex: bridge the native `update_plan` tool into agent-context tasks (parity with Claude's TodoWrite)** (`pkg/generator/{extras.go,configs_hooks.go,platform_profiles.yaml,templates/extras/post_tool_use_task_sync_plan.json.tmpl}`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_task_sync.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_task_sync.go`): Codex produced **zero** tasks for the flexdeck `/projects` PM view because, unlike Claude, it had no task-sync wiring. Codex actually supports `[[hooks.PostToolUse]]` with a `tool_name` matcher (since v0.129.0) and ships a native plan/todo tool, `update_plan` (`{explanation, plan:[{step, status}]}`) — so a matched PostToolUse hook bridges it exactly like Claude's `TodoWrite`. Generated wiring: the new `postToolUse_taskSyncPlan` extra emits a Codex `PostToolUse` hook **matched to `update_plan`** that pipes the payload to `loom agent task-sync`. Because the match is tool-specific it fires only on plan updates — NOT every tool call — so it does not reintroduce the per-tool fork that ruled out a PostToolUse *heartbeat* for Codex; it is ordered after `telemetry_eventEmit` so the unmatched telemetry walker doesn't also inject there. The extra carries a new `bootstrap` flag (extras normally only enrich already-built event slots; Codex emits no PostToolUse heartbeat, so the task-sync extra must create the slot). The HUD `/api/agent/task-sync` handler gains a `case "update_plan"` → `syncUpdatePlan`, which **reconciles by (session, normalized title)**: existing plan-sourced tasks (tagged `codex-plan`) get a status update when it changed, only genuinely-new steps are created — avoiding the duplicate-per-call explosion the Claude `TodoWrite` path is prone to (it re-creates every todo each call, and task IDs embed a timestamp so they never collapse). The `update_plan` agent-id resolves to the scopeless `codex-<WS_HASH>` the keepalive registers (Codex's PostToolUse payload has no `session_id`, so `hookAgentIDBootstrap` yields no SESSION_SCOPE). The new hook is self-trusted by construction (`buildCodexHookTrustEntries` hashes every hook in the emitted hooks.json). VENDOR_SPECS.md line 112's stale "no per-tool granularity" note for Codex is corrected. Takes effect after `loom sync codex --regen` + a new `loom` binary; **live acceptance gate** (a real Codex run emits `update_plan` → task appears under the right project) is pending the embedder + namespace-minting rollouts. Tests: `TestCodexHooks_UpdatePlanTaskSyncBridge` (PostToolUse `update_plan` block pipes to task-sync, no `agent heartbeat` fork, trust entry present), `TestMapPlanStatus`/`TestSyncUpdatePlan_ParsesPlan`/`TestHasTagAndNormalizeTitle`, and the updated `TestGenerateHooksConfig_CodexEmitsHooksJSON` (precise invariant: no *unmatched* PostToolUse). Plan: `.loom/plan-task-integration-pm-2026-06-20.md` (Slice 3).
- **agentcontext: stamp `project` on context entries so decisions/findings federate per-project** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema.go,svc_context_add.go,qdrant_convert.go,qdrant_indexes.go}`): context entries carried `session_id`/`namespace` but no canonical `project`, so flexdeck's `/projects` federation (flexdeck!197) could not filter the agent-context decision journal per project — the decisions lane was forced empty. `ContextEntry` now has a `Project` field, stamped on add from the owning session via the same `canonicalProject(session.Project, session.Namespace, session.PipelineRef)` helper used for sessions (so a decision recorded under namespace `services/flexdeck/<branch>` resolves to project `services/flexdeck`). It rides the Qdrant payload (`EntryToPayload`/`PayloadToEntry`) and is added to the `CollContext` keyword index so `{project, entry_type:decision}` scrolls are indexed, matching how tasks already filter. Forward-looking: pre-existing entries have no `project` and won't federate (acceptable — decisions are forward-looking; the federation degrades gracefully). Unblocks flexdeck Slice 5 (decisions lane). Tests: `TestEntryToPayload`/`TestPayloadToEntry` extended for the `project` round-trip. Plan: services/flexdeck `.loom/plan-unified-project-tracking-2026-06-19.md` §0.
- **mcp-pm: minimal `pm_risks` store + MCP server for unified project tracking** (`pkg/pm/{risk,config,store,payload,embed,service}.go`, `cmd/mcp-pm/{main,tools}.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `Makefile`): a new MCP server owning RISKS — the one cross-cutting planning entity that nothing else in the FlexInfer platform models (agent tasks, GitLab issues/milestones, and the agent-context decision journal cover the rest). Tools: `pm_risk_create`, `pm_risk_list` (Qdrant payload keyword filter on `project`/`status`), `pm_risk_update`, `pm_risk_link`. A `Risk` carries `project` (canonical key = GitLab `path_with_namespace`), `title`, `likelihood`/`impact` (low/medium/high), `mitigation`, `owner`, `status` (identified/mitigating/accepted/closed), `links[]`. Persists to a dedicated Qdrant collection `pm_risks` (1536/Cosine) that is **reaper-immune by construction** — the agent-context task reconciler is bound to `CollTasks` only, so a separate collection is never swept (kill-test verified 2026-06-19). **Write is decoupled from embed**: embedding `Title+Mitigation` is best-effort, and on any embedder failure (e.g. the current `gte-qwen2-1.5b` HTTP 400 pooling error) the risk still persists with a deterministic fallback vector and stays payload-filterable — a risk write never fails because embedding failed. Consumed read-only by flexdeck's `/api/projects` federation (flexdeck!197). Tests: `pkg/pm` 87% coverage, hermetic (fake Qdrant HTTP + fake embedder), including embed-failure-still-persists. Brainstorm/plan: services/flexdeck `.loom/{brainstorm,plan}-unified-project-tracking-2026-06-19.md`.
- **proxy: per-tool/per-server output caps — the apply-lever for the flightdeck "chatty tool" bench** (`internal/daemon/config.go`, `cmd/loom/{proxy_truncate,proxy_handlers}.go`): the hub proxy already truncated oversized tool results at a single global `MaxToolResultBytes` (48KB) to protect MCP clients. This adds per-`(server, tool)` overrides so a specific verbose tool the flightdeck bench flags as wasteful (e.g. `gitlab/list_pipeline_jobs` ~19KB/call, `read_mcp_resource` ~34KB/call) can be capped on its own **without lowering the global limit**. New `ProxyConfig.ToolCaps []ToolCap{Server, Tool, MaxBytes}` (config.yaml `proxy.tool_caps`); `proxyMaxToolResultBytesFor(server, tool)` resolves the cap at the existing truncation hook in `handleProxyToolsCall` with most-specific-match precedence — exact `(server,tool)` > server-wide (`tool` empty or `"*"`) > global `MaxToolResultBytes` — floored at 256B. This is the closing half of the telemetry→efficiency loop (services/loom-flightdeck Slice 1 bench, !25/!27/!28): capping shrinks the result the agent ingests, so flightdeck's `tool_calls.output_bytes` drops and the bench's `compare_chatty?change_boundary=` reports predicted-vs-actual savings — no new instrumentation. Only MCP tools routed through the loom hub are cappable (built-in `Read`/`Bash` are outside the proxy). Tests: `TestLookupToolCap_Precedence` (exact/server-wide/`*`/zero-ignored/unknown), `TestProxyMaxToolResultBytesFor` (cap/global-fallback/floor).
- **agentcontext: wire the gte-Qwen2-1.5B flexinfer fallback + cold-start timeout** (`pkg/agentcontext/{config,service}.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`): wires the write-path embedding fallback (added previously) to the self-hosted **1536-dim** `gte-qwen2-1.5b` lane on flexinfer (matches the `agent_context_v1` collection dimension), so `agent_context_add` survives a hosted-Morph outage. The registry agent_context env now defaults `AGENT_CONTEXT_EMBED_FALLBACK_{PROVIDER=flexinfer,MODEL=gte-qwen2-1.5b,BASE_URL=https://litellm.flexinfer.ai/v1,API_KEY=LITELLM_API_KEY}`. Adds `AGENT_CONTEXT_EMBED_FALLBACK_TIMEOUT` (default **60s**, vs the primary's 3s) so the secondary tolerates the cold/serverless gte lane's first-call activation (~30-60s) instead of tripping the short primary timeout. Pairs with flexinfer!650 (the gte Model CR) and flexinfer!651 (the llamacpp `--pooling last` operator fix gte-Qwen2 requires).
- **HUD: flightdeck "N blocked — waiting on a human" badge on the fleet dashboard** (`internal/hud/blocked_store.go` (new), `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed,domain_adapters}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mobile/{deps,handler_dashboard}.go`, `web/loom-fleet-widget/src/{FleetOverview.tsx,hooks/useFleet.ts,lib/mcpBridge.ts}`): the loom HUD was blind to flightdeck's most actionable real-time signal — which agents are stalled waiting on a human. The `flightdeck-hud-bridge` (loom-flightdeck!23) now derives permission stalls from the raw hook stream and emits `agent.blocked`/`agent.unblocked` to loomd `/events/publish`; this consumes them. (1) **persist** — both types are added to `daemonEventLogTypes` so they are retained in the HUD EventLog (dashboard timeline/audit), not just broadcast on the live SSE stream (a live test against the running loomd proved custom types broadcast on SSE but were dropped from the timeline without the whitelist — an early "retained" reading was a false positive from the test's own curl text captured inside a `tool.call.start` event). (2) **fold** — a new `blockedStore` keyed by `session_id` tracks the currently-blocked set (`agent.blocked` adds, `agent.unblocked` removes, 30-min TTL safety net for a missed unblock), fed from `IngestDaemonEvent` and exposed via a new `mobile.Deps.BlockedSessions()` accessor. (3) **surface** — `/api/mobile/v1/dashboard` gains `blocked_count` + a `blocked[]` list (longest wait first; `session_id`, `agent_id`, `reason`, `tool_name`, `cwd`, `waited_seconds`), and the `loom-fleet-widget` renders an "N blocked — waiting on a human" badge plus a per-session list, shown only when someone is blocked. Producer→loomd wire live-verified in loom-flightdeck!23 (publish 204 + `agent.blocked` on the SSE stream). Tests: `blocked_store_test.go` (block/unblock/TTL/ordering, payload parse, `IngestDaemonEvent`→store, the App→wire adapter), `handler_dashboard_test.go` (`blocked_count` + list + empty `[]`). Roadmap: services/loom-flightdeck `.loom/31` Phase K.
- **HUD: session chapters surfaced on the Live Agents table** (`cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{chapters.ts,chapters.svelte.ts,events.svelte.ts}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`): Claude Code's `mcp__ccd_session__mark_chapter` tool marks session chapters that previously went nowhere. The already-installed matcher-less PostToolUse `telemetry_eventEmit` hook pipes the call (with `tool_input.{title,summary}`) to `loom agent event-emit`, which now mints a first-class `chapter.marked` event (verbatim title/summary — user-authored labels, not redacted) instead of an opaque `tool.call.end`. The daemon broadcasts it on `/api/events`; a new frontend `chapters` store buckets chapters by `conversationId` (same axis as the table grouping, so a chapter marked in one repo shows on that chat's row wherever it moved) and the conversation lead row shows a "▸ latest title · N" pill. Tests: `TestHookToEvent_MarkChapter{EmitsChapterMarked,RequiresTitle}`, `chapters.fixture.ts`. Plan: `.loom/151-plan-hud-chapters-ingestion-2026-06-14.md`. Follow-up (deferred): durable chapter history + a timeline drawer.
- **Registry gate `telemetry_event_emit` to drop `loom agent event-emit` hooks (Phase E HUD cutover)** (`pkg/generator/flightdeck_hooks.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_claude.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_gemini.go`): a new `platform_permissions.<platform>.settings.telemetry_event_emit` gate, mirroring the sibling `flightdeck_capture` gate. When set `false`, `loom sync <platform> --regen` no longer emits the `loom agent event-emit --hook …` lifecycle hooks for that platform. This is the cutover switch for services/loom-flightdeck Phase E: on a host running the new `flightdeck-hud-bridge` daemon (loom-flightdeck!17/!18), the bridge tails the capture spool and republishes `session.*`/`tool.call.*` to loomd `/events/publish`, so event-emit would double-publish the same events to the HUD; the gate lets that host drop event-emit and make the bridge the single source. The gate **defaults on** (key absent ⇒ event-emit wired) so it is purely additive — no behavior change until a registry sets it false. It is applied at the claude + gemini call sites via `withEventEmitGate` (which filters the `telemetry_eventEmit` extra), leaving the agent-context presence path (`loom agent keepalive` / `session-end`) untouched. NOTE: event-emit also mints special events the bridge does not yet replicate (e.g. the `chapter.marked` event above, and `agent.status.change`), so the gate must NOT be flipped false until the bridge reaches event parity — otherwise those features regress. Blast radius is documented in code: the registry is shared, so disabling it darkens the HUD on hosts without the bridge — disable only once the bridge is deployed on every HUD-feeding host, or scope per-host. Codex/Antigravity use separate generator paths and are not gated here (follow-up). Tests: `eventemit_gate_test.go` (default/explicit-on keep hooks; `false` drops all event-emit while the rest of the hooks block stays intact; per-platform isolation).
- **Daemon: muxstdio close-cause observability** (`pkg/transport/muxstdio/transport.go`, services/loom-core!700): `readLoop` swallowed the `inner.Recv` error, so the chronic hub-WS transport storm (~1k `transport closed` lines/hour, root-caused in `.loom/149-plan-backend-hub-transport-stability-2026-06-12.md`) was undiagnosable — no WebSocket close code, no reason. The first reader error is now captured as `closeCause`, logged once at WARN (`muxstdio: inner transport closed`, with the count of in-flight calls it failed), and annotated onto every `ErrClosed` returned from `Send`/`Recv` — so the callpipeline's existing `hub transport failed` / `clearing pool` log lines print WHY the transport died (gorilla close errors stringify the close code, e.g. `websocket: close 1006`). The `%w` wrap preserves `errors.Is(ErrClosed)` and the `"transport closed"` substring matched by the daemon's transport-failure detection; deliberate `Close()` stays quiet. Tests: `close_cause_test.go` (cause propagation + substring/errors.Is contract, deliberate-close silence).
- **HUD: fleet snapshots carry explicit degraded-state instead of silently masking partial fetches (Slice 5a of `.loom/149`)** (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`): when a sessions/presence coupled-view sub-fetch fails (typically the chronic `muxstdio: transport closed` storm), the fleet monitor carries over the previous snapshot's roster so the HUD shows live data rather than blank rows — but `UpdatedAt` keeps advancing, so the frontend's `staleAfter` banner never fires and the degradation was *silent* (the only signal was a server-side log line). `FleetSnapshot` now exposes `degraded` / `degraded_reason` / `degraded_since`: the partial-failure branch sets `Degraded=true`, names the failing side (`degradedReason()` → "sessions" / "presence" / "sessions and presence fetch failing"), and stamps `DegradedSince` at the onset of the degraded streak (persisted across consecutive degraded refreshes via the previous snapshot; all three reset to zero on recovery since each refresh starts from a fresh `FleetSnapshot{}`). The fields ride the existing fleet API + SSE JSON, so they reach clients additively and backward-compatibly. Backend-only; the frontend "degraded since HH:MM (reason)" banner that consumes them is deferred to Slice 5b (needs the `go:embed`'d HUD dist rebuild). Tests: `TestDegradedReason`, `TestFleetMonitor_DegradedStateSurfacing` (healthy → degraded(onset) → still-degraded(onset persists) → recovered).

### Changed
- **deps: bump `libs/mcp-go` to `18b68e7` — activates hub-WS keepalive + singleflight reconnect (Slice 2 of `.loom/149`)** (`go.mod`, `go.sum`): pulls in the transport-durability change (mcp-go !7) so the daemon's hub WebSocket pool gets a background keepalive ping per connection (prevents idle close-1006 reaping), liveness gating on cached connections, and singleflight reconnect (one upstream close → at most one redial). Pairs with the exponential prefer-hub backoff below (Slice 2b); together they are the durable fix for the chronic `muxstdio: transport closed` storm root-caused in `.loom/149`. Tier-2 live confirmation (idle-1006 rate → ~0) is verified on redeploy.
- **daemon: prefer-hub backoff is now exponential with hub-success reset (Slice 2b of `.loom/149`)** (`internal/daemon/routing.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_{errors,stages,routing}.go`): the prefer-hub override backoff was a flat **30s**, so a persistently-unavailable hub kept re-dialing every 30s (the ~32s sawtooth in the `.loom/149` evidence). It is now a per-server exponential schedule — `30s → 60s → 120s → … capped at 5m` — driven by a consecutive-failure streak (`nextPreferHubBackoff`, `base << (streak-1)`) that **persists across expired windows** so repeated post-expiry failures back off progressively further. A successful hub call (`recordSuccessMetrics` with `target == Hub`) clears the window and resets the streak to base — required because while backed off, traffic is suppressed to local, so the reset must hang off an actual hub success rather than a local one. `setPreferHubBackoff` keeps its `dur` param (an explicit value still works, used by tests); `dur <= 0` selects the schedule. Complements the `libs/mcp-go` Slice 2 durability change (mcp-go !7): singleflight + keepalive cut how often the backoff is armed; this cuts the redial frequency while the hub is genuinely down. Extending backoff to hub-only/hub-delegate routing is intentionally out of scope (without a local fallback a suppression window is a no-op). Tests: `TestPreferHubBackoffExponentialSchedule` (growth + 5m cap + reset-to-base), `TestSetPreferHubBackoffExplicitDurLeavesStreak`.
- **HUD: explicit "Degraded since HH:MM — <reason>" banner instead of the silent/late stale pill (Slice 5b of `.loom/149`)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/fleet.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/ConnectionBanner.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`): consumes the `degraded`/`degraded_reason`/`degraded_since` snapshot fields added in Slice 5a (!721). The fleet store parses them in `applySnapshot` (a healthy snapshot clears them — `degraded` is serialized without omitempty); `ConnectionBanner` renders an amber `connection-degraded` banner reading "Degraded since HH:MM — sessions/presence fetch failing" whenever SSE is connected and the monitor flagged a carried-over partial fetch. It takes priority over the generic `connection-stale` pill (degraded is the specific, backend-sourced signal vs the client-side staleness guess) and stays hidden below the higher-severity reconnecting/disconnected/circuit-open states. Without it the degradation was effectively invisible: the monitor advances `UpdatedAt` on every carry-over, so the 90s `staleAfter` banner frequently never fired. `go:embed`'d HUD dist rebuilt. Build-verified (vite compile + go embed); the live-banner kill-test (induce a degraded fleet on a connected HUD) is deferred — the local preview cannot produce a connected-but-degraded backend (`.loom/156`).
- **HUD: namespace column shows repo + branch; Codex rows stop fragmenting** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/{namespace.ts,agents.ts}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`): two display-layer improvements complementing the generator namespace fix. (1) The Live Agents namespace cell now parses `<repo-2-level>/<branch>` into a primary repo line + a dimmed branch line via a new `parseNamespace()`, and renders a muted "—" for synthetic/fallback namespaces (`agents/<agent-id>`) or malformed ones (`////main`) instead of the raw noise — so the column reads even before the backend fix deploys and old sessions age out. (2) `conversationId()` is now vendor-aware: Codex mints workspace-anchored ids (`codex-<WS_HASH>` from its notify hook) and the fleet sees both scopeless and scoped variants for one app, so Codex folds by `WS_HASH` (a new `WORKSPACE_ANCHORED_BASES` set) instead of fragmenting by scope into multiple rows; Claude/Gemini keep the cross-repo `SESSION_SCOPE` axis. Smoke tests: `namespace.fixture.ts`, `agents.rootGrouping.fixture.ts` (Codex fold cases).
