TranscriptTab / ToolsTab / FilesTab / ErrorsTab map their error branches to a ⚠ EmptyState carrying the same error text as the description, and their empty branches to per-tab icon + heading + hint (💬, 🔧, 📄; ErrorsTab's normal payload is errors so its empty state is ✓ *"No errors recorded — The spawn has not reported any errors."*). The now-unreferenced `.tab-empty` / `.tab-error` local CSS rules are deleted; `grep -c 'tab-empty\|tab-error' internal/hud/frontend/dist/assets/index-*.css` is `0`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt. Does not touch `EmptyState.svelte` itself or any other panel.
- **HUD Fleet "Live Agents" table cells no longer float in the vertical middle of each row** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`, services/loom-core!641): the Operations → Fleet table rendered every single-line cell (status dot, evidence pills, namespace, activity, heartbeat, Session/Traces buttons) centered in the row's full height, disconnected from the agent it described — and the `Heartbeat` header truncated to `HEARTBE…`. `DataTable` sets `vertical-align: middle` on every `td`; the fleet *agent* cell is a tall multi-line stack (id + meta-row + hierarchy pills), so each sibling cell centered itself in that tall row instead of sitting on the agent's top baseline. The intended `vertical-align: top` override existed only on the agent cell, written as `:global(.data-table.stable-layout) tbody td.agent-cell` — but that split form (a scoped `tbody td` combinator **outside** `:global()`) targets DataTable's internal DOM, which carries no FleetTable scope hash, so the Svelte compiler **dropped it as an unused selector** (verified: zero `vertical-align:top` in the prior bundle; the agent cell's `white-space: normal`/`word-break` were silently dropped too). Fix: both the agent-cell override and a new **all-cells** top-align rule now live fully inside `:global()`, anchored on `.fleet-table-card` so they stay confined to this table (specificity `0,3,2` beats DataTable's `0,1,2` default); sibling cells get the agent cell's top padding so all columns share one baseline; the Heartbeat column widens `80px → 108px`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt. Verified against the compiled CSS (rule present + specificity) and a before/after harness rendered with the real bundle.
- **Mills codex spawns pin `--model` to a ChatGPT-account-supported model (Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge blocker)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, services/loom-core!640): the live A2 kill-test on 2026-06-06 (`gitops!230` flipped prod `stage_substrate`→harvester-vm; canary `MILLS-CANARY-A2-20260606-011720`; reverted `gitops!231`) proved the prior four fixes work — codex had the CLI + workspace, did not hang on stdin, authenticated via OAuth (`auth_mode: "chatgpt"`), and **started a turn** — then failed at the *first* spawn (`plan_slice`, on k8s; `implement` never reached a VM) with **HTTP 400 `invalid_request_error`: "The 'gpt-5.3-codex' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account."** `buildAgentCommand` ran `codex exec` with **no `--model`**, so codex used its CLI default (`gpt-5.3-codex`), which OpenAI **deprecated for ChatGPT-account (sign-in-with-ChatGPT) auth** ([developers.openai.com/codex/models](https://developers.openai.com/codex/models), fetched 2026-06-06: supported with ChatGPT sign-in are `gpt-5.5`/`gpt-5.4`/`gpt-5.4-mini`; `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` is ChatGPT-Pro only; deprecated: `gpt-5.2`, `gpt-5.3-codex`). The substrate-independent 400 escalated cleanly via the `nonempty_diff` gate (no junk MR). The codex exec now appends `--model <resolveCodexModel()>`: new `resolveCodexModel()` returns `SPAWN_CODEX_MODEL` (trimmed) or the `defaultCodexModel` constant (`gpt-5.5`) — the env override lets operators retune **without a rebuild** the next time OpenAI shifts the supported set (the failure class itself). The model is `shellQuote`d like the prompt. Tests: `TestBuildAgentCommand` now asserts `--model 'gpt-5.5'` present and `gpt-5.3-codex` absent; new `TestResolveCodexModel` covers default, env override, and blank-override-falls-back. Plan: `.loom/129`; evidence: `.loom/local/handoffs/mills-harvester-vm-slice-a2-killtest-2026-06-05.md`. (The SDK-driver path `buildSDKDriverCommand` is not the prod codex route — the canary ran `use_sdk_driver=false`.)
- **HUD now groups Live Sessions and Fleet "Live Agents" by workspace-agent root, and agent counts dedupe per-conversation `agent_id`s** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/agents.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/fleetRows.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{liveSessions,fleet}.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{LiveSessionsCard.svelte,fleet/FleetTable.svelte}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`): the lifecycle hooks mint `agent_id` **per conversation** as `<base>-<WS_HASH>-<SESSION_SCOPE>` (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`), where `WS_HASH` is `cksum` of the git workspace root (stable per workspace) and `SESSION_SCOPE` is `cksum` of the conversation id (changes every conversation). One agent running several conversations therefore showed as several unrelated agents — `claude-code-552019522-2804496862` + `claude-code-552019522-3116397616` (same repo), `codex-4188162495` + `codex-4188162495-2303882182` — rendered as flat sibling rows, and the "Active Agents" headline / nav badge / footer "N live agents" over-counted (6 sessions read as "6 agents" when there were 5). The bug was purely in the deployed-HUD display layer: `parent_session_id`/`root_session_id` are *correctly* null for sibling top-level conversations (they are siblings, not parent/child), so the existing session-tree grouping (`fleet.svelte.ts` `sessionTree`) could never collapse them. New `rootAgentId()` strips the trailing session-scope segment (everything up to and including the first all-numeric segment; ids with no numeric segment pass through) and `groupSessionsByRootAgent()` collapses sessions by it. The Live Sessions card renders one header per logical agent with sessions nested; `summarizeUnifiedAgents().live_agents` + new `fleetStore.liveAgentCount` count distinct roots. `buildFleetRows` buckets session-tree roots by `rootAgentId` so sibling conversations nest one level under a lead (flagged `agentRootChild` → "same agent" pill instead of "root session"), with "N root groups" counting distinct workspace agents; genuine Mills/Weaver subagent hierarchies (real parent linkage) are preserved inside each bucket. Regression: runnable `agents.rootGrouping.fixture.ts` (17 assertions: `rootAgentId` across real id shapes, 6→5 collapse, count dedup) and `fleetRows.rootAgent.fixture.ts` (11 assertions: 2-root-group collapse, depth-1 nesting, `agentRootChild`), both via `pnpm dlx tsx`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt.
- **Mills `plan_slice` no longer dies on prompt backticks run as shell commands (Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge blocker)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `buildAgentCommand` interpolated the agent prompt with Go `%q` into a command string executed via `sh -c` (`backend.StreamExec` / `K8sBackend.Exec`). `%q` yields a *Go* string literal, not a shell-safe argument, so shell metacharacters in the prompt stayed live — backticks and `$(...)` were evaluated as command substitution before the agent CLI ran. The Mills canary `SpecDoc` (`cmd/loom/cmd_mills_pipelines.go`) wraps the fixture path and backlog id in backticks (`` `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md` ``, `` `MILLS-CANARY-...` ``), so on the k8s `plan_slice` spawn path (run `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260605-145334`) the shell tried to execute them — `sh: testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md: Permission denied` / `sh: MILLS-CANARY-...: not found` — and the agent received a prompt with the backtick spans silently stripped, blocking the first autonomous merge (north-star `autonomous_merges_24h` = 0). Affected all three agent types (claude-code, codex, gemini); the `buildSDKDriverCommand` path already used `shellQuote` and was unaffected. Swaps `%q` → `shellQuote(task)` on all three legacy CLI paths so every prompt metacharacter is passed through literally; `agentID` stays `%q` (controlled identifier inside the trap's single-quoted body). Complements the codex `< /dev/null` stdin-hang fix on the same line. Regression: `TestBuildAgentCommand_PromptShellQuotedAgainstInjection` executes the built command under a real shell with stubbed agent binaries and asserts a backtick/`$()`/`$VAR`-bearing prompt reaches the CLI verbatim with no shell evaluation (fails on the old `%q` form for all three agents).
- **Mills classifies the k8s exec-stream `502 Bad Gateway` (kubelet dial) as transient, not a code bug** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`): a `plan_slice` codex spawn whose `kubectl exec`/streaming failed because the apiserver could not reach the target node's kubelet (`error dialing backend: proxy error from 127.0.0.1:6443 while dialing <node>:10250, code 502: 502 Bad Gateway`) fell through `Classify` to `ClassCode` and **escalated** after the attempt cap (`turn_count=0`, `$0` cost) instead of free-retrying. The transient patterns only matched the `status 5xx` shape (GitLab/devbox clients), not the exec dialer's `error dialing backend` / worded `502 Bad Gateway` form — so a transient konnectivity blip (node Ready before and after) was indistinguishable from a real code failure. Surfaced by the `.loom/126` Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (2026-06-05): two consecutive harvester-vm canaries escalated at `plan_slice` on the k3s-w-10 kubelet dial 502, never reaching the `implement` stage on the VM. Adds `"error dialing backend"` to the transport-transient set and the worded gateway forms (`bad gateway`/`service unavailable`/`gateway timeout`) to the 5xx set, so these konnectivity blips become free retries (bounded by `transientRetryCap`) and land on a healthy connection instead of burning the attempt budget. Test: `TestClassify_KubeletExecDial502` (the real A2 `log_tail` fixture + worded 503/504). Note: this is the classifier half — a persistently-degraded node still needs infra remediation (cordon / restart konnectivity).
- **Codex spawns no longer hang/exit on `Reading additional input from stdin...` (the `.loom/44` agent-execution gap)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `buildAgentCommand`'s codex invocation now appends `< /dev/null`. codex 0.120.0+ `exec`, even with the prompt passed as an argument, inspects stdin — when stdin is a non-TTY pipe that is open but never written/closed, it prints `Reading additional input from stdin...`, treats stdin as additional prompt input, and hangs (or, on a session whose stdin EOFs oddly, exits 1) instead of running the single turn. **Both** spawn exec paths leave stdin in exactly that state — the K8s `StreamExec` sets `PodExecOptions{Stdin:false}` and the harvester-vm SSH session leaves `session.Stdin` nil — so codex started a thread + turn then died with `turn_count=1` and an empty diff. Surfaced by the harvester-vm A2 re-run after the workspace/CLI provisioning fix landed (`spawn-ced0192f6540`: `thread.started`/`turn.started` on stdout, `Reading additional input from stdin...` on stderr, exit 1); it is also the latent cause of codex's empty implements on k8s. The prompt is already an argument, so `< /dev/null` removes the spurious stdin read with no behavior loss. Ref openai/codex#20919. Test: new `< /dev/null` assertion in `TestBuildAgentCommand`.
- **mcp-devbox `harvester-vm` sandboxes SSH as `agent`, not the stale `ubuntu`** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go`, comment fix in `internal/hud/app.go`): `cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go` hardcoded a `"ubuntu"` fallback for `DEVBOX_HARVESTER_SSH_USER`, which overrode the backend's canonical default. Slice 2d.5c moved the VM's cloud-init login + auth-file home to the uid-1000 `agent` user for home-parity with the K8s spawn pod (`backend.defaultHarvesterSSHUser = vmAgentUser = "agent"`), so a devbox-launched harvester-vm sandbox was SSHing as the wrong user and mounted agent auth files (`~/.codex/auth.json` etc.) would not resolve. The default is now empty so `NewHarvesterVMBackend` applies the single-source-of-truth `agent` default — matching the operator/HUD spawn path, which already passes the env value through (empty → `agent`). Closes the `.loom/126` Slice A1 leftover ("reconcile the SSH-user default to `agent` everywhere"); behavior is covered by the existing `TestNewHarvesterVMBackend` default-user assertion. Also corrects a stale `(default: "ubuntu")` doc comment on `app.HUDConfig.SpawnHarvesterSSHUser`.
- **Mills empty-implement runs no longer reach the MR stage / open 0-commit MRs** (`pkg/mills/gates/nonempty_diff.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/gates.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/noop_dispatcher.go`): the `post_implement_gate` ran `{diff_size, scope, path_policy, secret_scan, commit_format}`, none of which fail on an empty diff — `scope`/`path_policy` early-return *pass* when `FilesChanged==0`, the size/secret gates pass on a zero diff, and the LLM rubric scores an empty diff 1.0. So an `implement` stage that produced **no change at all** (the agent did no work — e.g. codex exiting `turn_count=0` on the harvester-vm) sailed through every gate to the `mr` stage and opened a 0-commit MR (`head_sha=null`). That empty-MR false-positive killed every canary in the `.loom/44` autonomy round (`!518`/`!520`/`!522`) and the `.loom/126` Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (`!598`). Adds a deterministic **`nonempty_diff`** gate (fails iff `FilesChanged==0 && DiffPatch` empty), registered in `gates.Default()` and prepended to `post_implement_gate` so it short-circuits before the size/scope checks; with the stage's `RetryFrom: "implement"`, an empty implement now retries and then **escalates to a human** instead of producing a junk MR — independent of *why* the agent produced nothing. `NoOpDispatcher` (the in-code embodiment of the false-positive) now emits a non-empty placeholder diff while keeping `FilesChanged` empty so the scope/path_policy gates still pass on no-slice smoke items. Defensive guard only — the live agent-execution gap (codex-on-VM) stays gated separately. Tests: `nonempty_diff_test.go` table + updated `TestDefault_HasAllCoreGates`. Plan: `.loom/128-iteration-plan-mills-empty-diff-gate-2026-06-04.md`.
- **Harvester-VM spawns now provision the workspace + agent CLI at Start (Mills A2 empty-diff root cause)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/backend.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`): with the buffered-exec fix making failures visible, a direct codex canary on `substrate=harvester-vm` exited **127 in 176ms** with `cd: /workspace/services/loom-core: No such file or directory` **and** `codex: command not found`. The harvester-vm `Build` is a no-op against one shared base image, so a booted VM had **neither the repo nor the agent CLI** — unlike the K8s path, which hydrates the workspace via a git-clone init container and bakes the CLI into the per-agent runtime image. `HarvesterVMBackend.Start` now provisions over SSH after the VM is ready: it `git clone`s `<GitBaseURL>/<project>.git` into `StartOpts.WorkDir` and checks out the requested branch (created from `BaseBranch` when origin lacks it, mirroring `gitCloneInitContainer` — the VM disk IS the worktree), then runs a guarded, idempotent agent-CLI install (`StartOpts.AgentCLIInstallCmd`, built by `agentCLIInstallShell` from the same pinned versions as `agentCLIInstallLines`). The script is a fast no-op on a future curated base image (Phase B1) and on reused VMs; the git token is resolved via the existing `SecretResolver` and exported only for the provisioning SSH session, never persisted to the cloud-init env file. `HarvesterVMBackendConfig` gains `GitBaseURL`/`GitSecret` (wired from `SpawnGitBaseURL`/`SpawnGitSecret`); `StartOpts` gains `AgentCLIInstallCmd` (the K8s backend ignores it). Tests: `buildProvisionScript` (clone+CLI, https scheme, base-branch default, no-branch, CLI-only, empty no-op, empty-token guard, special-char quoting), `cloneRequested` table, `agentCLIInstallShell` (guarded + version-pinned). Refs `.loom/126` Slice A2, `.loom/45` §Slice 1.5/B1.
- **Council run duration is real, not always 0ms / "instant"** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`): `FlexInferCouncilEditor.Edit` never stamped `Sidecar.StartedAt`, so the `IsZero()` guard in `pkg/mills/council/artifacts.go` always fired and set `StartedAt` to the same post-write `now` as `EndedAt` — every successful council run wrote `StartedAt == EndedAt` → 0ms duration → an "instant" badge on every Council-tab row, even for multi-second runs with real findings. The editor now captures `started := time.Now().UTC()` before the `Chat` call and stamps it onto `Sidecar.StartedAt`; the writer's `IsZero()` guard preserves it. The Council tab reports real elapsed time and the "instant" badge correctly reserves itself for sub-1s crashes. Regression test `TestFlexInferCouncilEditorStampsStartedAt`. (Original branch `fix/mills-editor-stamps-started-at`, rebased + shipped 2026-06-04.)
- **Harvester-VM spawns no longer silently swallow the agent result** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): the buffered (non-streaming) exec branch — taken by every `harvester-vm` spawn, since that backend isn't `streamExecCapable` — never fed the agent's output to the telemetry parser, never checked `execResult.ExitCode` (only the Go-level `execErr`; `Backend.Exec` returns a nil error for command-level nonzero exits), and discarded `StderrTail`. Net effect during the Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test: a codex run that exited in ~0.24s was marked **`completed`** with `turn_count=0` and **zero diagnostics**, hiding the in-VM failure. Now the buffered branch best-effort-parses the stdout tail, **fails the spawn on a nonzero exit** surfacing the stderr tail (new `bufferedExecFailure` helper), captures stderr into the agent-output context entry, and logs a full exec summary (exit code, durations, stdout/stderr tails). Confirmed by a live harvester-vm canary repro. (Full real-time telemetry on harvester-vm still requires making the backend `streamExecCapable` — tracked separately.) Unit test in `internal/hud/spawn_buffered_exec_test.go`.
- **Mills Overview health banner no longer falsely claims "No successful merge on record"** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{mills.svelte.ts,mills.systemHealth.ts}`): a residual of the same active-only blind spot fixed in !619. The `broken`-state banner detail line (`OverviewPanel.svelte`) reads `health.last_successful_merge_at`, but `computeSystemHealth` derived it **only** from `millsStore.pipelineRuns` — the active-only run list, which never contains a terminal `done` run — so it was ~always `null` and the banner wrongly read *"No successful merge on record"* even when the Mills had merged plenty (just not in the last 24h, which is exactly the `broken` condition). Adds an **all-time** lookup `PipelineDAO.LatestMergedAt` (`MAX(ended_at)` over `state=done`), surfaced as `last_merge_at` on `/api/mills/status` (symmetric with the existing `last_council_at`), and threaded into `computeSystemHealth` as an authoritative `lastMergeAt` override that wins over the blind derivation — mirroring the `mergedRuns24h`/`escalatedRuns24h` override pattern. Additive + graceful: until the operator ships the new field the frontend falls back to the prior (blind) derivation, no regression. Tests: `TestPipeline_LatestMergedAt` (DAO: nil before any merge, newest `done` wins over a newer escalation) + a `blind-list-override` case in `mills.systemHealth.fixture.ts`. No `OverviewPanel.svelte` change — the bug was purely the data source.
- **Spawn session-registration failures are now observable** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `runSpawn` silently swallowed `StartSession` errors at spawn-start, leaving `state.SessionID` empty — which makes `persistTelemetrySummary` skip the write, so the spawn's turn-level telemetry (`turn_count` / `stop_reason` / `last_message`) vanishes with no log. That blind spot hid the in-VM codex failure during the Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (codex exited `turn_count=0` with an empty diff, invisible from operator logs). Extracted `registerSpawnSession`, which logs a `Warn` on registration failure (or empty session id) instead of swallowing it. Regression tests in `internal/hud/spawn_telemetry_persist_test.go`.

### Security
- **Bump Go toolchain 1.25.10 → 1.25.11** (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION`, and the `golang:*-alpine` builder base in `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.custom-server`, `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator`, `Dockerfile.custom-server.local`, `Dockerfile.local`): patches 3 stdlib vulnerabilities flagged by `security:govulncheck` — `GO-2026-5039` (net/textproto: arbitrary inputs included in errors unescaped), `GO-2026-5038` (mime: quadratic complexity in `WordDecoder.DecodeHeader`), `GO-2026-5037` (crypto/x509: inefficient candidate hostname parsing). All three are fixed in `stdlib@go1.25.11`. The govulncheck job (lint stage, `allow_failure:false`) was failing on `main`, which blocked the `deploy` stage from building images — so this also unblocks the post-!611 `loom-mills-operator` image build that carries the autonomy kill-switch (plan-42 Slice 1b).

### Added
- **Claude hook event names are validated against the installed CLI before emission** (`pkg/generator/claude_hook_events.go`, wired into `claudeHooks()` in `pkg/generator/configs_claude.go`): a live probe on 2026-06-09 proved Claude Code CLI 2.1.61 **silently disables the entire hooks config** when `settings.json` contains even one unknown hook event name — zero events fire, no error or warning anywhere (documented in `~/workspace/.loom/60-product-spec-loom-flightdeck.md`, "Risks"). One typo in `platform_profiles.yaml` (`session_end_event`, `heartbeat_event`) or a generator regression would therefore kill hook-based session tracking in every synced repo after `loom sync claude --regen`. The generator now resolves the accepted event set by scanning the locally installed `claude` binary for its embedded event-name enum (chunked `bytes.Index` anchor-and-expand on `"PreToolUse"`, ~0.1s over the 178MB 2.1.61 binary; labeled with `claude --version`), falling back to a pinned 18-event baseline extracted from 2.1.61 when no binary is probeable (e.g. CI) or the probe fails a sanity gate (≥8 events incl. the lifecycle core). Unknown event keys are stripped with a loud stderr `WARN` naming the dropped events and the provenance of the accepted set, so the rest of the hooks survive instead of all dying. Validating against the **installed binary** rather than docs matters: code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks listed 30 events on 2026-06-09 (including `PermissionDenied`) while the installed 2.1.61 accepts only 18 — `PermissionDenied` among the missing, and writing it is exactly the silent-death case. Audit result: all 5 events currently emitted for Claude (`SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `SubagentStart`) are valid in 2.1.61. Regression tests pin the baseline to the exact 2.1.61 enum, assert every emitted event is accepted (`TestClaudeHooks_EmittedEventsAreAccepted`), simulate the YAML-typo class end to end, and exercise the chunked binary probe across a >4MB read boundary. Complements the docs-drift checker (`vendor_specs.yaml`) — that asserts docs mention what we emit; this asserts the *installed CLI* accepts it.
- **`millsStore` + `otelStore` join the HUD SSE staleness cohort (plan-117 last open follow-up)** (`internal/hud/monitor/{otel,mills}.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{otel,mills}.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): the two OverviewPanel stores were the last secondary stores still blind-polling with no SSE subscription, so they couldn't drop to the 60s watchdog cadence the rest of the cohort uses (plan-117 §"Remaining follow-ups": "bumping them to 60s would add a 30s data lag"). Closes that by adding the two missing event families. **OTel** (`hud.otel`): new `OTelMonitor` mirrors `CostMonitor` — a thin `RefreshFunc` over the daemon `loom/otel-status` RPC; its `OnRefresh` broadcasts the `bridge.OTelStatusResult` snapshot, and `otelStore` now subscribes (`applySnapshot` applies it directly — the json tags already match the `OTelStatus` interface) + registers `stalenessStore.register('otel', …)` (`staleAfter=90_000`). **Mills** (`hud.mills`): new `MillsMonitor` GETs the operator status endpoint directly (the status route is an open read — the proxy only injects the admin bearer for mutations) and broadcasts it; because the Mills store fans out to many operator endpoints, the frontend uses the event as a **refresh signal** (re-runs `fetchAll`) rather than a partial-apply, so the live tabs/drawers keep their full data with no per-tab regression. The monitor is only constructed when `LOOM_MILLS_OPERATOR_URL` is set (no `hud.mills` events on laptops), and `millsStore.isStale` is suppressed while `disabled` so the ConnectionBanner pill stays quiet there. Both stores switch their watchdog poll to `!eventStore.connected || this.isStale` and align to 60s; `ConnectionBanner` surfaces them automatically via the existing `stalenessStore.staleStores` registry (no banner edit). Monitors started at 30s (otel) / 15s (mills) server-side so one poll fans out to every browser over SSE instead of each browser polling. Tests: `otel_test.go` (zero snapshot, stop-idempotent, OnRefresh fires) + `mills_test.go` (status-URL construction incl. trailing-slash trim, httptest refresh decode+broadcast, non-200 → error, stop-idempotent). Plan: `.loom/117-implementation-plan-hud-ux-overhaul-2026-05-15.md`.
- **Mills HUD: Backlog + Eval rows are clickable for detail (drilldown gap closed)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/{BacklogDetail.svelte,BacklogPanel.svelte,EvalPanel.svelte}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!634): the Mills row→detail drilldown was only ever wired for the **Pipelines** tab; **Backlog** (the work queue) and **Eval** were static read-only tables with zero click handlers — the operator-reported "can't click row entries for details, still mostly useless." The backend already proxied every per-entity detail endpoint (`internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go:41-101`) and a reusable drawer (`shared/DetailDrawer.svelte`) already existed, so this was a frontend-wiring gap, not missing capability — **no operator change**. **Backlog** rows now open a right-edge detail drawer (`BacklogDetail.svelte`, wrapping the shared `DetailDrawer`) rendering the full item from `GET /api/mills/backlog/{id}` (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_backlog.go:111` returns the whole `BacklogItem`): spec doc + anchor, slice decomposition, success criteria, budget/policy, dependencies, cost estimate, council-run + GitLab-issue refs, **cross-links to the pipeline run(s) executing the item** (active + history, via the new `millsStore.pipelineRunsForBacklog()` helper), and a **Start-pipeline** admin action (`millsStore.startPipeline()` → `POST /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{backlog_id}/start`) when no run exists yet. **Eval** rows now expand inline (matching the Council/Audit/Squads expander idiom) to show the rubric **breakdown**, judge, and full notes, and cross-link `pipeline_run` subjects into the run drawer. Store adds backlog-detail drilldown state mirroring the proven pipeline-run pattern: `selectedBacklogID` + `backlogDetailByID` cache, a `currentBacklogDetail` getter (named distinctly from the `openBacklogDetail()` method — a getter and method sharing a name collide as duplicate class members and fail silently), `open/closeBacklogDetail`, and `refreshOpenBacklogDetail()` wired into `fetchAll` for 15s live refresh. Council/Audit/Squads already had working click-to-expand drilldown and are intentionally untouched. All clickable rows carry `role`/`tabindex`/`aria` + Enter/Space keyboard handlers. Riskiest-assumption kill-test passed via operator source (deployed HUD is behind Cloudflare Access, returns 302). Plan + evidence: `.loom/65-plan-hud-mills-row-drilldown-2026-06-05.md`.
- **Agent-context recall second-stage reranker wired into `HandleUnifiedRecall` (flexinfer `/v1/rerank`, default-off)** (`pkg/agentcontext/service.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/service_recall.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/reranker_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the recall `Reranker` abstraction + `FlexInferReranker` + `ApplyReranker` helper shipped in Slice A1 but were deliberately **never called** from the recall path (`ApplyReranker` carried a "NOT yet called … wired separately in a later slice (see .loom/88 §2.A1)" note). This lands that wiring as the FlexInfer hardware-utilization arc's S1.4 consumer slice. `Service` gains a `reranker Reranker` field constructed once in `NewServiceFromEnv` from `LoadRerankerConfigFromEnv()` (default `NoopReranker`/"off" → zero network I/O, response path byte-stable) with a `WithReranker` option for test injection. `HandleUnifiedRecall` now applies the reranker as a **second retrieval stage** over the merged context candidate set — after embedding retrieval + priority-sort + budget-trim, before the entries are returned — via a small `rerankRecallEntries` wrapper that gates on the wired backend (`off`/nil → no-op) and soft-fails to the embedding/priority order when the proxy is unavailable (timeout/404 annotate `rerank_status` and preserve order — recall never fails because rerank is down). When active, the backend tag surfaces on `recall_meta.rerank_backend`. **Enablement** (default-safe, opt-in): `WEAVER_RERANKER=flexinfer` (or `bge`), `WEAVER_RERANKER_BASE_URL=$FLEXINFER_URL`, and — for the homelab flexinfer proxy specifically — `WEAVER_RERANKER_MODEL=rerank` (the proxy's litellm alias; the generic default `bge-reranker-v2-m3` is **not** a routable alias there). **Live before/after evidence** against the proxy `/v1/rerank` route (bge-reranker-v2-m3 co-active with bge-large embeddings on the Radeon VII, gfx906): on a 5-query graded-relevance eval, mean nDCG@6 **0.554 → 0.778 (+22.4 pts)** and mean gold-doc rank **3.40 → 2.40**, with rerank moving the gold answer to rank 1 on 3/5 queries, tying on 2 hard cross-vocabulary queries, and **never regressing**. New tests pin the wiring: nil reranker and `off` backend are no-ops (empty backend tag, order preserved), and an active backend reorders + reports its tag. `go test ./pkg/agentcontext` + `go vet` green; `cmd/mcp-agent-context` builds.
- **Mills HUD autonomous-merge north-star + truthful health banner** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{mills.svelte.ts,mills.systemHealth.ts}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go`): the operator's `KPIWriter` has emitted `pipeline_merged_runs` (autonomous merges in the rolling window) every reconciler tick since 2026-05-19, but **nothing surfaced it** — the frontend KPI-snapshot interface omitted the count metrics, so the north-star "is the Mills actually merging anything?" number was invisible. Worse, `computeSystemHealth` derived `merges_24h`/`escalations_24h` from `millsStore.pipelineRuns`, which holds **only active runs** (terminal merged/escalated runs are excluded by the active-only pipeline endpoint), so those counts were ~always 0 — the Overview banner literally hardcoded *"0 merged in 24h"* and the `broken` state was barely reachable. Now: the store's `MillsKPISnapshot.metrics` exposes `pipeline_merged_runs`/`pipeline_escalated_runs`/`escalation_rate`/`council_runs`/`pipeline_runs`; `computeSystemHealth` accepts authoritative `mergedRuns24h`/`escalatedRuns24h` overrides from the 1d KPI snapshot and prefers them over the blind run-list derivation; the Mills Overview gains a leading **"Auto-merges 24h"** north-star card (green when > 0); and the health banner reports the real merged count. Also corrects the stale `handleKPIs` comment that claimed the recorder hadn't shipped. (No backend logic change — the recorder already worked; `KPIWriter`'s 7 existing tests still cover emission.)
- **Mills HUD pipeline run history** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`): the Pipelines panel could only ever show **in-flight** runs — `handlePipelineRunsList` returned the active-state union, so a run *vanished the instant it merged, escalated, or paused*, leaving an operator no way to review what the Mills actually did (the comment even noted "Slice 5.2 will add a query param to surface recent terminal runs" — never landed). Adds `PipelineDAO.ListRecentTerminal` (terminal states `done`/`escalated`/`paused`, newest-first, `since`-windowed, limit-capped at 200) and a `state=terminal` mode on the list endpoint (with `since=`/`limit=`; back-compat default still returns active runs). The Pipelines panel gains an **Active / History** toggle: History loads finished runs (default last 7 days) and the table now also surfaces **MR** and **Cost** columns in both views. History refreshes on the same 15s cadence only while the History view is open, so an operator who never opens it pays nothing. Tests: `TestPipeline_ListRecentTerminal` (DAO) + `TestHandlePipelineRuns_TerminalHistory` (handler, incl. 400s on bad `since`/`limit`).
- **Mills HUD global autonomy kill-switch (plan 42 Slice 1b — the 5th day-2 action)** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_policy.go,config.go,main.go,server.go}`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{stores/mills.svelte.ts,components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte}`, `platform/gitops!216`, services/loom-core!611): closes the last of the five plan-42 day-2 HUD actions — a pause/resume autonomy button on the Mills Overview panel. It was deferred from !609 as the only action needing a cross-repo write. Routing (operator interview 2026-06-02) is a **GitOps auto-PR** flipping `policy.enabled` in `platform/gitops/k3s/mills/configmap-policy.yaml`, not a live write-through (Flux owns the file). New admin endpoint `POST /api/mills/policy/kill-switch` reads that file, flips the `# kill switch`-anchored `enabled:` line, and opens a branch+commit+MR (`action` ∈ `pause|resume|toggle`; a no-op opens no MR; `reason` is recorded in the commit + MR, which is the durable audit trail). The HUD proxies the route; the Overview button reads `policy_enabled`, opens the returned MR, and toasts the link. **Kill-test (2026-06-03):** the pipeline GitLab token (`loom-mills-gitlab`) gets 404 on `platform/gitops` and per the `platform/gitops/**` protected-path guardrail mustn't write there, so the kill-switch uses a **separate gitops-scoped token** (`loom-mills-gitops` secret; env `GITOPS_GITLAB_TOKEN`/`GITOPS_GITLAB_PROJECT`; unset → endpoint 503). Verified end-to-end that a gitops-scoped token creates the exact 1-line flip as a branch+commit+MR. New GitLab client methods `GetRawFile` + `CreateCommit` (commits API) + a `UserAgent` config (the public GitLab edge Cloudflare-403s the default Go UA with code 1010; the in-cluster operator bypasses the edge). Plan: `.loom/127`, `.loom/42`. **Live 1e operator kill-test PASSED 2026-06-03** against the deployed operator (`:20260603-170700`): an authenticated `POST /api/mills/policy/kill-switch {"action":"pause"}` returned `changed:true, previous_enabled:true, desired_enabled:false` and opened a real gitops MR with the exact 1-line `enabled: true→false` flip; the probe MR was closed unmerged and its branch deleted, so `main` policy stayed `enabled: true` (prod autonomy never actually paused).
- **Mills HUD day-2 action parity** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/{shared/millsActions.ts,CouncilPanel,PipelineRunDetail,AuditPanel}.svelte`, services/loom-core!609): closes the HUD↔CLI parity gap for Mills day-2 operator actions so they no longer force a drop to a terminal, after the plan-42 Slice 0 operator kill-test passed (2026-06-02; all 5 documented actions wanted, gate ≥3). Wires HUD buttons for 4 of the 5: **Replay council** + **Dryrun** (CouncilPanel, reusing `POST /api/mills/council/run` + `/dryrun`), **Force-escalate** (PipelineRunDetail, in-flight runs only, behind a confirm, reusing `/pipeline/runs/{id}/escalate`), and **Audit by MR iid** (AuditPanel search → matched run(s) → run drawer). Adds the one missing backend piece: `store.ListByMRIID` + a `mr_iid` query param on the pipeline-runs list handler so the audit lookup resolves the run(s) that produced a merged MR (Loop B attribution), terminal or not. New `shared/millsActions.ts` `runAdminAction()` wraps mutations with confirm+toast; all calls route through the admin-injecting HUD proxy so the browser never holds a token. The 5th action (global pause/resume autonomy kill-switch) is deferred to a follow-up — it needs a cross-repo GitOps auto-PR and the HUD proxy has no `platform/gitops` creds; autonomy state is already surfaced read-only on the Overview panel. Plan: `.loom/127`, `.loom/42`.
- **GitOps-safe k8s ops tools + scoped guardrail** (`cmd/mcp-ops/main.go`, `cmd/mcp-ops/main_test.go`, `pkg/policy/engine.go`, `pkg/policy/engine_test.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `pkg/generator/templates/policies/gitops_flux.yaml`, services/loom-core!608): routine operations (cycling pods, rolling restarts) kept forcing a fall back to raw shell `kubectl` because the `ops_mcp` tools errored on kubeconfig resolution, and the blanket `gitops_flux` guardrail false-denied any tool call whose args merely *mentioned* a blocked verb (e.g. a note recorded via `agent_context_add`). Adds `resolveKubeconfig` (robust fallback: explicit → `$KUBECONFIG` → `K3S_KUBECONFIG` → `~/.kube/k3s.yaml` → `~/.kube/config`), so the tools work when the configured `platform/gitops/.kube/k3s.yaml` is absent on a local/fallback run. Adds three sanctioned, non-drifting tools — `k8s_rollout_restart`, `k8s_delete_pod`, `k8s_rollout_status` (kind constrained to deployment/statefulset/daemonset). Adds `PolicyRule.AppliesTo`: command guardrails now only scan the args of command-executing tools (shells, `exec`, `ssh`, `run_repo_script`); the tool-name check stays unscoped. Guardrails default to that exec-tool scope; `applies_to: ["*"]` opts back into matching every tool. The `gitops_flux` message now points operators at the sanctioned tools. Raw shell `kubectl` mutations stay blocked — the dedicated tools are the smooth path. Mirror `platform/gitops/mcp/context/registry.yaml` kept in sync.
- **Registry-driven SKILL.md generation for the `zed` and `opencode` sync profiles** (`pkg/skills/generator.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_bundle.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_codex_gemini.go`, `pkg/skills/registry.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_regen.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_bundle_test.go`, services/loom-core!602): `zed` and `opencode` declared themselves skill-capable (`SkillsTarget`/`SkillsHomePath`/`SkillsDirectToHome` in `pkg/sync/manager.go`) but the skills generator had no case for them, so `loom sync skills all` failed both with `unknown target: {zed,opencode}` and they never received any of the registry's skills — only `codex`/`claude`/`kilocode`/`gemini`/`antigravity` did. Adds a generic Anthropic-compatible SKILL.md bundle writer (`generateBundleSkill`) shared by these targets, mirroring the codex/gemini layout (`<skillsHome>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` + copied scripts/references/assets, atomic SKILL.md write). `generateCodexSkillMD` is refactored into `generateBundleSkillMD(skill, target)` so `${SKILL_PATH}` resolves per target; `ResolveInstructions` resolves it to `$HOME/.config/{zed,opencode}/skills/<name>`. Because `Skill.IsEnabled` defaults to `true` for unspecified targets, the full 85-skill registry reaches both profiles with **no per-skill `targets` edits**. `ops_regen.go` points `OutputDir`/manifest at the parent of `SkillsHomePath` for these targets, since Zed's skills home (`$HOME/.config/zed`) differs from its MCP-config `HomeDir` (`Library/Application Support/Zed`). Verified: `loom sync skills all` now runs warning-free across all 8 profiles; `zed` and `opencode` each generate 81 SKILL.md bundles into `~/.config/{zed,opencode}/skills` with valid frontmatter and no stray app-support dir. New tests pin bundle generation, default-enablement, and `${SKILL_PATH}` resolution.
- **`agent_loop` consumes the proxy prefix-cache-hit-rate header (F4 row-195 follow-up)** (`pkg/agentloop/{metrics,client}.go`, `pkg/agentloop/metrics_test.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-loop/{tools,main}.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): mirrors the flexinfer client wiring ([flexinfer!536](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/536)) so the loom-core MCP agent loop closes the row-195 gap end-to-end. The flexinfer proxy now emits `X-Flexinfer-Prefix-Cache-Hit-Rate` when a request opts in via `X-Flexinfer-Want-Prefix-Hit` ([flexinfer!535](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/535)). `ChatClientConfig.WantPrefixHit` sets the opt-in header per request (library default `false`, backward-compatible); `TurnMetrics` gains `PrefixCacheHitRate` (`*float64`, `omitempty`) parsed from the response header — the **direct** prefix-cache signal even when the engine omits per-request `cached_tokens` (gemma4), distinct from the existing `PrefixHitRatio` (derived from `cached_tokens`). The `agent_loop_run` tool exposes a `want_prefix_hit` boolean param (default `true`, since the tool's purpose is the metrics report). New `metrics_test.go` pins: the header parses (and stays nil when absent), the opt-in header is sent + the rate round-trips without `cached_tokens`, and the default path sends no opt-in header. gofmt/`go vet`/`go test ./pkg/agentloop` green.
- **HUD mirror forwards per-session tool-call activity so distributed agents' "calls" surface on the central HUD** (`internal/hud/mirror/mirror.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_contracts.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_session.go`, `internal/hud/mirror/mirror_toolcall_test.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/toolcall_ingest_test.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): completes the "Live Sessions / 0 calls" fix chain (after !583 agent_id + !588 embedded event bridge) for the **distributed** case. A Mac/laptop agent's tool calls go through its OWN local daemon, never the central `mobile-hud` daemon's EventBus, and the central daemon's `/events/publish` is loopback-only — so the central HUD never saw their per-session activity (only its own `agent_context` polling). The one existing daemon-to-daemon channel is the HUD presence mirror (`LOOM_HUD_MIRROR_URL`, posts `/api/agent/heartbeat` every 15s). This rides it: the local daemon's EventLog (populated by the !588 embedded bridge from its own `emitAudit` tool.call emissions) is read per active session via the new `App.RecentToolCallsForSession(sessionID, sinceUnixNano, limit)` (newest-first, returns the batch + the max event ts), and the mirror attaches up to 25 fresh calls as `recent_tool_calls` on each heartbeat, advancing a per-session cursor (`tcCursor`) so the same calls are never re-forwarded. The central HUD's heartbeat handler unpacks `recent_tool_calls` and re-broadcasts each as a `tool.call` event (`ingestRecentToolCalls` → `BroadcastAgentEvent` → central EventLog → `buildSessionTrace`), backfilling `session_id`/`agent_id` from the heartbeat and bounding ingestion to 50/heartbeat. New `mirror.ToolCallReader` seam (wired via `SetToolCalls(app)`); `HeartbeatRequest` gains `recent_tool_calls`. Tests pin: mirror forwards then dedups on the next cycle; the App reader filters by session + cursor and advances maxTS past a truncated batch; the handler re-broadcasts with backfill + bound. **Activation**: ships in the binary; takes effect when a distributed daemon runs this build with the mirror configured (and the mirror healthy — requires the !579 recv-budget fix locally). Central receive half verified via a live `/api/agent/heartbeat` kill-test.
- **F4 tool-loop-as-prefix engine exposed as an MCP tool (`cmd/mcp-agent-loop` + `pkg/agentloop`)** (`cmd/mcp-agent-loop/{main,tools}.go`, `pkg/agentloop/*.go`, `Makefile`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`): ports the append-only ReAct engine — validated as a CLI in flexinfer slice 1 ([flexinfer!532](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/532)) and live-proven on the gemma4 APC canary (flexinfer matrix row 195, PASS 2026-06-01: 22.5× prompt growth with `upstream_ms` tracking the per-round token delta → prefix-cache reuse observed client-side) — into loom-core so any MCP client gets the same prefix-cache-paying loop. `pkg/agentloop` is mirrored from `services/flexinfer/internal/agentloop` (different Go modules → layout copied, not imported): `Conversation` enforces an append-only + mutability-ordered invariant (system+tools immutable prefix, history grows by `Append` only); `ChatClient` pins `X-Flexinfer-Cache-Key=session` and parses proxy instrumentation headers into per-turn `TurnMetrics` (`CachedTokens` stays nil when the engine omits `cached_tokens` — the gemma4 fallback); `Budget` encodes `maxModelLen − system − output` and returns a typed `BudgetError` (F4-413-as-feature, in-process) so the loop stops cleanly before HTTP 400; `FSTools` are real read-only, path-jailed `read_file`/`list_dir`. The new `cmd/mcp-agent-loop` server (mcpscaffold house style) exposes two tools: `agent_loop_run` (bounded live ReAct session returning a per-turn metrics report; **not** `always_allow`'d — hits an external endpoint + reads files) and `agent_loop_self_check` (offline wiring gate; `always_allow`'d, read-only). Wired into `MCP_SERVERS` + a Makefile target + a `registry.yaml` entry (`always_allow: [agent_loop_self_check]`, `FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL` env, 600s timeout). Verified: gofmt clean, `go vet`, `go test ./pkg/agentloop` (8 tests incl. the offline self-check), and a live stdio smoke of the built binary (`tools/list` → both tools; `agent_loop_self_check` → `passed: true`, all 5 checks green). Completes both F4 client forms (a CLI, b MCP tool).
- **`harvester-vm` spawn substrate wired into the prod `mobile-hud` deployment (Mills roadmap Slice A1 — deploy)** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`, platform/gitops!196): the `mobile-hud` Deployment (which runs the prod spawn orchestrator) now mounts the harvester admin kubeconfig from the `harvester-kubeconfig` Secret (loom-hub ns, added in gitops!196) at `/etc/harvester/kubeconfig` and sets `SPAWN_HARVESTER_KUBECONFIG` + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_NAMESPACE=default` + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_STORAGE_CLASS=longhorn-image-mc9ph` (stock Ubuntu image until the curated `mills-devbox-base` image lands) + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_NAD=default/lan10g`. Combined with the daemon-config fix below, `initSpawnOrchestrator` now constructs the `HarvesterVMBackend` (was silently k8s-only), so a `mills-canary-harvester-vm` item routes implement+tests to a KubeVirt VM. SSH user left unset → backend defaults to `agent` (Slice 2d.5c home-parity). **Security FIXME**: full hypervisor admin kubeconfig in a network-facing pod — to be scoped to a namespaced harvester ServiceAccount. Unblocks the Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test.
- **`harvester-vm` substrate now wireable from the prod daemon (Mills roadmap Slice A1)** (`internal/daemon/config.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed_test.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `.loom/126-plan-mills-full-vision-roadmap-2026-06-01.md`): the prod HUD/spawn orchestrator runs as `loomd → startEmbeddedHUD` (the `mobile-hud` deployment), **not** the `loom hud` subcommand — and that daemon path silently dropped all nine `SpawnHarvester*` config fields. `initSpawnOrchestrator` gates harvester-vm registration on `SpawnHarvesterKubeconfig != ""`, so with the field always empty in prod, **every `harvester-vm`-targeted spawn fell back to k8s regardless of deployment env**. Added the nine `SpawnHarvester*` fields to `EmbeddedHUDConfig` and extracted the env→`hud.Config` mapping into a pure, testable `buildEmbeddedHUDConfig(cfg, registryPath)` that reads `SPAWN_HARVESTER_{KUBECONFIG,BASE_IMAGE,NAMESPACE,STORAGE_CLASS,NAD,DEFAULT_VCPUS,DEFAULT_MEM_MI,DEFAULT_DISK_GI,SSH_USER}` (file config wins; env is the K8s-injection fallback). `SPAWN_HARVESTER_SSH_USER` is intentionally left unset so the backend applies its `agent` default for Slice 2d.5c home-parity; the stale `loom hud` flag help (`default: ubuntu`) is corrected to `agent`. **Inert until opted in**: with no env set, `SpawnHarvesterKubeconfig` stays empty and the substrate stays unregistered (current k8s-only behavior). New tests pin env-mapping, the safe empty default, and file-config precedence. This unblocks the Phase-A kill-test (first end-to-end autonomous canary merge through harvester-vm); the remaining A1 step is mounting the harvester kubeconfig into `mobile-hud` + setting the env in `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`.
- **`harvester-vm` codex home-parity kill-test PASSED + three live-only fixes (Slice 2d.5d)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm{,_objects}_test.go`, `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`, services/loom-core!577): the Slice 2d.5c kill-test owed in the entry below was run **live on-LAN** (`TestHarvesterVMBackend_Integration_CodexHomeParity`, `integration` build tag) and **PASSED 2026-06-01 (110s)** — but only after surfacing **three bugs the shape-only unit tests could not catch**, each a hard blocker on a real codex `SecretMount` booting + authenticating. (1) **Inline cloud-init 2048-byte cap**: the real 4784-byte codex `auth.json` (base64 ≈ 6.4 KB) embedded in `cloudInitNoCloud.userData` is rejected at VM-create by the KubeVirt admission webhook (`userdata exceeds 2048 byte limit`), so the VM never boots. Fixed by delivering cloud-init out-of-line via a per-VM Secret + `cloudInitNoCloud.secretRef` (new `CloudInitSecret` field + `secretGVR`). **Gotcha**: the wire key is `secretRef`, **not** `userDataSecretRef` (only the *network* ref is `networkDataSecretRef`); the wrong key is silently dropped, then the VM is rejected for "no userdatasource set". (2) **Base image lacks `qemu-guest-agent`**: stock-Ubuntu `longhorn-image-mc9ph` boots to VMI `Running` but `AgentConnected` never flips and no IP is ever reported, so `waitForVMReady` times out and the backend can never SSH in. Fixed with a self-healing first `runcmd` (`command -v qemu-ga || apt-get install -y qemu-guest-agent`), a no-op once the curated `mills-devbox-base` image lands. (3) **PVC/Secret ownerRef race**: the post-create ownerRef `Update` lost a conflict to CDI/Longhorn (`object has been modified`), orphaning a 20Gi PVC per run. Fixed with `setOwnerRefWithRetry` (re-Get + `retry.RetryOnConflict`) for both the PVC and the cloud-init Secret; `CleanupBuilds` now also label-sweeps orphan cloud-init Secrets. **Live PASS evidence** (SSH as `agent`): `$HOME=/home/agent`; `/home/agent/.codex.auth/auth.json` owned `agent:agent` and byte-for-byte == the k3s `cluster-agent-auth`/`codex-auth-json` secret; `~/.codex/auth.json` symlink resolves; re-run clean with no orphaned PVC/Secret. New regression assertion pins the `qemu-guest-agent` self-heal runcmd; `secretRef`/Secret-shape assertions replace the old inline-`userData` reads across the backend test files. **Honest scope**: the harness proves codex's auth file is in place, agent-owned, and resolvable (the load-bearing home-parity claim) — running the `codex` binary itself (the final "doesn't 401" check) is owed to the curated `mills-devbox-base` image and recorded as a separate end-to-end step. Full trail (off-LAN BLOCKED → on-LAN FAILED → fixes → PASS) in `.loom/local/handoffs/mills-harvester-home-parity-killtest-2026-05-30.md`.
- **`harvester-vm` agent-user home-dir parity (Slice 2d.5c)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm{,_objects}_test.go`, `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`, `.loom/125-iteration-plan-mills-harvester-home-parity-slice2d5c-2026-05-30.md`, services/loom-core!576): the harvester-vm devbox backend now provisions a cloud-init `agent` user with `home: /home/agent` (was `ubuntu`), mirroring the K8s spawn pod's uid-1000 `agent` user so mounted auth files resolve at the `$HOME/.…` paths every agent CLI expects (`injectAgentConfig` + every `/home/agent` SecretMount path). `HarvesterVMBackendConfig.SSHUser` now defaults to `agent` (new `defaultHarvesterSSHUser = vmAgentUser`); new `vmAgentUser`/`vmAgentHome` consts. **Riskiest-assumption discipline surfaced two cloud-init ordering traps**: (1) the `write-files` module runs *before* `users-groups`, so the agent user does not exist when secret files are written — they are now written **root-owned** (`vmWriteFilesOwner = root:root`); (2) pre-creating the user in `bootcmd` is unsafe because some cloud-init versions skip `ssh_authorized_keys` application for a pre-existing user, breaking SSH login. Robust fix: the `users:` stanza creates `agent` canonically (reliable SSH key) and a `runcmd` step `chown -R agent:agent /home/agent` (`vmSecretFileOwner`) hands the files over after the user exists. New `TestBuildVMManifest_CreatesAgentUserForHomeParity` pins `name: agent` / `home: /home/agent` / no `ubuntu` user / root-owned write_files / the runcmd chown; owner + SSHUser assertions updated across the backend test files. **Kill-test still owed**: unit tests pin the manifest shape but cannot prove a live VM boots with codex auth working — a live boot (SSH as `agent`, confirm `~/.codex/auth.json` resolves to the mounted file) must run before declaring codex unblocked on harvester-vm (queued as the next slice).
- **Mills `pipeline.stage_substrate` policy field (harvester-vm Slice 2 schema)** (`pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/policy_test.go`, `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`, services/loom-core!TBD): lands the policy-schema half of Slice 2 from the harvester-vm substrate spec. Adds `PipelinePolicy.StageSubstrate map[string]string` (yaml `pipeline.stage_substrate`) + `Policy.SubstrateForStage(stage) string` accessor with `SubstrateDefault = "k8s"` fallback for nil/missing/empty entries. Validation closes both sides: keys must be one of `plan_slice`/`research`/`implement`/`tests`/`pr_self_review` (the spawn-driven stages — `mr`/`ci_watch`/`merge`/`cleanup` are GitLabWorker-driven and have no sandbox to choose), values must be one of `k8s`/`harvester-vm`. New tests pin: nil-policy fallback (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Defaults`), per-stage lookup matrix (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_LookupHits`), validation errors on unknown keys/values/empty strings (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Validate`), and a YAML roundtrip with the canonical spec example (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Roundtrip`). **No runtime consumer yet** — the field is operator-configurable but the spawn dispatcher still picks backend at startup via `DEVBOX_BACKEND` env. Slice 2.5 (next, recorded in the spec) threads the per-stage substrate through `SpawnWorker.Run` → `pipeline.SpawnRequest` → `hudSpawnRequestBody` → HUD spawn orchestrator so the HUD picks the matching backend instance at pod-start time. Closes the schema portion of the merge-blocking Slice 2 acceptance gate (1 successful end-to-end auto-merge via `harvester-vm`).
- **HUD secondary stores join staleness tracking (B3 follow-up #2)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{memory,cost,coordination,mergeQueue,shuttle}.svelte.ts`, `.loom/117-implementation-plan-hud-ux-overhaul-2026-05-15.md`, services/loom-core!TBD): five of the six secondary OverviewPanel stores aligned to 60s polling in MR !559 now also get full staleness coverage matching the canonical B3 pattern (fleet/tasks/sandbox/spawn/servers/stream). Each adds `staleAfter = 90_000` ms + `isStale` getter via `isStaleFromTimestamp`, calls `stalenessStore.register(<name>, ...)` after instantiation, switches the fallback poll predicate to `!eventStore.connected || this.isStale`, and bumps the `startPolling` default 30000 → 60000 to match the OverviewPanel callsite. `rbacStore` deliberately skipped — it subscribes only to `access.denied` push events (no snapshot cadence), so a "stale" flag would fire on every quiet system regardless of SSE health. ConnectionBanner's existing stale pill (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/ConnectionBanner.svelte`) now surfaces `memory`/`cost`/`coordination`/`mergeQueue`/`shuttle` when SSE goes silent, so a silent SSE failure on `hud.memory`/`hud.cost`/`hud.fleet` no longer leaves their tiles silently stale. Plan-doc 117 follow-up list updated: this item ✅; remaining open item is `millsStore`+`otelStore` SSE wiring (each needs a new `hud.mills`/`hud.otel.*` event family before they can join the 60s cohort).

The Overview $effect comment is updated to spell out the SSE-have vs. SSE-have-not split so the next agent doesn't bump mills/otel without first wiring `hud.pipeline*`/`hud.otel.*` subscriptions. Plan-doc 117 follow-up list updated: 6/8 marked ✅, mills + otel SSE wiring + staleness coverage for the 6 newly-aligned stores recorded as the next candidate slices.
