- **mcp-icc Status Draft composer + triage filters + per-tool attribution header** (`cmd/mcp-icc/tools.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc/tools_drafts.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc/tools_reads.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc/tools_writes.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc/tools_quick_capture.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc-capture/tools.go`, `internal/iccclient/client.go`, `cmd/mcp-icc/README.md`, services/loom-core!555 paired with services/project-management!267): closes the gaps surfaced while authoring the four daily-PM workflow skills (icc-daily-pm-loop, icc-weekly-status-loop, icc-triage-loop, icc-handoff-prep) so the workflows compose entirely against MCP with no `iccctl` fallback or post-filtering. **New tools**: `icc_draft_status_compose` wraps `POST /api/drafts/status` (SD-1/SD-2/SD-EA composer; read-shaped, no write gate); `icc_artifact_list` is the structured-filter list distinct from FTS `icc_search`; `icc_inbox_summary` returns one-call per-project + workspace totals of unpromoted code_refs + un-promoted session_links + active `_inbox` artifacts. **Extended tools**: `icc_code_ref_list` and `icc_session_link_list` accept `unpromoted=true` / `unattributed=true` filters; `icc_artifact_reclassify` description documents the new `already_classified=true` no-op marker returned by the backend so callers can render idempotent confirmations without comparing timestamps. **Per-tool attribution**: new `iccclient.WithTool(ctx, name)` context helper threads the MCP tool name through `setTrustedContextHeaders`, which now sets `X-ICC-MCP-Tool: <tool_name>` on outbound requests. Backend already reads the header for the `icc_mcp_writes_total{tool,outcome}` metric (per `services/project-management/docs/connectors.md` §"MCP Write Path"); the metric stays at zero today only because the iccclient never sent the header. Helpers in `cmd/mcp-icc/*` (`makeListReader`, `makeCreateHandler`, `makeIDPayloadHandler`, `makeIDInURLHandler`, `makeProjectScopedReader`, `makeGetByIDReader`, individual handlers) inject `WithTool` automatically using their existing `label` arg; `cmd/mcp-icc-capture/*` wraps each registered handler with a small `withTool(name, h)` helper at registration time. Handlers themselves are unchanged. Test coverage: new unit tests cover composer body shape, list-filter forwarding, header attribution across list/write/POST flows, and a representative capture-side wrap. Tool counts updated in `cmd/mcp-icc/README.md` and `tools.go` comment: 23 read tools (22 GET-shaped + 1 POST-shaped composer) + 16 write tools + 1 convenience.
- **`harvester-vm` devbox backend (Slice 1)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm{,_objects,_integration}_test.go`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/manager.go`, services/loom-core!538): new parallel-to-`k8s` Backend implementation that runs each mills stage inside a fresh KubeVirt VM on the existing Harvester host instead of a buildah-in-pod sandbox on k3s. Closes the 22%-of-failures buildah-DinD class and the 21% pod-GC-race class measured in 14d prod telemetry (see `.loom/local/handoffs/mills-autonomy-killtest-2026-05-24.md`). Substrate validated end-to-end in the Slice 0 + Slice 1.5 kill-tests (`.loom/local/handoffs/mills-harvester-vm-{killtest,slice15}-2026-05-25.md`): single Harvester host hosts 4 concurrent VMs at 72% CPU / 78% MEM peak; cold-boot 53s wall-clock to `Phase=Running`, DHCP IPv4 visible via `qemu-guest-agent`, SSH-reachable on the per-VM ed25519 keypair. Backend implements all 11 `Backend` interface methods via `client-go` dynamic.Interface against KubeVirt v1 CRDs; manifest builder in `harvester_vm_objects.go` is a pure function with 100% test coverage (full coverage 52.1% of statements; SSH-path methods deferred to gated integration test). Pre-baked base image build pipeline lives in `platform/gitops!180` and closes the 60s boot target. Selected via `DEVBOX_BACKEND=harvester-vm`; new env vars `DEVBOX_HARVESTER_*` (`KUBECONFIG`, `BASE_IMAGE`, `NAMESPACE`, `STORAGE_CLASS`, `NETWORK_ATTACH_DEF`, `DEFAULT_VCPUS|MEM_MI|DISK_GI`, `SSH_USER`). Mills opt-in (Slice 2: `policy.stage_substrate` per-stage map) lands in a follow-up; this MR is just the substrate. Real merge-blocking gate is Slice 2 acceptance: 1 successful end-to-end auto-merge of a mills canary via `harvester-vm` (prod metric currently 0/56). Spec: `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`. Side-effect platform MRs deprecated the broken `default/lan10g-whereabouts` NAD (`platform/gitops!178/!179`) after it allocated the LAN gateway to a Slice 1.5 test VM.
- **Antigravity 2.0 MCP config + native hooks support** (`pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`, `pkg/generator/configs_antigravity.go`, `pkg/sync/manager.go`, `pkg/generator/doctor.go`, `pkg/generator/VENDOR_SPECS.md`, services/loom-core!TBD): Antigravity now generates and syncs the dedicated `mcp_config.json` file expected by Google Antigravity 2.0. Workspace config moves from the legacy `.antigravity/mcp.json` mirror to `.agents/mcp_config.json`; home sync targets `~/.gemini/antigravity/mcp_config.json` for MCP and `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json` for hooks. The new Antigravity-specific `hooks.json` generator emits Google's top-level hook-name schema, JSON stdout decisions, `PreInvocation`/`PostToolUse`/fully-idle `Stop` lifecycle hooks, `run_command` GitOps guardrails, telemetry event emission, and an `ask_permission` hook that auto-allows the `mcp(loom/)` namespace via `permissionOverrides`.
- **Mills KPIWriter populates the five frontend Overview cards + Mills sub-tab count badges + Pipelines run-id shortening** (`pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer_test.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/ViewShell.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, services/loom-core!TBD): the existing `KPIWriter` was already recording `gate_pass_rate` + `cost_per_merged_pipeline_usd` (the latter gated on `mergedRuns > 0`), but `MillsKPIRow.svelte` renders five cards consuming a different key set (`cost_per_merged_change_usd`, `slice_to_merge_p50_seconds`, `gate_pass_rate`, `auto_merge_rate`, `regression_rate`) — so on the live cluster only one card lit up. **Backend**: `KPIWriter.snapshot` now also computes `cost_per_merged_change_usd` (today aliases per-pipeline cost until backlog→change attribution lands in Phase 8+), `auto_merge_rate` (= `mergedRuns / (mergedRuns + escalatedRuns)` — Mills always uses the auto-merge path so this measures autonomous-success rate), `regression_rate` (= `escalatedRuns / (mergedRuns + escalatedRuns)` — best-effort proxy until post-merge regression tracking exists), `council_roi` (= `mergedRuns / councilCost`), and `slice_to_merge_p50_seconds` (median wall-clock from `started_at` to `ended_at` over runs that reached state=done in the window). Median is computed in Go because SQLite lacks a built-in percentile function; new `PipelineDAO.ListByStateSince(ctx, state, since)` returns only the merged runs in the window so the writer doesn't pull the full table. All five keys are conditionally omitted when their denominator is zero so cards render "—" rather than misleading "$0" / "0%". New tests `TestKPIWriter_FrontendKeys_PopulatedWhenDataAvailable` / `TestKPIWriter_FrontendKeys_OmittedWhenInsufficientData` / `TestKPIWriter_P50_OddCountReturnsExactMiddle` pin the present/absent contract and both median branches. **Frontend (polish)**: `ViewShell` gains an optional `count?: number` field on its `subViews` prop, rendered as a small pill next to the tab label when `count > 0`; `App.svelte` enriches Mills sub-tabs (`pipelines`, `backlog`, `council`, `eval`, `policy`) with live counts from `millsStore` so an operator sees at a glance which tabs hold activity. Mills `PipelinesPanel.svelte` ids (~40 chars) are elided to `prefix-18…suffix-4` in the table while the row's `title` keeps the full id for hover + the drilldown drawer's click-to-copy. `pnpm build` clean; `go test ./pkg/mills/...` green across 13 packages.
- **HUD Mills > Pipelines pipeline-run drilldown drawer** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelineRunDetail.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!444): closes the long-standing dead-click on the Mills > Pipelines table noted during the 2026-05-17 Mills UI/telemetry review. Clicking any pipeline row now opens a right-edge drawer rendering the full `{run, stages, gates}` payload that `GET /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}` (handler at `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go:44-67`) already returns — **no backend changes**. Drawer surfaces: (a) **run header** with state pill, total `CostUSD`, attempt count, duration, worktree path, GitLab MR link, parent-run / backlog cross-refs, click-to-copy run id; (b) **stages timeline** where each `StageResult` is expandable to show `StartedAt → EndedAt` times, `SpawnID`, an artifacts kv table, and the persisted `LogTail`, with rows color-tinted by `Outcome` (`success` / `gate_fail` / `error` / pending in-flight); (c) **gates list** where `GateOutcome` rows are sorted fails-first and the `Reasons[]` field is surfaced verbatim so a `gate_fail` is actionable without diving into operator logs. New `selectedRunID` + `pipelineDetailByRun` store state plus `openRunDetail()` / `closeRunDetail()` / `refreshOpenPipelineDetail()` methods; the refresh hook is wired into the existing 15s `fetchAll` poll so an open drawer on an in-flight run updates its stage timeline live without forcing close+reopen, and is a no-op when no drawer is open. `PipelineRun` interface gains the detail-only fields (`WorktreePath`, `MRIID`, `CostUSD`, `ParentSessionID`) — list responses can still omit them. Rows in `PipelinesPanel` become `role=button` with `tabindex=0` plus Enter/Space keyboard handler, hover/`focus-visible`/`selected` styling, and the drawer is mounted at panel root following the existing `AuditDrawer` scrim+aside pattern. `pnpm build` clean (the a11y warnings on scrim/aside match the existing `AuditDrawer` / `DetailDrawer` patterns).
- **HUD responsive layout + operator embed subset (Slice B5)** (`internal/hud/app.go`, `internal/hud/routes.go`, `internal/hud/domain_adapters.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/embedConfig.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/router.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/{DataTable,DetailDrawer}.svelte`, `internal/hud/app_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): two coordinated pieces of the HUD UX overhaul. **(1) Responsive ≤800px layout.** Extends the existing 768px breakpoint to 800px and reflows the top `.nav-tabs` into a bottom-fixed bar (thumb-zone navigation) with `min-height: 44px` tap targets and `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` aware padding. `DataTable` switches to stacked-card mode at ≤800px — header hidden, each `<tr>` becomes a vertically-stacked block card so wide horizontal tables stay readable on phones without horizontal scroll. `DetailDrawer` becomes full-screen at ≤800px with a slide-up animation, stopping short of the bottom-fixed nav. **(2) Operator embed subset.** New `EmbedSubset` field on `hud.Config` plus `--subset` CLI flag (`loom hud --embed --subset operator`) gates the HUD UI to Overview + Operations/Fleet + Activity/Stream when running inside another product's iframe. Defense-in-depth: backend skips non-allowlisted domain registrations at handler-register time (sandbox, spawn, mills, graph, weaver, codebase, shuttle, merge, handoff, webhook); frontend reads `/api/hud/config` at boot and filters the nav + guards `router.navigate` to redirect hidden routes to Overview. New backend tests: `TestHUDConfig_DefaultIsFullSubset`, `TestHUDConfig_OperatorSubsetAllowlist`, `TestDomainRegistry_OperatorSubsetSkipsNonAllowlisted`, `TestDomainRegistry_FullSubsetKeepsEverything`. `pnpm build` clean; `go test ./internal/hud/...` green (30 packages).

### Fixed
- **Shuttle + context-health HUD monitors stop blowing the daemon recv budget (last two offenders on the heavy session-list path)** (`internal/hud/shuttle/monitor.go`, `internal/hud/shuttle/engine.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/context_health.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list_test.go`, services/loom-core!610): the recurring `agent_session_list` 3s-timeout cascade (`project_hud_no_agents_session_list_timeout`) resurfaced at ~875 sessions and surfaced to the user as **"flaky" gitlab/devbox MCP drops** — a slow poll repeatedly reconnects the shared upstream transport, and under `--hub-prefer` that lands as `transport closed` on unrelated servers. The `!578`/`!591` light/active migration had been applied to `fleet.go` and `mirror.go` but **not** to two other hot-loop monitors that still called the heavy non-light `AgentBridge.Sessions()` (`light=false`, `limit=1000`): the shuttle monitor (3s poll) and the context-health monitor (5s poll). On the non-light path each zero-entry ended session triggers its own Qdrant scroll for stat recompute; `a4e84836` bounded that fan-out to `limit`, but at `limit=1000` every poll still fanned out to ~1000 serial round-trips and blew the budget. A kill-test (`.loom/brainstorm-session-list-root-cause-2026-06-03.md`) confirmed the light path stays under budget at N=875, so the recompute fan-out — not the O(N) scroll — is the dominant term. **Fix**: shuttle → `FleetSessions()` (and `shuttle.Bridge` narrowed to the light method so the heavy path can't be reused there by mistake); context-health → `ActiveSessions()` (it only inspects active sessions and reads fields all carried by the light projection, with active token/entry stats overlaid server-side — lossless, and shrinks the fetched set from ~1000 to the live-agent count). **Source-side defense**: the non-light recompute fan-out is now hard-capped at `maxSessionListRecompute=64` regardless of caller `limit` (sorted `StartedAt DESC`, so the most recent keep recomputed stats; the tail keeps persisted counts), so no future large-limit caller can reproduce the cascade. Regression `TestSessionList_RecomputeFanoutHardCapped` (limit=1000 over 2000 zero-entry sessions → recompute==64, results still len 1000). Don't-persist-empty-sessions (inflow) and a bounded-query index remain de-scoped scaling follow-ups per the kill-test.
- **Hub-routed interactive tool calls carry their identity to the central daemon (relocated keystone of the "0 calls" chain)** (`internal/daemon/callpipeline_stages.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_hub_identity_test.go`, services/loom-core!607): an interactive agent's MCP calls that route over the hub to the **central** daemon left the forwarding daemon as a bare `{name, arguments}` `tools/call`; `buildForwardRequest` (`callpipeline_stages.go:302-334`) did not propagate `p.params.AgentID`/`SessionID`. The central daemon then parsed `agent_id:""`/`session_id:""` into its `callParams`, so `emitAudit` stamped an empty `agent_id` and (with `session_id` empty) published no `tool.call` event — every HUD "Live Sessions" row showed **0 calls** despite all upstream plumbing (!583/!588/!591/!592/!593) working. This is the keystone relocated in `.loom/spec-hud-agent-id-correlation-2026-06-01.md` "Correction 2026-06-02": the deployed kill-test proved Slices 1+2 (proxy `<type>-<WS_HASH>` derivation + HUD base-match) are inert on the cross-host path because the gap is the forwarding daemon's hub egress, not the proxy id. **Fix**: `buildForwardRequest` now merges the call's **effective** `agent_id` (from `params`, or resolved from the proxy session lease's presence id via `resolveEffectiveAgentID`), `session_id`, and `agent_type` into the forwarded params **only on hub egress** (`p.target == router.TargetHub`); local-target calls already stamp from `params` directly and are untouched. Handles both the constructed `{name, arguments}` and verbatim raw-`params` branches via the new `injectForwardIdentity` helper, and no-ops when there is nothing to inject or the params are not a JSON object. **Verifies the previously-UNVERIFIED sub-assumption in-binary**: the receiving daemon parses those top-level fields back into `callParams` through the same `handleCall → parseAndResolve` path (no special hub-ingress parser) — `TestParseAndResolve_ParsesForwardedIdentity` — which `emitAudit` then stamps and publishes (`TestEmitAuditPublishesSessionScopedToolCall`). Egress coverage pins the constructed branch, the verbatim-params branch, the session-lease fallback, and no-op on local target. **Remaining gate** (next slice, not in this MR): the deployed kill-test on the central HUD with a real agent's hub-routed call before any downstream HUD base-match work ships.
- **HUD overview stops showing contradictory fleet counts and de-janks the hero/empty states** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/LiveSessionsCard.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/overview/HeroSummary.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/EconomicsPanel.svelte`, services/loom-core!604): the dashboard rendered three+ disagreeing numbers for one fleet at once — Live Sessions header "18 active", status bar "17 active sessions", and an Attention lane "22 agents" (with only 2 agents live). **(1)** The Live Sessions header counted `liveSessionsStore` (SSE event store) entries while the status bar counts the REST `fleetStore.activeSessions` snapshot, so the two sources drift by a session or two. `LiveSessionsCard` now takes a canonical `sessionCount` prop fed from `fleetStore.activeSessions.length` (the same derived value the status bar uses), so the headline and status bar can never disagree; the per-row list still streams from `liveSessionsStore`. **(2)** The "Attention" lane value is a coordination needs-review backlog that can exceed the live-agent count, so "N agents" beside "2 live agents" read as a contradiction — relabeled to "N flagged". **(3)** `HeroSummary`'s two-column grid let the short headline float at the top of the taller attention-lane column, leaving a large void; `align-items: center` balances the block. **(4)** `EconomicsPanel`'s bespoke empty-state icon now uses the shared `EmptyState` chip treatment (bordered circle) for one visual language across panels, keeping its compact inline layout. Frontend-only (`dist` rebuilt). Does **not** touch the separate "0 calls / no captured activity" chain, which is daemon `tool.call` session_id correlation tracked above.
- **Spawn telemetry summary persists under the spawn's own session_id (Mills A2 unblock)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/spawn/types.go`, `internal/hud/spawn_telemetry_persist_test.go`, services/loom-core!600): the spawn orchestrator created an agent-context session at spawn-start (`spawnSessionID`) but only handed it to the live telemetry accumulator — it was never stored on the durable `spawn.State`, so terminal transitions called `AgentBridge.ContextAdd("", …)` with an empty `session_id` in both `persistTelemetrySummary` and `failSpawn`'s error-entry write. `agent_context_add` requires a `session_id` that resolves to an existing session (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_context_add.go`), so every spawn logged `failed to persist spawn telemetry summary … session_id: is required` and the agent's turn-level stdout/`turn_count` was dropped — which made the Mills Slice A2 harvester-vm empty-diff (`codex turn_count=0`) impossible to debug. Adds `State.SessionID` (stamped after `StartSession`, flushed when the spawn is marked running) and a `resolveSpawnSessionID` helper (resolution order: spawn session → `Request.ParentSessionID` → agent's active session). Both call sites thread the resolved session and skip the write (debug log) when none resolves, instead of emitting a guaranteed-to-fail call. New tests pin the resolution order, the spawn-origin persist path, and the skip-when-no-session case.
- **Interactive agents' tool calls now correlate to their HUD session (keystone of the "0 calls" chain)** (`cmd/loom/proxy_heartbeat.go`, `internal/hud/app_routes_operations.go`, tests, services/loom-core!TBD): completes !583/!588/!591/!592/!593. Tool calls reached the HUD with an empty `agent_id` and a proxy-lease `session_id` that never equals the agent-context roster session id, so `eventMatchesSessionTrace`/`filterSessionTraceAuditEntries` (which can only correlate via `agent_id`+time-bounds, the session-id namespaces differ) dropped every call → "0 calls" everywhere. Root cause: the proxy derived a process-scoped `claude-code-<host>-<pid>` id (only codex used the stable workspace key), and `--agent-hint`/`LOOM_AGENT_ID` were empty. Fix (Option A, .loom/spec-hud-agent-id-correlation-2026-06-01.md): (1) `stableWorkspaceProxyAgentID` now applies to ALL platforms, deriving `<type>-<cksum(git toplevel)>` — the SAME base the CLI session hooks register (`hookAgentIDBootstrap`); (2) the HUD base-matches `agent_id` boundary-aware (`agentIDMatchesSession`: exact OR `<base>-<scope>` either direction) since the proxy cannot know the per-Claude-session suffix (no session_id is exposed to MCP servers). **KNOWN LIMITATION**: workspace-grouped — an agent's activity may surface under multiple concurrent same-workspace sessions of the same type; exact per-session attribution needs Claude Code to pass its session_id to MCP servers. Also stops the per-restart host-pid roster proliferation. Tests pin the base-match (exact / base↔scoped / no cross-workspace or cross-type leakage / boundary-without-separator) and that every platform now yields the workspace-scoped id.
- **HUD mirror uses the lightweight active-session list so it stops timing out and actually posts heartbeats** (`internal/hud/mirror/mirror.go`, `internal/hud/mirror/mirror_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the presence mirror read `AgentBridge.Sessions()` (the heavy full-fidelity path: `limit=1000`, no `light` projection) for per-agent enrichment. On a daemon with a large session history that call blows the 3s tools/call recv budget every cycle (`agent_session_list … timeout during recv after 3s`), and because it runs before any heartbeat is posted it starved the ENTIRE mirror — so a distributed agent's presence AND tool-call telemetry never reached the central HUD. Switched the mirror to `ActiveSessions()` (active-only + `light=true`, the !579 variant), which stays inside the budget and is exactly what the mirror needs (it only enriches active agents). `PresenceReader.Sessions()` → `ActiveSessions()`; fake reader updated.
- **Mirror-forwarded tool calls are ingested on every heartbeat path, not just the happy path** (`internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_session.go`, `internal/hud/app_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): follow-up to !591. `ingestRecentToolCalls` was placed at the heartbeat handler tail, but mirror heartbeats use `ensure_session=true` whose bootstrap branch returns early (handler lines ~159/184) — so a first-sight distributed agent's forwarded `recent_tool_calls` were silently dropped (caught by a live kill-test: heartbeat 200 OK but no `tool.call` in the trace). Tool-call ingestion is independent of presence registration, so it now runs unconditionally up front, right after body decode/validate and before any presence/coalesce return. New App-level regression `TestHandler_AgentHeartbeat_IngestsToolCallsOnBootstrapPath` drives the ensure_session bootstrap-failure (502) path and asserts the `tool.call` still lands in the EventLog.
- **HUD "Live Sessions" panel no longer shows "no captured activity / 0 calls" for every session — the embedded HUD now consumes the daemon EventBus into its activity log** (`internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon_call.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge.go`, `internal/hud/runtime.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge_test.go`, `internal/daemon/toolcall_emit_test.go`, services/loom-core!588): follow-on to the audit `agent_id` fix (!583). The in-cluster HUD (`mobile-hud`) runs as `loomd → startEmbeddedHUD`, which **bypasses the standalone SSE event consumer** (`runtime.go` `startStandaloneEventConsumer`, gated on `cfg.MetricsAddr`). `StartMonitors` created an `EventLog` but **nothing ever subscribed the daemon EventBus to it**, so no daemon event (`session.*`, `agent.status.change`, `tool.call*`) reached the `EventLog` that `buildSessionTrace` reads — every per-session trace was empty regardless of `agent_id` stamping. Confirmed by a live kill-test: an authenticated `POST /events/publish` of an event carrying a real `session_id` never surfaced in `GET /api/sessions/{id}/trace`. Three coupled parts: **(1) embedded bridge** — `startEmbeddedHUD` subscribes `d.EventBus()` and forwards each event to the new `App.IngestDaemonEvent(eventType, ts, data)`, which appends the activity types to the `EventLog`, refreshes the fleet on lifecycle events, and broadcasts to the SSE hub (the embedded-mode equivalent of the standalone consumer; goroutine exits on ctx cancel). **(2) source** — `emitAudit` publishes a session-scoped `tool.call` event (carrying `session_id` + the resolved `agent_id`), gated on `params.SessionID != ""` so ambient/ID-less daemon traffic stays off the bus. **(3) sink parity** — the standalone consumer (`runtime.go`) also appends `tool.call`/`tool.call.start`/`tool.call.end`. `tool.call*` is excluded from fleet-refresh (high-frequency, roster-neutral). New tests: `IngestDaemonEvent` surfaces a session-scoped `tool.call` in `buildSessionTrace` (isolates other sessions, ignores non-logged types, nil-safe before monitors wire); `emitAudit` publishes the scoped event and stays silent without a `session_id`. **Scope**: clears activity for agents whose calls route through *this* daemon (incl. session lifecycle for agents using the central agent-context). Routing distributed (remote-daemon) agents' tool-call telemetry to the central HUD is a tracked follow-up.
- **`agent_session_list` recv-budget timeout bounded at the source so the HUD fleet poller stops timing out on large session histories** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list_test.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_session.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_session.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, services/loom-core!579): the `FleetMonitor` polls `agent_session_list` every ~5s with no status filter and `limit=1000`. Once thousands of ended/summarized sessions accumulate, the call exceeded the daemon's 3s `tools/call` recv cap and timed out on every refresh (`fleet: failed to fetch sessions ... tools/call timeout during recv after 3s`), spamming WARN logs; pre-!578 it also blanked the agent roster (the "No active agents" bug). !578 added a client-side active-only fallback — this removes the timeout itself. **Two coupled costs:** (1) the per-session stat recompute (`countContextEntries`, wired in `service.go`) ran *inside the scroll loop* for every one of up to `sessionListScrollCap` (10000) points — each `EntryCount==0` ended session (the shape of short hook-driven sessions) fired its own Qdrant scroll, so one list call fanned out into thousands of serial round-trips, **independent of `limit`**; (2) the full per-session payload for ~1000 rows is large. **Fix A (root cause):** move the recompute *after* sort+truncate so it runs at most `limit` times instead of once per scrolled point (O(scrollCap) → O(limit)); benefits every `agent_session_list` caller. **Fix B (payload):** new optional `light` projection to `agent_session_list` returning only the fleet essentials (id, agent_id, namespace, project, status, started_at, ended_at, entry_count, total_tokens, parent/root ids) and skipping the recompute entirely — in-memory live stats are still overlaid, so active sessions keep accurate token counts. New `AgentBridge.FleetSessions()` (light unfiltered) + `ActiveSessions()` passing `light:true`; `FleetMonitor.Refresh` uses both, with the !578 active-only fallback retained as defense-in-depth. Regression test `TestSessionList_LargeHistoryBoundsRecompute` seeds 2000 `EntryCount==0` ended sessions and asserts full mode recomputes ≤`limit` (pre-fix fan-out was ~2000) and light mode recomputes 0 while omitting the heavy fields. Follow-up (separate): periodic auto-prune of ended sessions so the count stays bounded — now storage hygiene, not a timeout blocker, since the recv-budget fix is count-independent.
- **HUD Live Sessions panel shows active sessions despite ID-ordered Qdrant scroll** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_list_test.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_session.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/bridge_backed_test.go`, services/loom-core!575): the HUD's Live Sessions card rendered "No active sessions yet" while ~12 sessions were actively running across the workspace. Direct `agent_session_list status=active` returned 12 actives with fresh heartbeats; HUD `/api/fleet` returned 1000 sessions of which **0 were active** (750 ended + 250 summarized). Root cause: `SessionSvc.List` called Qdrant's `/points/scroll` with the caller-supplied `limit` and no `order_by`, then sorted the truncated result in memory. Qdrant scroll returns points in internal point-ID order (SHA-256 of session ID via `toPointID`), so a thousand-row session collection returns an ID-ordered window dominated by old ended/summarized rows — the most-recently-started actives sit past the truncation boundary and the post-scroll `sort.Slice(StartedAt DESC)` has nothing to recover. **Fix A (root cause)**: new `sessionListScrollCap = 10000` constant — scroll up to the cap and only truncate to the caller's limit after the StartedAt-DESC sort. The cap is bounded by the 72h prune horizon and matches `workflow_persist.go`'s "fetch everything then filter" pattern. **Fix B (defense-in-depth)**: new `AgentBridge.ActiveSessions()` calls `agent_session_list status=active` explicitly (the filtered scroll path was always correct), and `FleetMonitor.Refresh` unions its result into `snap.Sessions` by ID. So live counters and the stale-session reaper stay accurate even if the unfiltered list path ever regresses again. Existing consumers of `snap.Sessions` (mobile session history, dashboard cards, agent rows) keep their ended/summarized rows intact — this is a strict union. New regression tests: `TestSessionList_LimitDoesNotDropMostRecentSessions` (seeds 30 ended + 5 active, asserts all 5 actives survive `limit=10` via a Qdrant stub that mirrors production's insertion-order scroll), `TestSessionList_FilterByStatusActiveStillWorks` (guards the filtered path), `TestFleetMonitor_MergesActiveSessionsWhenUnfilteredListMissesThem` (mock daemon returns only ended for unfiltered call + one active for filtered call; asserts snapshot unions both and `ActiveSessions=1`).
- **`proxySessionHeartbeat` clears `proxySessionID` on transport error so the keepalive ticker stops re-firing into a dead unix-socket FD after a loomd restart** (`cmd/loom/proxy_session.go`, `cmd/loom/proxy_session_test.go`, services/loom-core!552): the cosmetic stderr-spam tail flagged out-of-scope in !549. After loomd restarted, the proxy held a dead daemon FD; `proxyDaemonRoundTrip` returned a `proxyTransportError` on every keepalive tick (interval driven by `nextSessionKeepaliveInterval`), and the proxy logged `loom proxy: session heartbeat failed: …` once per tick. Tool calls themselves were always fine because `handleProxyToolsCall`'s transport-error path already calls `resetTransport()` + `ensureDaemon()`, which re-opens the session — but the heartbeat goroutine has no closure handle to that machinery and kept hammering the dead FD. Mirror the existing `resp.Error != nil` rejection branch: on transport error, clear `proxySessionID` and return. The keepalive goroutine's existing guard at `cmd/loom/proxy.go:336` (`proxySessionID == ""` ⇒ skip) then short-circuits subsequent ticks, and the next inbound tool call drives the reconnect via `ensureDaemon()` → `proxyOpenSession`, which sets a fresh `proxySessionID`. Net effect: one log line per session generation instead of one per tick, no behavior change for healthy daemons, and no new bookkeeping. New regression test `TestProxySessionHeartbeat_TransportError` captures stderr across two back-to-back heartbeats against a `sessionStubTransport` with `sendErr` set, asserts exactly one `heartbeat failed` line and that `proxySessionID` is empty afterwards.
- **`perConnTransport` mints unique internal JSON-RPC ids per Send so concurrent pool conns sharing a muxstdio transport can't collide on `n:1`** (`internal/daemon/muxcache.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_errors.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_mux_test.go`, services/loom-core!549): closes the local-side counterpart of !534 (which fixed it for the embedded HUD's `bridge.LocalCaller`) and !TBD (which fixed it for the hub WebSocket pool). A transient hub blip on 2026-05-25 (~22:08 PT) forced agent_context calls to fall back from hub to local; multiple concurrent embedded-hud callers — agent_presence_heartbeat, agent_session_list, agent_workflow_list, agent_memory_stats, agent_context_search — fanned out across distinct pool conns over the shared `mcp-agent-context` subprocess. Each conn's `perConnTransport.Send` forwarded the caller's request id straight to the shared `muxstdio.Transport`, which requires unique in-flight ids per instance. Inevitable collision on `n:1` from fresh handshake retries surfaced as `tools/call failed during send: muxstdio: duplicate in-flight id: n:1`, then the daemon's `transportFailure` interpreted that as subprocess death and called `stopServerProc` + `runningServers.Delete`. The respawned subprocess immediately raced with still-queued retries → another duplicate-id → another kill. The ~30s hub blip turned into a ~7 minute restart cascade (live evidence in `~/.config/loom/logs/daemon.err` lines 24960–26527). **Fix #1** (`muxcache.go`): `perConnTransport.Send` now allocates a process-unique id from a daemon-wide `atomic.Int64` (`perConnNextInternalID`), shallow-copies the message, rewrites the outbound id, remembers the caller's original. `Recv` waits on the internal id and restores the caller's original on the response so the upstream `req.ID == resp.ID` check at `callpipeline_stages.go:433` keeps passing. **Fix #2** (`callpipeline_errors.go`): extends the existing recv-timeout keep-alive treatment (see comment block at lines 28–42) to `muxstdio.ErrDuplicateID` as defense-in-depth in case any future caller bypasses the rewriter — subprocess stays alive, pool discards the unhealthy conn on next `Get()`. New OTel span event `daemon.server.muxer_duplicate_id_no_restart` distinguishes the path. New tests: `TestPerConnTransport_ConcurrentCallersWithCollidingCallerIDs` spins 16 conns all sending caller id=1 and asserts every response routes back correctly with id restored; `TestCallPipeline_MuxstdioDuplicateID_DoesNotRestartSubprocess` feeds `ErrDuplicateID` through the pipeline and asserts `runningServers` retains the entry. Both fail on main with the exact symptom logged by the live daemon. Out of scope: why the hub transport closed in the first place (hub-side k8s/cloudflare investigation, separate); the loom MCP proxy heartbeat that spams `broken pipe` on stale unix FDs after a loomd restart (`cmd/loom/proxy_session.go:78-80`, cosmetic only, separate change).
- **Hub WebSocket pool conns share a per-id demuxer so concurrent calls don't drain each other's responses** (`internal/daemon/daemon.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon_new.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon_lifecycle.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): closes the third visible failure mode in the cluster `/api/agent/heartbeat → 502` chain, which only surfaced once the LocalCaller unique-id fix (!534), the carry-over snapshot fix, and the no-kill-on-timeout fix (!540) cleared the upstream noise. `mcp.WebSocketClient.GetConnection(serverName)` returns the **same** cached `*WebSocketTransport` for every Dial of a given server, so multiple `pool.Conn` entries for one hub-routed server all read off the same WebSocket. Concurrent Send/Recv on those Conns race for incoming messages and the pipeline's post-execute guard (`callpipeline_stages.go:433`) catches it as `response ID mismatch: sent X, got Y (possible transport corruption)`. Live evidence post-`--hub-prefer` flip on the laptop daemon: 12,930 of these errors in 60 seconds. New `Daemon.hubMuxCache *muxCache` (parallel to the existing local-side `muxCache`) wraps every hub-side transport in one `muxstdio.Transport` per server; the hub pool's `DialFunc` now returns a `perConnTransport` shim per pool.Conn so id-keyed Recv routes each response to the right caller. **Always-on** for hub (unlike `muxStdio`'s `LOOM_MUX_STDIO` opt-in for local) because the sharing is structural to the WebSocketClient, not an opt-in. Cleanup wired into `daemon_lifecycle.go` shutdown via `hubMuxCache.CloseAll()`. Build + tests + lint green; existing `muxstdio` and `perConnTransport` test coverage exercises the demux behavior at the wrapper layer.
- **Mills spawn end-to-end: implement stage now produces real commits, push, and merges instead of empty MRs** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/spawn_test.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/k8s_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/k8s_test.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn_test.go`, services/loom-core!525 + !527 + !535): three coupled fixes for the long-standing "Mills spawns reach status=completed with turn_count≥1 but files_changed=0 and origin/<branch> at base HEAD" pathology. Each fix was necessary but not sufficient on its own — the next gap only became legible after the previous one no longer blocked the agent. (1) `internal/devbox/backend/k8s_objects.go` git-clone init container now exports `SPAWN_BRANCH` / `SPAWN_BASE_BRANCH` and checks them out after the full-history clone (was: stayed on default branch after `--depth 1`), and Mills' `attachGitContext` fetches origin and diffs `base...origin/<branch>` instead of `base...HEAD`. (2) Same init container now sets `umask 002` and runs `chown -R 1000:1000 <dest>` after checkout so the runtime container's `agent` uid can actually modify the workspace fsGroup chowns the mount point but does not propagate to files an init container writes inside. (3) Codex CLI invocation switches from `--sandbox workspace-write` (no network → silent `git push` failure) and the intermediate `--sandbox danger-full-access` (still pauses for shell-command approval in headless pods because injected config `approval = "auto-edit"` auto-approves edits but not shell ops) to `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` — codex's documented "externally-sandboxed environment" flag and the equivalent of the `--dangerously-skip-permissions` flag claude-code has been using on Mills since day one. Tests pin the umask + chown lines, the branch checkout block, the origin/<branch> diff ref, and a regression check that the codex command must NOT contain `--sandbox workspace-write` or `--sandbox danger-full-access`. Full chronology in `.loom/44-mills-autonomy-retro-2026-05-25.md` (Post-merge verification log section).
- **Daemon no longer kills upstream MCP subprocess on a per-call recv timeout** (`internal/daemon/callpipeline_errors.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): closes the second half of the cluster `/api/agent/heartbeat → 502` cascade. After the LocalCaller unique-ids fix landed (!534), a NEW failure mode became visible in the cluster mobile-hud logs: a single >3s call to agent_context would emit `tools/call timeout during recv after 3s` and `transportFailure` would tear down the entire subprocess via `stopServerProc + runningServers.Delete + pool.ClearServer`. Every other concurrent caller on the shared `muxstdio.Transport` then drained with `transport closed`, which the heartbeat handler turned into 5xx → Cloudflare 502. The subprocess was alive the whole time — just slow on one query. `transportFailure` now short-circuits the nuke path when `p.target == TargetLocal && stage != "send" && isRPCTimeout(err)`: marks the conn unhealthy (the pool discards it on next Get) but leaves the subprocess and the shared muxstdio alone, so other in-flight callers receive their responses normally. Genuine transport faults (EOF, broken pipe, `muxstdio: transport closed`) still respawn the subprocess. New OTel span event `daemon.server.recv_timeout_no_restart` distinguishes the two paths. New tests `TestTransportFailure_LocalRecvTimeoutKeepsSubprocessAlive`, `TestTransportFailure_LocalRecvTransportErrorStillKillsSubprocess`, and `TestTransportFailure_LocalSendTimeoutStillKillsSubprocess` pin the discriminator (timeout vs transport-fault, recv vs send).
- **HUD fleet refresh carries over Sessions/Agents from the previous snapshot when either `agent_session_list` or `agent_presence_list` errors** (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/bridge_backed_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the existing whole-snapshot-empty guard only fired when every sub-fetch returned zero, so a partial failure (typically a transient MCP lock-timeout on `Sessions()` or `PresenceList()`) committed a snapshot where `/api/fleet` showed `active_agents: 0, sessions: []` while tasks/memory/graph/etc. still reflected fresh data. Observed on `http://localhost:3333` during the 2026-05-25 presence-federation work even though `agent_session_list` and `agent_presence_list` were returning 6+ active rows. The refresh path now tracks `sessionsOK`/`presenceOK` and, when either is false, skips the stale-session filter + presence/session join (both produce false negatives or false-positive `agent_session_end` reaps when one side of the coupled view is missing) and restores `Sessions`, `Agents`, and their derived counts (`ActiveSessions`/`TotalSessions`/`TotalTokens`/`StaleSessions`/`ActiveAgents`/`IdleAgents`/`OfflineAgents`/`OrphanAgents`) from the prior snapshot. The healthy case is unchanged. Independent of !534 — which addresses the unique-id race that causes most of these MCP timeouts — this is defense-in-depth so any future MCP transport failure stops blanking the visible HUD. New tests `TestFleetMonitor_CarryOverOnSessionFetchError` and `TestFleetMonitor_CarryOverOnPresenceFetchError` pin both failure modes against the mock daemon.
- **`bridge.LocalCaller` mints unique JSON-RPC ids per call so concurrent embedded-HUD monitors don't collide on the daemon's shared muxstdio transport** (`internal/hud/bridge/local_caller.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/local_caller_test.go`, services/loom-core!534): every `LocalCaller.Call()` previously stamped `id=int64(1)`. When the embedded HUD's monitors (fleet, shuttle, workflow, memory, …) fanned out concurrently to the same upstream MCP server, the daemon's shared `muxstdio.Transport` rejected the second send with `duplicate in-flight id: n:1`, closed the transport, and drained every queued caller as 5xx. The `/api/agent/heartbeat` handler is on the same path → handler returns 5xx → Cloudflare → **502 at the edge** (reproduced live 2026-05-25 against the cluster's mobile-hud). Add an `atomic.Int64` counter to `LocalCaller` and increment it on each `Call()`, matching the existing `DaemonClient.reqID.Add(1)` pattern (`internal/hud/bridge/daemon.go:350`). New regression test `TestLocalCaller_UniqueRequestIDs` spins 32×8 concurrent `Call()`s and asserts every id is distinct. Daemon's response handler already preserves the inbound id end-to-end so callers see no behavioral change. Follow-up: `callpipeline_stages.go:336` still forwards `p.msg.ID` from inbound to upstream — socket-mode clients hardcoding id=1 in their JSON-RPC would resurface the same race, and a daemon-side rewrite (mint a unique outbound id, translate back on response) is the durable fix.
- **LLM-judged gates (spec_conformance, pr_self_review) are skipped for mills-canary labeled items so deterministic fixture canaries can reach merge instead of being model-quality bounded (M8)** (`pkg/mills/gates/llm_judge.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/llm_judge_test.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner_test.go`): the Mills canary is a one-line edit to `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md` whose only purpose is to prove end-to-end pipeline plumbing works. The diff carries no spec to conform to and nothing meaningful for self-review to grade, and gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq hallucinates fail verdicts on the trivial content — live evidence from `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-M6-164007-1779036007` (2026-05-17) returned `score=0.00 below threshold=0.70 | Example: file.py:10 - debug print found`, fabricating a Python file that exists nowhere in the diff. After M2.5 + M6 the canary retried 3× and escalated honestly instead of crashing, but it could never reach merge because the verdict is model-quality bounded. `LLMGate.Evaluate` now short-circuits when `StageInput.Item.Labels` contains `gates.CanaryLabel` (`"mills-canary"`), returning `Outcome{Pass: true, JudgedBy: "skipped:canary"}` with `err=nil` before any FlexInfer call. Pure-Go gates (`path_policy`, `secret_scan`, `diff_size`, `scope`, `commit_format`) still run for canaries — they're cheap and they catch real things (the canary's `safe-fixture` label is what lets `path_policy` permit the markdown edit). Audit-trail token `"skipped:canary"` is searchable: operators reviewing a canary `gate_outcomes` row grep for it to see WHY no model was consulted. Same precedent as M1b (24h dedupe) and the tests-stage scope tightening (`fmt`-only on canaries). New tests: `TestLLMGate_CanaryLabelShortCircuitsJudge`, `TestLLMGate_CanaryLabelShortCircuitsPRSelfReviewToo`, `TestLLMGate_NonCanaryLabelsDoNotShortCircuit`, `TestLLMGate_NilItemDoesNotShortCircuit`, `TestLLMGate_EmptyLabelsDoNotShortCircuit`, `TestItemHasCanaryLabel_PureFunction`, `TestRunner_CanaryItemSkipsLLMGatesAndReachesMerge` (end-to-end: backlog item labeled `mills-canary` reaches `pipeline_runs.state=done` with `gate_outcomes.judged_by="skipped:canary"` rows for both LLM gates and zero `recordingJudge.calls`).
- **Rubric prompts for spec_conformance and pr_self_review gain anti-hallucination instructions, structural-output reinforcement, and explicit empty-diff handling so the gemma judge returns parseable verdicts grounded in the actual diff** (`pkg/mills/gates/spec_conformance.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/pr_self_review_gate.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/rubric_boilerplate.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): after M6 populated `DiffPatch` in the rubric prompt and M2.5 added retry-on-unparseable, the live `pr_self_review` LLM gate produced verdicts but gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq kept hallucinating on small / fixture-only diffs. Live evidence from canary `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-M6-164007-1779036007` (2026-05-17): the actual diff was a one-line markdown heartbeat bump but the judge returned `score=0.00 below threshold=0.70 | Example: file.py:10 - debug print found` — fabricating a Python file that doesn't exist in the diff. The parallel `spec_conformance` rubric kept returning `judged_by=flexinfer:unparseable` because the prompt template let the model reply with prose instead of the score envelope. Shared `rubricGroundingInstructions` (anchor every concern to one specific line of the diff; on empty / fixture-only diffs return score 1.0) plus `rubricStructuralOutputInstructions` (JSON-only response, no clarifying questions, explicit empty-diff fallback verdict) are appended to both rubric bodies. `composePrompt` always emits the `=== Diff ===` section now — empty diffs render as `(empty diff)` so the model sees an explicit empty anchor rather than missing context. Stays prompt-engineering only: gemma4-26b is the only Ready model in the cluster's flexinfer per the 2026-05-16 policy patch. New template snapshot tests (`TestRubricTemplate_PRSelfReviewIncludesGroundingInstructions`, `TestRubricTemplate_SpecConformanceIncludesStructuralInstructions`, `TestRubricTemplate_EmptyDiffRendersExplicitPlaceholder`, `TestRubricTemplate_NonEmptyDiffSkipsPlaceholder`) pin the load-bearing phrases so future drift is intentional. Actual gemma verdict-quality on a fixture corpus is a follow-up eval slice.
- **HUD Fleet view downgrades unified rows for terminal spawns instead of hardcoding `active`** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/agents.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/agents.terminalSpawn.fixture.ts`, embedded `frontend/dist/`): the previous Fleet reaper landed at the backend layer (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go` 90s heartbeat filter, MR !431), but the frontend's `buildUnifiedAgents` had its own bug — every spawn row was added with `status: "active"` unconditionally (`agents.ts` line 317), so `spawn-claude-code-*` rows for closed CI pipelines (e.g. `spawn-claude-code-10fa8a6eb214` for pipeline 9839 failed on `fix/mills-persi…`) kept showing as live agents on hud.flexinfer.ai. Live screenshot 2026-05-17 16:41 EDT: 33 live agents in the panel while `/api/fleet` correctly returned 11 (5 active, 6 offline) post-backend-filter. Spawn rows now flip to `offline` when `spawn.status` is in `{completed, failed, escalated, stopped, paused, cancelled, canceled}`, both on standalone spawn-only rows and when a stale CLI keepalive presence row would otherwise keep the unified agent `active`. New `isTerminalSpawnStatus` helper + `agents.terminalSpawn.fixture.ts` (runnable via `pnpm dlx tsx`) pins the four cases: spawn-only completed → offline, spawn-only running → active, presence+terminal-spawn → offline downgrade, presence+running-spawn → active.
- **HUD Fleet view reaps stale presence rows and matches counts to visible work** (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet_test.go`): the live cluster's Fleet panel reported "35 live agents · 4 active sessions · 2 orphans" while only ~5 of the rows were actual working agents — keepalive heartbeats from completed sessions, orphan-tagged rows still counting as live, and spawn-derived agents from terminated pods all stuck at `Status=active` until the 10min MCP-side reaper or the orphan reaper got around to them. Root cause: `agent_presence_list` keeps a row in `status="active"` until ~10 minutes after its last heartbeat, and the Fleet snapshot copied that status verbatim into `snap.Agents` — both `/api/agents` and the frontend's `liveAgents` filter (`status === 'active' || status === 'idle'`) then counted every still-heartbeating row regardless of whether it had a current session. New `fleetLivePresenceStaleAfter = 90s` threshold: in the same pass that counts `ActiveAgents` / `IdleAgents` / `OfflineAgents` after `fleetview.Join`, any presence-backed row whose heartbeat is older than 90s OR is flagged `IsOrphan` is downgraded to `Status=offline` so it stops showing up in the "live agents" list while keeping its `ORPHAN` evidence pill and underlying presence data intact. Synthetic session-only rows (`HasPresence=false`) are exempt because they have no heartbeat clock. New `TestFleetMonitor_LivePresenceFilterDowngradesStaleAndOrphanRows` pins the boundary (89s → stays active, 91s → flipped to offline) and asserts the post-filter counter matches the visible-row count.
- **Mills reconciler re-drives non-terminal pipeline_runs on every tick so a transient HUD error or operator rollout no longer strands a run forever (M7)** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler_test.go`): live wedge on 2026-05-17 (`PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-VERIFY2-045207-1778993527`) sat in `state=planning, current_stage=plan_slice` for ~9 hours because a transient `getSpawnState` error during the startup-Resume poll caused `runner.Drive` to write a pending `stage_results` row and exit with `errStagePending`. The runner goroutine returned, and nothing scanned non-terminal `pipeline_runs` after that — `Reconciler.ResumeInFlightRuns` only fires once at operator startup. `Reconciler.Tick` now calls the new `pickupInFlightRuns` (using `Store.Pipeline.ListInFlight`) after the existing `pickupQueuedSubruns`, handing each non-terminal run to `Starter.Start`. Idempotency is enforced downstream by `Runner.Start`'s `r.active.LoadOrStore` (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go:259-262`): a live goroutine returns nil with a warn log; a missing goroutine gets re-spawned and reattaches to the pending spawn via `SpawnResumeClient.Resume`. Stays inside Tick's existing policy + autonomy gates so a paused operator stays paused. New tests: `TestReconciler_TickReDrivesInFlightRuns` (the smoking-gun scenario with a pending `stage_results.spawn_id` row), `TestReconciler_TickReDriveIsIdempotent` (fake starter that mirrors `LoadOrStore` semantics — repeated ticks do not duplicate dispatches), `TestReconciler_TickReDriveSkipsWhenAutonomyBlocked`, `TestReconciler_TickReDriveSkipsTerminalRuns`. Full diagnosis with file:line evidence at `.loom/120-diagnosis-mills-runner-no-resume-after-rollout-2026-05-17.md`.
- **LLM-judged gates retry on unparseable judge output instead of escalating immediately (M2.5)** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer_test.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/llm_judge.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/llm_judge_test.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner_test.go`): the `spec_conformance` / `pr_self_review` gates previously bubbled a parse error from `parseRubricEnvelope` ("no parseable score envelope in response") up through `gates.Registry.EvaluateAll` and `pipeline.Runner.Drive`, hitting the no-retry escalation branch at `runner.go:276`. Live regression on 2026-05-16 canary `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-M1D-VERIFY-2`: gemma4-26b returned free-text ("please provide the diff…") and the entire pipeline escalated on the first `spec_conformance` call even though every prior stage succeeded. New `ErrRubricUnparseable` sentinel (plus a `*rubricParseError` wrapper exposing the `IsRubricUnparseable() bool` duck-typed predicate) lets `LLMGate.Evaluate` translate a parse miss into `Outcome{Pass:false, JudgedBy:"flexinfer:unparseable"}` with `err=nil`, so the runner's existing gate-retry path (rewind to `RetryFrom`, bump attempt counter, re-run `pr_self_review`) fires naturally. Transport failures (network, timeout, 5xx) still surface as errors and still escalate — only the "judge ran but answer wasn't graded" path was reclassified. New tests: `TestLLMGate_UnparseableJudgeReturnsSoftFailNotError`, `TestLLMGate_DuckTypedUnparseableErrorAlsoSoftFails`, `TestLLMGate_TransportErrorStillEscalates`, `TestParseRubricEnvelope_WrapsSentinelOnNoJSON`, `TestParseRubricEnvelope_WrapsSentinelOnOutOfRangeScore`, `TestRubricJudge_UnparseableContentExposesPredicateAcrossWrap`, `TestRunner_LLMGateUnparseableOutcomeTriggersRetry`.
- **HUD stale-session reaper ignored spawn pods that registered presence without heartbeating** (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet_test.go`): `fleetStaleSessionReapAfter` (10min) failed to fire on spawn agents whose presence rows carried `LastHeartbeat=""` — visible in the HUD as `spawn-claude-code-*` rows stuck in `STATUS=active` with `HEARTBEAT=---` long after the spawn pod died. Root cause: `fleetview.AgeSeconds` clamps empty / unparseable timestamps to `0`, and the reaper's lookup table seeded `heartbeatBySession[s.ID] = 0` for these rows. Age 0 < 600s threshold ⇒ session never reaped ⇒ immortal "active" session. Fix: skip presence rows with empty `LastHeartbeat` when seeding the heartbeat maps so the reaper falls through to its `session.StartedAt` clock, which is the documented semantic for "presence exists but never reported liveness." Regression test `TestFleetMonitor_StaleSessionReaper_EmptyHeartbeatFallsBackToSessionAge` pins the scenario: active session + presence row with `last_heartbeat=""` + `started_at=20min ago` must end up reaped.
- **HUD traces filter resolves to root agent for subagent rows** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/TracesPanel.svelte`): Fleet table's "Traces" button passed the row's own `agent_id`. For subagent rows (Claude Code Task-tool children like `claude-code-NNN-MMM`), the audit stream had nothing under that id because subagents share their parent's MCP session — their tool calls are recorded under the *root* agent's id. So `entry.agent_id === agentFilter` never matched. Now resolves to `row.rootSession.agent_id` when the row is a child. TracesPanel's empty state distinguishes "no traces matched a filtered agent" (with a hint about subagent sharing) from "no traces in the stream at all."
- **HUD Live Agents: stale ORPHAN badge alongside SESSION evidence + cramped "no active session match" divider** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/agents.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`): agents with a locally-matched live session were rendering the `ORPHAN` evidence pill even though the SESSION pill was lit, when raw presence/session data arrived at the client out of order (a snapshot could carry `agent.is_orphan=true` alongside a now-matching session because raw presence was sampled before the session existed). The client's `buildUnifiedAgents` trusted the server's `is_orphan` flag unconditionally while computing `has_session` locally — the two could disagree. Now the client mirrors the server's `markOrphan` invariant ("orphan ⇒ no active session"): when the client determines `has_session=true`, `is_orphan` is forced to false regardless of the snapshot flag. The same clear is applied in the session-matching second pass. The "No active session match" divider label was also absolutely positioned with `top: -6px`, so it visually overlapped the row above; relayed as an inline-block pill below the dashed border instead. `pnpm build` clean; `go test ./internal/hud/fleetview/...` green (existing `TestOrphan_StalePresenceWithoutSessionIsOrphan` covers the server-side invariant).

### Added
- **HUD SSE migration across decomposed stores (Slice B3)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{events,fleet,tasks,sandbox,health,spawn}.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/staleness.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/ConnectionBanner.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`, plus 9 panel callsites): moves the 5 decomposed HUD stores (fleet, tasks, sandbox, health/servers, spawn) onto an SSE-first data flow with 60s watchdog polling and explicit staleness tracking. New `events.subscribe<T>(type, handler)` typed helper plus a `SUPPORTED_HUD_EVENTS` registry documenting all 24+ `hud.*` events the daemon emits with their emit cadences. New `stores/staleness.svelte.ts` exposes a `clockStore` that ticks every 5s and a `stalenessStore` aggregator that any store can register an `isStale` getter against — read by `ConnectionBanner` to surface a "stale data" pill whenever a registered store hasn't received a snapshot within `staleAfter` (90s for fleet/tasks/sandbox/health, 180s for spawn given idle daemons emit no events). Catches silent SSE failures the existing connection-state banner cannot see (connection up, no events flowing). Polling intervals bumped from 5–30s to 60s across the affected panels (SpawnPanel, ServersPanel, TasksPanel, SandboxPanel, FleetPanel, OverviewPanel, PresencePanel, LifecyclePanel, OverlayShell, SpawnDetailPanel); spawn store's previously-unconditional poll is now gated on `!eventStore.connected` like the others. Expected before/after: a busy operator dashboard polled the 5 stores ~12 req/min combined; with SSE up that drops to 0 req/min from these stores (SSE snapshots and granular events drive all updates), with watchdog polls only firing during SSE outages. `pnpm build` clean; `go test ./internal/hud/...` green.
- **HUD GraphPanel decomp — shell + GraphFullView split (Slice B2.5)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/GraphPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/graph/GraphFullView.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/graphHelpers.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): applies a minimal-viable variant of the B1 panel decomposition pattern to the Activity → Graph view. **GraphPanel.svelte: 1030 → 21 lines** (target was <300). Pure helpers in `lib/utils/graphHelpers.ts`: `typeVariant`, `typeBarColor`. Note: GraphFullView (1017 lines) is intentionally not further sub-decomposed in this slice — further granular extraction (GraphStats / GraphExplorer / GraphViz / GraphModals) is a follow-up. Behavior preserved: polling, all CRUD modals with their form state, expandable entity cards with properties + inbound/outbound relations, EntityGraph mini-viz, drill drawer with relations, path finder, delete confirm. `pnpm build` clean with no new warnings on touched files.
- **HUD SandboxPanel decomp — offline vs live split (Slice B2.4)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/SandboxPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/sandbox/{SandboxOffline,SandboxLive}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/sandboxHelpers.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): applies a minimal-viable variant of the B1 panel decomposition pattern to the Sandbox (Labs) view. **SandboxPanel.svelte: 1479 → 41 lines** (target was <300). Note: SandboxLive (639 lines) is intentionally not further sub-decomposed in this slice — the offline/live split is the high-value structural change, and per-card extraction (SandboxHeader / SandboxStartToolbar / SandboxExecToolbar / SandboxProjects / SandboxActivity / SandboxRail) is left as a follow-up. - **HUD FleetPanel decomp + panel decomposition pattern (Slice B1)** (`docs/HUD_PANEL_DECOMP.md`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/FleetPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/{FleetTable,FleetStatsGrid,ActivityCard,MemoryTiersCard,SessionDetail}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/fleetRows.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/fleet.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): kicks off Slice B (Foundations) by establishing the panel-decomposition pattern that B2 will reuse for SpawnPanel/ServersPanel/TasksPanel/SandboxPanel/GraphPanel. (1) New `docs/HUD_PANEL_DECOMP.md` documents the layout (`components/<panel>/`, `stores/<panel>.svelte.ts` owns interactive state, `utils/<panel>Rows.ts` for pure cross-store joins), the store contract (`filter`, `search`, `sortKey`, `sortDir`, `selected` as `$state`; `filtered`/`visible` as `$derived`), and the recommended order of decomp (state → row builders → cards → drawer). (2) FleetPanel canary: 1777 → 173 lines (target was <300). The monolith decomposes into a composition shell that wires polling + the cross-store row joins + drawer drill, plus 5 extracted components (`FleetTable`, `FleetStatsGrid`, `ActivityCard`, `MemoryTiersCard`, `SessionDetail`) in `lib/components/fleet/` and one pure utility module (`lib/utils/fleetRows.ts`) holding the typed `FleetRow` contract, `buildFleetRows` (sort + root-session-grouping + ungrouped-tail logic), `compareFleetAgents`, `buildSpawnByAgentId`, and `buildExpiringClaims`. (3) Per the new contract, sort + grouping state moved into `fleetStore` as `sortKey`/`sortDir`/`groupByRootSession` `$state` plus `setSort()` and `toggleGrouping()` setters; the prior local panel state is gone. (4) `SessionDetail` owns the entire drawer lifecycle including the load-on-sessionId-change effect and the `clearDrawerError` cleanup, removing one of the panel's largest non-shell concerns. Behavior preserved: routes (`#agents/fleet`, `#agents/fleet/<sessionId>`), bookmarks, polling cadences (`fleet 5s` / `traces 15s` / `tasks 5s` / `workflows 10s` / `memory 10s` / `graph 15s` / `stream 3s` / `spawn 15s`), drill targets (session, traces, spawn), DataTable sort persistence, root-session grouping, expiring-claims badges, orphan/idle counts. `pnpm build` clean with no new warnings on touched files; `go test ./internal/hud/...` green.
- **HUD triage overview rebuild — operator inbox (Slice A2)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/overview/{InstrumentStrip,HeroSummary,InboxCard,InboxDeck,SupportingStrip}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/inbox.ts`, services/loom-core!401): rebuilds the 1504-line `OverviewPanel` monolith into a composed shell (433 lines, under the spec's 500-line target) that drives a 7-kind triage inbox on top of the A1 action toolkit. New `lib/utils/inbox.ts` exposes a typed `CardSpec` contract plus pure selectors that derive cards from existing stores — `file_conflict` (coordinationStore), `server_down` (healthStore), `blocked_task` (taskStore + coordinationStore), `pending_approval` (workflowStore), `orphan_session` (fleetStore unifiedSummary), `stale_session` (liveSessionsStore visibleSessions older than 5min), `rbac_denied_spike` (rbacStore). `InboxCard` renders severity-stripe + ConfirmDialog gating + `useAction` audit/toast wiring; the single-pending-approval card fires `workflowStore.approveStep` end-to-end. Drill-only cards navigate to the right panel; per-card mutation endpoints (orphan reap, stale recover) wait on a follow-up slice that adds the desktop `/api/agent/session-end` plumb-through. `InstrumentStrip` (signal rings + today chip), `HeroSummary` (command headline + attention lanes), and `SupportingStrip` (collapsible Memory/Stream/Sandbox/Graph chips) extract the prior monolith's three visual zones verbatim so routes, bookmarks, and KPI fetch behavior stay byte-compatible. Empty inbox renders a "System nominal" card. Pre-push hook ran the full `go test ./...` green; `pnpm build` clean with no new warnings on `overview/*`.
- **HUD action toolkit primitives + UX overhaul planning trio (Slice A1)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/action.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/useAction.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/action/{ErrorCard,AuditDrawer}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/{App.svelte,lib/components/CommandPalette.svelte,lib/components/shared/index.ts}`, `.loom/{115,116,117}-*-2026-05-15.md`, services/loom-core!399): foundational action substrate for the HUD UX overhaul. `useAction` wraps async mutations in a uniform `{ run, retry, pending, error, lastResult }` handle with optimistic + rollback support, automatic toast on success/failure, and audit recording. `actionStore` keeps a session-local ring buffer of the last 50 actions, mirrored to `sessionStorage`. `ErrorCard` standardizes inline retry/dismiss surfaces; `AuditDrawer` slides out from the right with status badges, source labels, and inline error detail. Wired into `App.svelte` next to the existing Toast layer; new `Cmd+K` palette entry "Open Recent Actions" opens the drawer. Existing `shared/ConfirmDialog.svelte` and `widgets/Toast.svelte` are reused — `useAction` delegates to the existing toast store instead of introducing a parallel ActionToast. Planning trio in `.loom/115/116/117` documents the two-slice plan: Slice A (Operator Inbox, ~1w) followed by Slice B (Foundations: decompose 5 monolith panels <300 lines, SSE migration, keyboard density, mobile/embed reflow, ~2w). F6 IA reorg explicitly deferred.
- **`loom sync mirror` keeps `platform/gitops/mcp/context` in lockstep with canonical `services/loom-core`** (`pkg/sync/mirror.go`, `pkg/sync/mirror_test.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_sync.go`, `scripts/ci/check_mirror_drift.sh`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, services/loom-core!383): `loom sync mirror --check` / `--apply` / `--dry-run` compare/apply the canonical `mcp/context/{registry,skills-registry}.yaml` to the GitOps mirror. `loom sync status` gains a `gitops-mirror` row. A pre-commit hook (gated to `main`/`master`) refuses to land a registry change without a matching mirror update, so the canonical-vs-mirror drift that produced 2,500-line skews stops at commit time instead of in production.
- **Codex `[hooks]` block wired through the generator** (`pkg/generator/configs_codex.go`, `pkg/generator/templates/hooks/codex.toml.tmpl`, `pkg/generator/configs_formats.go`, `pkg/sync/manager.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, services/loom-core!383): Codex v0.129.0 (2026-05-07) shipped a Claude-shape `[hooks]` block. Loom now emits `~/.codex/hooks.json` alongside `config.toml`, with `[features] hooks = true` so Codex loads it, and maps `SessionStart` + `PostToolUse` to `loom agent session-start --auto-recall` and `loom agent heartbeat`. The legacy `notify = [...]` keepalive stays as a fallback since the deprecation attempt in PR #20524 was reverted in #21152.

### Fixed
- **HUD spawn list no longer accumulates terminal records forever** (`internal/spawn/{controller,reconciler,controller_test}.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): operator complaint that the HUD spawn list still showed "old orphan spawns" from days ago, even though the underlying pods were obviously gone. Investigation: the `TerminalHook` reaper from `5ecdda2f` is deployed and firing — every entry in the live `mobile-hud-6d485b78c4-8wvqx /api/mobile/v1/agent/spawns` response had `cleanup_at` set, meaning the pod was killed and the agent-context session was ended on time. But the spawn `State` record was never evicted from either the in-memory `K8sController.spawns` map or the on-disk `FileStore`. `FileStore.PruneCompleted(maxAge)` was implemented (`internal/spawn/store.go:122`) and tested (`internal/hud/spawn_persist_test.go:TestSpawnStorePruneCompleted`) but had **no production caller** — so on the live HUD the earliest visible entry was 2026-05-12 22:50 UTC, ~3 days old, all terminal, all already reaped at the infrastructure layer. New `K8sController.Prune(ctx, maxAge)` removes terminal spawns whose `EndedAt` (or `CleanupAt` fallback) is older than the cutoff from BOTH the in-memory map AND the persistent store. New `StartPruneLoop(ctx, interval, maxAge)` drives it on a ticker. Wired into `App.initSpawnOrchestrator` next to the existing `StartReconcileLoop` with **24h retention on a 10min cadence** — keeps a useful triage window for recent failures without unbounded growth, and amortises the disk I/O against the existing 30s reconcile tick. Tests: `TestPruneDropsTerminalSpawnsOlderThanMaxAge` covers the matrix (old completed/failed/cleanup-only-fallback evicted; recent-completed, running, terminal-without-timestamps retained; both in-memory and store mirror); `TestPruneZeroMaxAgeIsNoOp` guards against accidental wipe via misconfigured retention. `go test ./internal/spawn/... ./internal/hud/...` green.
- **Spawned agent pods now run as a non-root user so claude actually executes the model** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/k8s_objects.go`, `internal/hud/spawn_test.go`, services/loom-core!390): the agent runtime Dockerfile shipped `FROM golang:1.25.10-alpine` with no `USER` directive, so the spawn pod ran as root. The mills `buildAgentCommand` passes `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, which claude code refuses under uid 0 ("cannot be used with root/sudo privileges for security reasons") — every claude-code spawn exited cleanly in ~5s with `turn_count=0`, `total_cost_usd=0`, zero token usage, and an empty file_changes telemetry block. The mills runner saw success, but `implement` produced no diff, so `pr_self_review` reviewed nothing and the downstream `spec_conformance` rubric judge asked the LLM to score a non-existent change set. Field evidence: `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260514-063626` completed plan_slice → research → implement → tests → pr_self_review in 11 minutes with $0.0001742 cost (the research flexinfer call), then escalated on the gate with raw model response "Please provide the unified diff and the list of files changed/slice scope. I cannot perform the review without the …". Runtime Dockerfile now creates `agent` (uid 1000), chowns `/workspace` + `/home/agent`, sets `HOME=/home/agent`, and switches via `USER agent`. Auth-secret mounts move from `/root/.{claude,codex}.auth/` and `/root/.gcp/` (unreadable by uid 1000 because `/root` is mode 0700) to `/home/agent/.{claude,codex}.auth/` and `/home/agent/.gcp/`, and the apiKeyHelper / codex symlink / gemini settings paths track the move. Pod `securityContext.fsGroup=1000` makes the workspace emptyDir + secret mounts group-writable so the root git-clone init can hand its checkout to the uid-1000 main container without ownership conflicts (legacy root-mode devbox sandboxes are unaffected because uid 0 ignores group-mode constraints). Test expectations in `TestAgentSecretMounts_{Claude,Codex}OAuthFromClusterSecret` retargeted against `AgentHomeDir`.
- **Mills flexinfer HTTP timeout raised from 30s to 5min, with `FLEXINFER_TIMEOUT` env override** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{config,main}.go`, services/loom-core!389): the prior 30s default on `FlexInferConfig.Timeout` clipped every research-stage call against a healthy backend. The mills research worker asks for up to 1024 tokens, and on the only currently-Ready model in `flexinfer-system` (`gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq`) a real research prompt finishes ~886 tokens in ~114s — 4× the prior cap. Canary `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260514-044309` escalated 3× on `context deadline exceeded` even after the model swap landed via platform/gitops!127. Default is now 5min (covers cold start + worst-case generation on the 26B model), and the operator picks up `FLEXINFER_TIMEOUT` (Go duration, e.g. `180s`) so the platform side can tune without rebuilding.
- **Codex hooks now self-trust through `loom sync codex --regen` instead of silently dropping after every regeneration** (`pkg/generator/codex_hooks_trust.go`, `pkg/generator/codex_hooks_trust_test.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_codex.go`, `pkg/generator/templates/hooks/codex.toml.tmpl`, services/loom-core!383): Codex v0.129+ silently skips every hook in `~/.codex/hooks.json` whose `trusted_hash` in `config.toml [hooks.state]` does not equal a canonical SHA256 of the hook content (see `codex-rs/hooks/src/engine/discovery.rs::hook_trust_status`). Trust is normally granted via the `/hooks` TUI command, but every `loom sync codex --regen` rewrites hooks.json (workspace paths, agent-id shell snippets), invalidating the trust hashes and leaving every hook a no-op until manual re-trust. Reproduced via sentinel hooks.json + `codex exec`: the tool ran, the hook file was never touched. The generator now computes the same canonical hash codex would (per `codex-rs/config/src/fingerprint.rs::version_for_toml`: snake_case event name, normalized handler with `commandWindows=None` and `timeout=unwrap_or(600).max(1)`, recursively-sorted-key canonical JSON, SHA256, formatted as `sha256:<hex>`) and writes matching `[hooks.state]` entries inline in the rendered `config.toml`. Two byte-level gotchas worth knowing: (1) the event_name inside the hashed body is snake_case (`post_tool_use`), not the CamelCase JSON key, and (2) codex's `serde_json::to_vec` does NOT HTML-escape `<`, `>`, `&` — Go's default `json.Marshal` does, so the encoder must run with `SetEscapeHTML(false)`. Validation: 4 of 5 freshly-emitted hashes byte-match the user's pre-existing `/hooks` TUI-granted trusted_hash values, and a live `codex exec` after regen fires SessionStart + UserPromptSubmit + PostToolUse without any TUI interaction.
- **Claude+Codex `Stop` no longer fires `loom agent session-end --summarize` on every model turn** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`, `pkg/generator/configs_hooks_test.go`, services/loom-core!383): Codex docs say verbatim "PreToolUse, PermissionRequest, PostToolUse, UserPromptSubmit, and Stop run at turn scope"; Claude docs say `Stop` fires "When Claude finishes responding". Mapping `session_end_event: "Stop"` queued a `--summary-async` summarization job on every single turn for both platforms. Claude profile now uses `session_end_event: "SessionEnd"` (the real per-session terminal event, matchers `clear/resume/logout/prompt_input_exit/bypass_permissions_disabled/other`). Codex profile drops `Stop` from events entirely with `session_end_event: ""` because Codex has no `SessionEnd` event — true session termination is signaled by the `keepalive-wrap` background process exiting with the codex CLI and calling `/api/agent/deregister`. `buildPlatformHooks` now gates the session-end block on `hp.SessionEndEvent != ""` so codex emits no `Stop` entry. `canonicalTelemetryHookForEvent` removes the `Stop`-to-`session-end` mapping; only the real `SessionEnd` event triggers `event-emit --hook session-end`.
- **`loom sync status` stops reporting false drift for `claude` / `gemini` profiles after `--all-projects`** (`pkg/sync/status.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_test.go`, services/loom-core!383): `compareGeneratedFile` had a `settings.json` exemption that ignored only the `hooks` key, but `SyncAllProjects` strips the profile's full `HomeManagedSettingsKeys` list (claude: `hooks`+`permissions`; gemini: 5 keys). Repo-vs-home comparison after a workspace-strip looked like drift even when the files were intentionally divergent. New `settingsInSyncIgnoringHomeManaged` honors the profile's actual stripped-keys list. Previously every regen left status reporting bogus claude/gemini drift; now 9/9 profiles in-sync after a clean `loom sync claude --regen`.
- **Daemon heartbeat-storm cascade in `loom-agent-hooks.log` no longer hits the 60s `tools/call` budget** (`internal/hud/bridge/agent_session.go`, services/loom-core!383): `PresenceHeartbeat` was inheriting the default 60s daemon RPC timeout. When `agent_context` hung (e.g., contended SQLite write), the per-server `callLock` was held for up to 60s, exhausting the 15s lock-acquire deadline for every queued heartbeat and producing the `acquire call lock for "agent_context" after 15s` cascade. Now capped at 5s per call. A hung upstream still degrades the system, but the failure mode is bounded-latency (caller sees a 5s error and the next heartbeat retries) instead of a wedge.
- **Best-effort retry on transient daemon transport drops** (`cmd/loom/cmd_agent_transport.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_transport_test.go`, services/loom-core!383): `withAgentFallback` now retries the daemon path once when the failure matches a recognised transient transport drop (`transport closed`, `use of closed network connection`, `tools/call timeout during recv`). The pool's `discardIfStale` reaps the bad conn on the next `Get`, so the retry typically lands on a healthy connection. Non-transient failures (auth, unknown tool) skip the retry path so the user sees the real error without latency.
- **Vendor specs refreshed against current docs and `rapid-dev-iteration-loop` skill restored** (`pkg/generator/vendor_specs.yaml`, `mcp/context/skills-registry.yaml`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `pkg/generator/VENDOR_SPECS.md`, services/loom-core!383): swapped the Claude Code marketing README in `vendor_specs.yaml` for the authoritative `code.claude.com/docs/en/{settings,hooks}` pages, replaced the bare gemini-cli README with `docs/hooks/reference.md`, added the codex `developers.openai.com/codex/hooks` page, and tightened must_contain + emitted_keys assertions for each vendor. `loom vendor-specs check` is green against the live docs. `rapid-dev-iteration-loop` skill source directory existed under `mcp/skills/` since April but the registry entry was never landed in `services/loom-core` (only in the GitOps mirror); restored so it generates for codex/claude/kilocode/gemini again. The `weaver` MCP server was ported back into canonical `registry.yaml` so the AGENTS.md "canonical is source-of-truth" policy holds.

### Changed
- **HUD theme sweep — alerts-only glow + flat panel wash + unified agent palette (Slice A3)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/{AgentCard,AgentTopology,SwimLaneTimeline}.svelte`, services/loom-core!TBD): closes Slice A of the HUD UX overhaul.
