(1) `--glow-success` and `--glow-info` collapse from saturated success/info hues to a near-invisible `rgba(212, 238, 244, 0.05)` neutral fg tint — glow now signals attention (still colored on `--glow-accent`/`--glow-error`/`--glow-warning`) instead of decorating healthy states. Box-shadow geometry on every consumer (status dots, stage chips, hover surfaces in 14 panels) stays intact, just stops adding chromatic noise. (2) `.panel-area` in `App.svelte` drops the two competing radial washes (`circle at top left` info + `circle at top right` accent) so the body's ambient background reads cleanly without a second-layer color wash fighting it. (3) Agent palette redefined as a 4-slot ramp derived from `--accent` via `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) Nx%, var(--fg-secondary))` — claude=100%, codex=76%, gemini=54%, copilot=32%. The original four hues (`#FF6B9D` / `#22E076` / `#00C8FF` / `#FFB830`) re-used semantic success/info/warning colors and made every agent-tagged surface compete with status chips. The same token names are kept so `.agent-color-*` / `.agent-dot-*` / `tokens.AGENT_COLORS` consumers keep working unchanged. Three SVG widgets (AgentCard, AgentTopology, SwimLaneTimeline) had stale duplicate hex maps inside their `<script>` blocks — they now route through the canonical CSS tokens too. SVG `fill={var(--agent-*)}` works on baseline-2023 browsers (Chrome 111+, Firefox 113+, Safari 16.2+ all ship `color-mix()` as a CSS color value usable in SVG presentation attributes).
- **Mills/spawn canary path now uses quota-aware devbox builds** (`internal/devbox/backend/{k8s,k8s_build}.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed,spawn}.go`, `cmd/{mcp-devbox,loom}/`, `k8s/base/servers/{devbox,mobile-hud}/deployment.yaml`, services/loom-core!356): the first real Mills production canary proved orchestration was alive but failed when HUD spawn image-build pods asked Kubernetes for `limits.cpu=3` while the `devbox` namespace had less than 1 CPU of remaining quota. K8s devbox Buildah pods now have configurable CPU/memory request+limit quantities and a per-backend build semaphore (`DEVBOX_K8S_BUILD_*`, `DEVBOX_K8S_MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS`), and embedded HUD spawn exposes matching `SPAWN_BUILD_*`, `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS`, `SPAWN_DEFAULT_CPU`, and `SPAWN_DEFAULT_MEMORY_MB` controls. The cluster manifests set Mills-facing spawn builds to one-at-a-time 250m/500m Buildah pods and spawned agent runtime pods to 0.5 CPU / 2Gi by default, so canary work can fit under tight `devbox` quota instead of failing before the worker starts.
- **Go toolchain bumped from 1.25.9 → 1.25.10** (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.custom-server`, `Dockerfile.custom-server.local`, `Dockerfile.local`, `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator`, services/loom-core!319): unblocks CI on `main` after every post-merge pipeline since EPIC 3 work began (CONFIG-1/2/3/4 — !315/!316/!317/!318) failed at `security:govulncheck` on 8 stdlib vulnerabilities, all explicitly marked `Fixed in: stdlib@go1.25.10` per the artifact pulled from job 92620: GO-2026-4980 (XSS escaper bypass in `html/template`), GO-2026-4977 (quadratic concat in `net/mail` consumePhrase), GO-2026-4976 (ReverseProxy forwards beyond urlmaxqueryparams), GO-2026-4971 (panic in `net.Dial`/`LookupPort` on NUL byte on Windows), GO-2026-4918 (HTTP/2 transport infinite loop on bad SETTINGS_MAX_FRAME_SIZE), GO-2026-4981 (crash on long CNAME response in `net`), plus two more stdlib fixes. The pipeline conditional logic skipped downstream image builds (`build:image:loom-core`, `custom-server`, `loom-mills-operator`) when security stage failed, so no images had pushed since 2026-05-07 ~16:23 UTC — production state lagged accordingly (mobile-hud ran `loom-core:20260506-212337` at ~27h old; MCP servers ran `custom-server:837a5789` at ~5d old) and none of the EPIC 3 work was actually deployed despite all 4 slices having green branch pipelines and merging to main. Same silent-stale-deployment pattern as the Harbor incident (`.loom/100`), different root cause.
- **HUD `bridge.*` deprecated type aliases migrated to `internal/visibility/contracts/*` callers** (`internal/hud/{app_routes_fleet,app_routes_operations,app_test,webhook_test}.go`, `internal/hud/{coordination,fleetview,monitor,shuttle,domain/mobile}/*.go`, `internal/contracts/golden_test.go`, services/loom-core!306): EPIC 2 Batch A (`08116b38`) added `// Deprecated:` annotations to `bridge.{ServerHealth,HealthEntry,HealthDivergence,HealthDivergenceEntry,HealthResult,StatusResult,CostStatsResult,PresenceInfo}` directing callers to the new `internal/visibility/contracts/{health,status,cost,presence}` packages, but the call sites were never migrated — leaving `golangci-lint` red on 23 SA1019 staticcheck errors and blocking every MR to main. The bridge symbols are pure type aliases (`type ServerHealth = healthpkg.ServerHealth`), so this is a pure rename with zero runtime change. Two files needed local-variable renames to avoid shadowing the new package import (`monitor/health.go` `health → sh`, `monitor/fleet.go` `status → daemonStatus`). All `internal/hud/...` and `internal/contracts/...` tests still pass; `golangci-lint run ./...` is clean. The bridge package retains the aliases for backward-compat per the deprecation plan; downstream consumers can migrate at their own pace.

### Fixed
- **Mills spawn telemetry now carries `DiffPatch` + `CommitMessages` so post_review_gate LLM judges can grade real change content instead of replying with clarifying questions** (`pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn_test.go`): `mapTelemetryToResponse` previously left `DiffPatch`/`CommitMessages` empty in the `SpawnResponse`, so the rubric prompt rendered without an `=== Diff ===` section and the gemma judge replied with free-text questions like "please provide the diff…". `parseRubricEnvelope` then surfaced "no parseable score envelope in response", which M2.5 routed through the retry path — but every retry hit the same empty prompt, so post_review_gate escalated after 3 attempts. Live evidence: canary `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-VERIFY3-145417` (2026-05-17 15:08 UTC) escalated with 6 unparseable verdicts despite 5 stages succeeding. The spawn client now opts-in `HUDSpawnConfig.GitRunner` (a `CommandRunner` already shared with `git_merger.go`) plus configurable size caps (`MaxDiffBytes=32KiB`, `MaxCommitMessagesBytes=8KiB`); on terminal-success spawns it runs `git diff <base>...HEAD` + `git log --pretty=%B base..HEAD` in the spawn's NFS-backed worktree, truncates with explicit markers, and fills the response fields. Empty change → `DiffPatch=""` (not nil) so the rubric template still renders a section; M2.5's retry path is the safety net for nominal no-op flows. New tests (`TestMapTelemetry_PopulatesDiffPatchFromGit`, `TestMapTelemetry_PopulatesCommitMessages`, `TestMapTelemetry_TruncatesOversizeDiff`, `TestMapTelemetry_EmptyChangeReturnsEmptyString`) pin the boundaries.
- **Spawn orchestrator clears stale `Error` when transitioning to `Completed`** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/spawn_complete_test.go`): when a spawn completed successfully but a transient reconcile tick had previously poisoned `state.Error` (e.g., `"pod not found during reconciliation"` set by `Reconcile` before the success path landed), the `loom-spawn-state` ConfigMap row was left reading `status=completed, error="pod not found..."`. Every downstream surface — SpawnPanel, Mills Overview health banner, KPI rows — then mis-classified a successful spawn as a failure. Live evidence on the post-M1d canary `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-M1D-VERIFY-2` (2026-05-16 22:13 UTC): all three spawns had stage `Outcome=success` yet the ConfigMap carried the poison error. `completeSpawn` now explicitly clears `state.Error` before persisting, and `reapTerminalSpawn` re-checks the live row when terminal status is `Completed`/`Stopped` and clears any leftover Error. New `spawn_complete_test.go` regression test simulates the poison race and asserts the resulting persisted state is `status=completed, error=""`. Reconcile itself is unchanged (the `client==nil` early return is correct); the source of the original poison write is investigated in `.loom/119-…`.
- **Mills escalator now decodes YAML responses from `agent_handoff_create`** (`pkg/mills/clients/handoff.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/handoff_test.go`): MCP servers running in "concise text output" mode emit YAML in the tool-result text body (`entry_count: 0\nhandoff_id: bcd72b1b8f9ad438\nok: true\n…`). The escalator's response decoder previously assumed JSON and surfaced `escalator: create handoff failed error="handoff: decode: invalid character 'e' looking for beginning of value;…"` for every live escalation, dropping the resulting handoff id and forcing operators to dig through warning logs. `decodeHandoffCreateResponse` now tries JSON when the body starts with `{`/`[` and falls back to `yaml.Unmarshal` otherwise; `handoffCreateResponse` gains matching `yaml:"…"` tags so snake_case fields parse on both paths. New tests pin the exact live raw text plus the JSON happy path, an MCP-envelope YAML round-trip through the full client, and the empty/invalid error surfaces.
- **Mills canary tests stage is scoped to the checks it can actually validate, and the gate never reports a blank failure** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate.go`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate_test.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher_test.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/devbox.go`): after the prior reaper + probe + stderr-capture fix, canary `MILLS-CANARY-20260513-000659` still escalated at the tests stage — but the failure mode shifted from "language: unknown" to "lint: Passed=false, ExitCode=0, Output=\"\"" with `devbox quality gate failed: 2 checks` as the only escalation hint. Two coupled root causes: (1) the deterministic Mills canary only modifies `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md`, but the tests stage ran `go vet ./...` / `go test ./...` against the entire codebase — exercising infrastructure (module cache, registry reachability) that the canary was never meant to validate, and any transient failure escalated the canary on infra unrelated to its intent; (2) some exec wrappers (k8s SPDY upgrade errors, `poll.RetryWithBackoff` paths that bubble typed errors) can produce errors whose `Error()` is empty, leaving `qualityCheckResult.OutputTail` blank and the escalation reason useless. `DevboxRequest` now carries a `Checks []string` selector that the operator's `DevboxWorker` populates from the backlog item's labels (items labeled `mills-canary` get `Checks=["fmt"]`, keeping the canary deterministic without touching production tests). The `mcp-devbox` quality gate now synthesizes a non-empty `OutputTail` whenever the exec error message is empty (`exec failed (<errtype>) for <cmd>`), captures any partial `result.StderrTail` even when the wrapper errored, falls back to `"<check> exited N (no output)"` when a non-zero exit produces no streams, and logs every check at INFO with stdout/stderr/exit so the next operator has a forensic trail in the mcp-devbox container even when an upstream encoder strips the artifact JSON.
- **Spawn reaper closes the pod + presence + session loop on terminal spawns** (`internal/spawn/{controller,types}.go`, `internal/spawn/controller_test.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`): Mills canaries that escalated were leaving their `spawn-spawn-*` worker pods running indefinitely (observed: 14 pods × 2Gi each saturating the `devbox-quota` ResourceQuota at 32448Mi/32Gi limit, blocking new buildah builds with `forbidden: exceeded quota`). In parallel, the HUD's `/api/agents` ("29 live agents") was 80% stale presence rows from the same orphans because the fleet's reap-by-registration-age path waited 10 minutes after registration before deregistering, regardless of when the pod actually died. Root cause: `failSpawn`/`completeSpawn` only fire on the orchestrator's own state transitions — a pod that exited out-of-band or that the operator restarted away from never reached either path, so neither the K8s pod nor the agent-context presence got cleaned up. `K8sController.Reconcile` now fires a new `TerminalHook` once for any spawn whose Status is terminal (or whose pod is gone) and whose new `State.CleanupAt` timestamp is unset; the HUD orchestrator wires the hook to `backend.Stop` (pod) + `agentBridge.PresenceDeregister` (HUD live-agent row) + `EndSession` (session row), all idempotent so partial cleanup never wedges the loop. Covers four real failure modes: (1) terminal-but-pod-still-alive orphans, (2) pod gone but state never advanced, (3) operator restart with terminal state on disk, (4) discovered-already-terminal pod on first sweep. Three new reconciler tests gate the behavior.
- **HUD Mills Overview "REAL" badge replaced with a real mode pill + capability context line** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`): every capability row was rendering the raw `mode` field as a wall-of-uppercase "REAL" labels, making the panel useless at a glance. The pill now suppresses itself when mode is "real" and status is "green" (the resting state), and otherwise surfaces "stub" / "degraded" / "disabled" with warning/error/muted intent. Each row also gains a small monospace context line under the id pulled from `config_key` / `source` / `message` so an operator sees what the capability is actually bound to (e.g. `/var/lib/loom-mills/state.db` under `sqlite_store`) instead of a bare id.
- **HUD Mills Pipelines table shows the active stage instead of the static template** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`): the panel previously showed `Template` column with the same `mills-default-pipeline` value for every row, which carried no information past the run id. Added `CurrentStage` to the typed PipelineRun (the operator was already serializing it) and rendered it as a stage chip so operators can see which run is in `plan_slice` vs `tests` vs `mr` at a glance.
- **HUD Mills Squads panel renders an explanatory empty state instead of bare zeros** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/SquadsPanel.svelte`): squad cards with no recorded outcomes used to show "0.0%" success rate, "0" total, "0" in flight — visually identical to a healthy squad with 100% success rate over zero items, just unhelpfully empty. When both total and in-flight are zero, the metrics block is now replaced by a "Not yet routed" card explaining that the router has not picked this squad and pointing at the path-glob config that gates routing.
- **HUD Spawn (Labs) cards format errors and tasks instead of dumping raw blobs** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/SpawnPanel.svelte`): failed spawns used to render the entire buildah/devbox error verbatim in red — often a wall of shell-quoted text that crowded out everything else on the card. The task description had the same problem with multi-line prompts. Tasks now render the first non-empty line as a clipped headline with a `title` tooltip to the full text. Errors render a classified single-line summary (`quota` / `build` / `network` / `throttle` / `missing` / `budget` / `timeout` / `syntax`) with the headline and a `<details>` body that scrolls the full original text on demand, with intent-colored class chips so a quota error reads as warning-yellow and a build error reads as error-red.
- **Mills canary tests stage surfaces stderr instead of blank failures, and the sandbox language probe is no longer gated on syncMode** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate.go`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate_test.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/devbox.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/devbox_test.go`): the deterministic Mills canary kept escalating with `language: unknown` and `Output: ""` even after `4e27dab7` deployed the TOON decoder and sandbox language probe. Two reasons surfaced from the live `pipeline_runs` events: (1) the probe was gated on `syncMode == "git-clone"`, so sandboxes that pre-dated the syncMode flip (and any tar-pipe deployments) silently fell through to `make fmt`; (2) the fallback `make fmt` exited 1 with its real message on stderr ("No rule to make target 'fmt'"), but the gate only captured stdout — leaving `Output: ""` in `stage_results.artifacts_json` and `devbox quality gate failed: 1 checks` as the only escalation reason on 19 consecutive canaries. The probe now runs whenever host fingerprint comes back empty (regardless of syncMode), iterates the per-project workdir then `/workspace` so a misplaced clone still resolves, uses newline-terminated `echo` instead of `printf` (no-newline output races kubelet stream close on some versions), and logs stdout/stderr/exit/error on every attempt so the next operator can see why the probe answered "unknown." `qualityCheckResult` now carries `stderr_tail` and the operator's `devbox` client falls back from `output_tail` to `stderr_tail` so the canary's actual failure text reaches both the artifact JSON and the escalation summary.
- **Mills devbox gates handle git-clone sandboxes without slow broken rebuild loops** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/`, `internal/devbox/{baseimage,dockerfile}/`, `pkg/mills/clients/devbox.go`): follow-up production canaries reached the tests stage but still felt slow because git-clone devbox builds missed the prebuilt Go 1.25 base, rebuilt common tooling layers for each new fingerprint, and produced sandboxes where `go`/`gofmt` were not on `PATH`. Devbox base-image lookup now accepts patch versions, git-clone fallback images inherit the prebuilt Go base with an explicit Go PATH, and quality gates probe the hydrated sandbox for `go.mod`/`package.json`/Python/Rust markers when local source is unavailable. The Mills devbox client also decodes TOON as well as JSON so a normal failed gate is surfaced as a quality result instead of a response decode error.
- **Mills canaries no longer stall on cold spawn/devbox image paths** (`internal/devbox/backend/{k8s,k8s_build}.go`, `cmd/{mcp-devbox,loom-mills-operator}/`, `pkg/mills/clients/devbox.go`, `k8s/base/servers/{devbox,mobile-hud}/deployment.yaml`): production canaries reached the real runtime path but a Buildah pod was evicted for `ephemeral-storage` because it requested `0`, and later test stages failed because the in-cluster devbox server had only `/app/services/loom-core/bin` rather than source files for local language detection. Buildah pods now request/limit ephemeral storage, skip existing runtime images via a registry manifest probe instead of pulling the whole image, avoid the degraded storage node set, and devbox git-clone mode falls back to a generic toolchain image when local source is unavailable so the cloned repo can run the actual `make` checks. Research is also wired to the same backlog-aware prompt builder as the other Mills stages instead of sending Weaver an empty prompt. Plain-text devbox MCP errors now surface as the stage error instead of a JSON decode wrapper.
- **Mills HUD spawn client tolerates short HUD rollouts** (`pkg/mills/{clients,pipeline}/`): production canaries launched during a `mobile-hud` rollout could burn all three `plan_slice` attempts in milliseconds on `connect: connection refused`, then escalate before the replacement HUD endpoint was ready. The HUD spawn client now applies bounded retry/backoff to POST/GET calls so brief Kubernetes Service gaps do not consume Mills stage attempts, and failed stage rows persist the dispatcher error in `log_tail` so HUD/API views show the real reason instead of a blank failure.
- **Spawn Buildah pods and mobile HUD avoid degraded Kubernetes nodes** (`internal/devbox/backend/{k8s,k8s_build}.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed,spawn}.go`, `cmd/{loom,mcp-devbox}/`, `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): production canary runtime builds were still slow and opaque when scheduled on `k3s-w-7`, where kubelet log proxy calls returned 502; HUD itself could also land there, hiding logs and exec. The devbox K8s backend now supports comma-separated Buildah node avoidance (`SPAWN_BUILD_AVOID_NODES` / `DEVBOX_K8S_BUILD_AVOID_NODES`) via required node affinity, mobile HUD sets `SPAWN_BUILD_AVOID_NODES=k3s-w-7`, and the mobile HUD Deployment avoids `k3s-w-7` directly. Spawn runtime image tags also drop the extra `mcp/` path component when `SPAWN_REGISTRY` already includes the Harbor project prefix.
- **HUD Mills spawns reuse stable agent runtime images and resume pre-runtime state after restart** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/{backend,docker,k8s_build}.go`): the lean runtime image still felt slow because each spawn tagged the image with a unique spawn id, defeating registry cache reuse, and a HUD restart during Buildah left persisted spawns stuck at `building` with no goroutine to finish the handoff. Runtime builds now use a content-hashed `spawn-runtime-<agent>:<hash>` tag, Buildah/Docker skip the build when that image already exists, and HUD startup resumes persisted `pending`/`building` spawns that have not created their runtime pod yet.
- **Mills spawn images no longer install project lint/security toolchains** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): HUD agent spawns now build a lean shared runtime image with Go, Git, Node/npm, Python, and the selected agent CLI instead of generating a project-specific devbox image that installs `gosec`, `golangci-lint`, and runs dependency setup during planning/implementation. This removes the 15+ minute Buildah delay seen in production canaries before the agent even starts.
- **HUD spawn requests are idempotent for active Mills run/stage work** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): repeated spawn requests carrying the same `LOOM_MILLS_RUN_ID` and `LOOM_MILLS_STAGE` now return the existing non-terminal spawn instead of accepting a duplicate Buildah/runtime path. This closes the remaining rollout race where the Mills store preserved the correct `spawn_id` but HUD still created an orphan duplicate during operator startup resume.
- **Accepted Mills spawns stay resumable across interrupted polls** (`pkg/mills/{pipeline,store,clients}/`): if an operator rollout interrupts HUD spawn polling after the spawn has already been accepted, the runner now leaves that stage attempt pending instead of burning retries and POSTing duplicate spawns. Stage upserts also preserve an existing `spawn_id` when a later error write has no spawn id, and terminal HUD statuses are marked explicitly so real failed/stopped spawns still fail the stage.
- **Mills pipeline starts no longer overwrite pending spawn identity** (`pkg/mills/{store,pipeline}/`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`): overlapping operator starts for the same run/stage attempt can no longer replace the already-accepted HUD `spawn_id` in `stage_results`; the runner suppresses duplicate starts inside one operator process, the store rejects pending-spawn conflicts across overlapping processes, and the operator's manual escalate endpoint now gives operators a clean way to close contaminated canary runs.
- **Mills pipeline resume now adopts accepted HUD spawns** (`pkg/mills/{pipeline,clients}/`, `internal/{hud,spawn}/`): operator rollouts could resume a run while its HUD Buildah spawn was still building, then burn all stage retries immediately because the accepted `spawn_id` was only held in memory. Spawn acceptance is now recorded as a pending `stage_results` row before polling, runner resume re-attaches to that pending spawn attempt, and HUD in Kubernetes stores spawn state in a `devbox` ConfigMap so mobile-hud rollouts keep the spawn registry instead of returning an empty list after restart.
- **Spawned agent images install Node/npm before npm-published CLIs** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): Mills canaries that resumed after the operator recovery fix reached HUD spawn image builds, then failed on Go-only projects because the generated `golang:*` Dockerfile appended `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code` without first installing `npm`. Agent CLI install layers now ensure `nodejs npm` exists on Alpine and Debian bases before installing Claude Code, Codex, or Gemini.
- **Mills backlog rows close when pipeline runs reach terminal state** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/{reconciler,pipeline}/`): canary pipelines could be correctly `done`/`escalated` while their owning backlog items stayed `running`, leaving stale Mills Backlog HUD rows after failed canary attempts. Runner and fan-out integrator terminal paths now move backlog items to `merged` or `escalated` with the pipeline, and operator startup repairs pre-existing running backlog rows whose pipeline runs are already terminal.
- **Mills operator resumes in-flight pipeline runs after rollout** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/{reconciler,store}/`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/`): production canary rollouts could strand a run in `planning`/`implementing` if the operator pod restarted after recording a stage failure but before the runner goroutine retried or escalated. Startup now resumes already-started non-terminal runs once, fatal asynchronous runner errors are escalated instead of only logged, and fan-out parents that already reached post-integration stages resume through the runner rather than re-running integration.
- **K8s devbox Buildah waits fail fast when build pods disappear** (`internal/devbox/backend/k8s_wait.go`): pod watch deletion events now return immediate errors for both runtime startup and build completion waits, so a deleted or rescheduled Buildah pod cannot leave HUD spawns stuck in `building` until the long build timeout.
- **Mills spawn canary no longer fails while Buildah is still preparing runtime pods** (`internal/spawn/controller.go`, `internal/devbox/dockerfile/compose.go`): K8s spawn reconciliation now preserves pre-runtime `creating`/`building` states that have no runtime pod yet, leaving Buildah-owned image creation to the HUD orchestrator instead of marking the spawn failed mid-build. Generated Go Dockerfiles now use Go 1.25.10 as the 1.25 floor, so stale 1.25.8 detection cannot break repos whose `go.mod` requires the patched toolchain.
- **Mills HUD spawn client accepts mobile response envelopes** (`pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`): production canary spawn requests reached the mobile HUD API, but Mills decoded the POST/GET bodies as flat spawn objects while the mobile API returns `{ok,data}` envelopes. The spawn client now unwraps envelope responses while keeping legacy flat-response compatibility, so successful HUD spawns no longer look like empty `spawn_id` failures to the operator.
- **Mills spawn git-clone init image now resolves** (`internal/devbox/backend/{k8s,k8s_objects,k8s_test}.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed}.go`, `internal/daemon/{config,hud_embed}.go`, `cmd/{loom,mcp-devbox}/`): the first quota-aware production canary got past CPU admission but the Buildah pod failed before cloning because `alpine/git:2.47` is not a published Docker Hub tag. The shared K8s devbox backend now defaults git-clone init containers to the verified `alpine/git:2.47.2` image and exposes `DEVBOX_K8S_GIT_CLONE_IMAGE` / `SPAWN_GIT_CLONE_IMAGE` overrides so future image-tag moves can be handled by config.
- **Mills operator MCP session recovery** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/{handoff,worktree}.go`): the operator no longer stays fail-closed forever when `mcp-agent-context` or the MCP gateway is unavailable during boot. It keeps the hub client configured, retries `agent_session_start` in the background, flips the `mcp_hub_session` capability green after recovery, and lets handoff/worktree clients read the recovered session id dynamically.
- **HUD Operations Fleet view — divider overlap + empty-state polish** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/FleetPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/EconomicsPanel.svelte`, services/loom-core!306): two-pass cleanup of UX issues that show whenever the system is idle. (1) Live Agents row 0 was rendering the "No active session match · N" divider+label even when every row was ungrouped — the absolutely-positioned label wrapped into the cell and overlapped the agent ID. Now only renders when grouped rows exist above (`ungroupedStartIndex > 0`), and `.ungrouped-label` gets `white-space: nowrap` as defense-in-depth. (2) Token Economics rendered six "INSUFFICIENT DATA / STUB / WEAVER METRICS UNREACHABLE" cards (~140px of dead vertical space) when no telemetry had accumulated yet; now collapses to a single banner. (3) Tokens / Memory Items / Graph Entities stat cards render `—` (dim) instead of bare `0` when their counts are zero, matching EconomicsPanel's null-ratio convention. (4) Infrastructure card replaces inline `<span>no data</span>` with `—` plus a proper `metric-sub` explainer ("no tunnels · no cache stats"). (5) Memory Tiers collapses the trio of 0% gauges into a single `EmptyState` ("No memory items yet — Tiers populate as agents record decisions, findings, and recall context") when all three tier counts are zero. Embedded HUD `frontend/dist/` bundle is rebuilt as part of the change since `internal/hud/app.go` ships it via `//go:embed`.
- **`/api/fleet/economics` no longer requires admin token** (`internal/hud/domain/fleet/economics.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/EconomicsPanel.svelte`): the Token Economics widget is on the default Operations panel, but its backing endpoint required an admin token (via `RequireAdminToken`), so any viewer without a configured operator token saw `401 invalid admin token` instead of the dashboard. Removes the gate — the endpoint returns aggregate ratios (token savings, cost ratio, context waste, compression, local utilization) with no PII, so a public read is correct. As a defence-in-depth follow-up, EconomicsPanel.svelte now also forwards `X-Admin-Token` from `labsAuthStore.adminToken` when one is configured, so future re-gating wouldn't break the widget for users with a token set.
- **Embedded HUD admin token now wired from env (closes 403 on admin endpoints)** (`internal/daemon/config.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`, services/loom-core!280): the `loomd → startEmbeddedHUD` path silently dropped `AdminToken` when constructing `hud.Config{}`, so admin-protected mutations (spawn launch, agent nudge queue policy, etc.) returned `403 admin token is not configured; set HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` even when the env var was set on the host. The standalone `loom hud` CLI wired it via `applyEnvString`, but the embedded path didn't. Adds an `AdminToken` field to `EmbeddedHUDConfig` (yaml: `admin_token`), sources `hudCfg.AdminToken` from `cfg.AdminToken` → `LOOM_HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` (preferred) → `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` (fallback) — same precedence as `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_nudge.go`. Adds the `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` env to the `mobile-hud` deployment manifest sourced from `loom-secrets` (key must exist in the Secret before this rolls out, else the pod CrashLoopBackOffs on missing-key).

### Added
- **Mills council participants can run on real FlexInfer backends** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): production operators with `FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL` now wire council reviewers, editor, and contradiction judge to FlexInfer-backed model calls instead of the old `FakeReviewer` / `FakeEditor` / `FakeLLMJudge` slice fallback. Local/degraded runs keep the deterministic fake fallback for smoke tests, and the autonomy readiness matrix still fails closed when that fallback is active.
- **Engram tech tree S4a — devbox-sandboxed command-proof verification** (`pkg/agentcontext/engram_verify.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/service.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/devbox_runner.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/main.go`, `mcp/skills/agent-engrams/references/engram-schema.md`, services/loom-core!340): closes the last `skipped` proof status in `agent_engram_verify`. Adds a `CommandRunner` interface that `verifyOne` invokes for `command:` proofs (extracts the first `command:` line from the proof block) — exit 0 maps to `verified`, non-zero to `failing` (stderr first line surfaced in the reason), exit 124 / timeout to `failing` with a timeout reason, transport errors (daemon down, sandbox unreachable) to `skipped` so transient infra blips can't downgrade an engram. Default 2m timeout, override per-call via `command_timeout`; `skip_command=true` still force-skips. `agentcontext` stays free of any direct devbox dependency — a new `cmd/mcp-agent-context/devbox_runner.go` adapter wraps the existing `toolexec.Client` (already used for workflow tool execution) to call `devbox_exec` over the daemon socket; project name comes from `LOOM_DEVBOX_PROJECT` or the same canonical-repo heuristic the verifier uses (`inferProjectName` ↔ `inferRepoName`, both worktree-aware). When `LOOM_SOCKET` is unset (mcp-agent-context running standalone), no runner is wired and command proofs return `skipped` with an operator-friendly reason. 8 new tests cover `extractCommand` parsing (case-insensitive, multi-line, empty payload), `firstLine`/`parseDurationArg` helpers, and verified/failing/timeout/transport-error/no-runner/skip-flag-overrides-runner/unparsable-command outcomes via a fake `CommandRunner` injected with `SetCommandRunner`. Race-clean. Followups deferred: S4b (seed engrams migration script), S4c (frontend summary badge), S4d (weekly CI scheduled `agent_engram_verify(all=true)` job).
- **Spectator plan Phase 5 — iOS `LiveSessionsView`** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/Models/LiveSessionEvent.swift`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/ViewModels/LiveSessionsViewModel.swift`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanion/Views/LiveSessions/LiveSessionsView.swift`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanion/ContentView.swift`, regenerated `apps/loom-companion-ios/LoomCompanion.xcodeproj`): closes Phase 5 of the spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). The iPhone companion gets a Live segment under the Agents tab — picker now reads "Roster · Sessions · Live" — that mirrors the HUD's `LiveSessionsCard` (Phase 3): one row per active session with the trailing handful of tool calls, in-flight indicators, and a tap-to-expand sheet showing the full ring buffer (last 20 calls, capped via `liveSessionsRecentCallsLimit`). New `LiveSessionEvent` Codable models in `LoomCompanionKit/Models/` mirror the daemon EventBus payloads (sessionStart/end, agentStatusChange, toolCallStart/end) with permissive decoding (`decodeIfPresent` everywhere) so a future Go-side payload addition does not crash the iOS client. New `@Observable @MainActor LiveSessionsViewModel` subscribes to the existing app-wide `SSEEventBroadcaster` (idempotent `subscribe(to:)` so multiple `.task` modifiers don't double-register), maintains a `[String: LiveSession]` keyed by `session_id`, applies the same five canonical event handlers as the HUD store (with the same late-arriving-agent_id backfill, synthetic-end-only entry for codex.turn coarse case, and 30s post-end retention), and runs a `MainActor` reap task every 5s. The new SwiftUI screen handles iOS-26 ViewBuilder type-check limits by extracting `LiveCallDetailRow` and per-section helpers; uses `Color.secondary`/`Color.red` (not the Hierarchical shape-styles) for foregroundStyle on Labels to keep the type system happy. ContentView's PeopleSection enum gains a `.live` case; the iPhone segmented picker widens to 260pt to fit the third option; subtitle text now switches via `peopleSectionSubtitle`. xcodegen regenerates the project after the new files land — `make mobile-ios-project-sync` was bundled. iOS sim build (`xcodebuild -scheme LoomCompanion -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro,OS=26.2' build`) passes. Phase 3.2 (auth-gated `redacted`-tier expanded view) defers; the iOS client pins TierPublic at the API layer for now and will gain a settings toggle once the HUD ships its auth-gated tier.
- **Spectator plan Phase 3 — HUD `LiveSessionsCard`** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/liveSessions.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/LiveSessionsCard.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/OverviewPanel.svelte`, regenerated `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`): closes Phase 3 of the spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). The Operations landing page now carries a live spectator card that subscribes to the SSE event stream from Phase 1.3/2.x and renders one row per active agent session with the trailing handful of tool calls, in-flight indicators, and an expand-on-click view of the full ring buffer (last 20 calls). New `liveSessionsStore` is event-driven (no polling): `eventStore.on('session.start' / 'session.end' / 'agent.status.change' / 'tool.call.start' / 'tool.call.end')` populates a `Map<sessionID, LiveSession>` keyed by `session_id`; each session keeps a status (active/idle/offline/expired/unknown) plus a capped most-recent-first array of `ToolCall` entries. Late-arriving `agent_id` on a session backfills the early placeholder so a `tool.call.start` that arrived before its `session.start` still renders correctly. Synthetic end-only entries are created when a `tool.call.end` has no matching `start` (the `codex.turn` coarse case from Phase 2.2c). Sessions retain visibility for 30s after `session.end` so operators see the final tail before reaping. The card renders an `EmptyState` until the first session arrives ("Sessions appear here as soon as a Claude Code, Gemini, or Codex CLI emits a SessionStart hook"). All payload data on the wire was already redacted at TierPublic by Phase 2.x producers; the UI only displays what the daemon publishes (no client-side unmasking). Wired into `OverviewPanel.svelte` between Surfaces and Mills KPIs as a new `live-sessions-section` so it's visible on the default landing view per the spec. Embedded HUD `frontend/dist/` rebuilt as part of the change since `internal/hud/app.go` ships it via `//go:embed`. Phase 3.2 (auth-gated `redacted`-tier expanded view) defers to a follow-up slice.
- **Spectator plan Phase 2.2b/c — Gemini and Codex platforms wired into `loom agent event-emit`** (`cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit_test.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_codex.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_hooks_test.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`): closes the remaining two slices of Phase 2.2 from `.loom/99-implementation-plan-agent-telemetry-spectator-2026-05-04.md`. **2.2b — Gemini**: the CLI gains a `--platform gemini-cli` mode that delegates to the same shared `nativeHookToEvent` normalizer Claude Code uses (Gemini's hook stdin schema converged on `tool_name`/`tool_input`/`session_id`/`tool_response`); `telemetryEventEmitPlatform` returns `"gemini-cli"` for the gemini-cli AgentID; `canonicalTelemetryHookForEvent` learns Gemini's `BeforeTool`/`AfterTool` event names so `appendTelemetryEventEmitHooks` injects event-emit on SessionStart, SessionEnd, and AfterTool with the correct `--hook` flag (post-tool-use); the gemini profile YAML opts in via `extras: [telemetry_eventEmit]`. **2.2c — Codex**: Codex has no SessionStart/PreToolUse/PostToolUse — just a single `notify` command per turn-end. The CLI gains a `--platform codex` mode that maps the notify payload to a coarse `tool.call.end` with `tool_name="codex.turn"` (per spec §"best-effort via notify hook only"; payload optionally surfaces `status`/`duration_ms`/`exit_code`/`error` when present, omits when absent). `codexNotifyCommand` learns a `telemetryEmit bool` parameter that, when true, appends a best-effort `printf "$NOTIFY_PAYLOAD" | loom agent event-emit --hook post-tool-use --platform codex --quiet || true` pipe after the existing keepalive-wrap background; `emitCodexPreamble` reads the codex profile's `extras` list to decide. Codex's notify shell snippet now reads from `${INPUT:-${1:-}}` so payloads passed as positional `$1` (the actual codex contract) feed both the existing session-id extraction and the new event-emit pipe. Codex profile YAML opts in via `extras: [telemetry_eventEmit]`. 13 new tests cover gemini's three-event injection (SessionStart/SessionEnd/AfterTool), AfterTool→post-tool-use canonical mapping, codex's coarse `tool.call.end` synthesis, codex non-post-tool-use rejection, codex absent-field omission, telemetryEmit-disabled-by-default in `codexNotifyCommand`, and YAML opt-in regression pins for both platforms. After this slice, the only platform without event-emit wiring is Kilocode (no native hook surface).
- **Spectator plan Phase 2.2 — Claude Code hook generator wires SessionStart/Stop/PreToolUse/PostToolUse into `loom agent event-emit`** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_hooks_test.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`, `pkg/generator/configs_claude.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_gemini.go`): closes Phase 2.2 (Claude Code slice) of the agent telemetry event bus + live spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). Adds a new `telemetry_eventEmit` extras case to `appendHookExtras` that walks the platform's existing hook event slots and appends a hook block piping the platform's stdin payload into Phase 2.1's `loom agent event-emit --hook <canonical> --platform <p> --agent-id "$AGENT_ID" --quiet || true`. Canonical mapping pins SessionStart→`session-start`, Stop/SessionEnd→`session-end`, PreToolUse→`pre-tool-use`, PostToolUse→`post-tool-use`. `appendHookExtras` signature evolved from `(hooks, extras []string, loomBinary)` → `(hooks, hp HookProfile, loomBinary)` so the new case can read `hp.AgentID` to pick the correct `--platform` flag without duplicating dispatch. `telemetryEventEmitPlatform(hp)` returns "" for AgentIDs without a CLI normalizer (Gemini/Codex slot in via follow-up slices once `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit.go` learns their stdin schemas), so the case is a silent no-op rather than emitting hooks pointing at a `--platform` the CLI rejects. Claude Code's profile in `platform_profiles.yaml` now opts in via `extras: [..., telemetry_eventEmit]`, so a single `loom sync claude --regen` produces a `.claude/settings.json` with all four canonical hooks wired end-to-end. 9 new tests cover canonical-hook mapping (including Stop→session-end), platform allowlist, all-four-events injection, no-op for unsupported platform / absent extra, best-effort `|| true` + `--quiet` posture, and a regression-pinning assertion that the YAML profile carries the extra. Three existing extras call sites + six existing test sites updated to the new signature; `golangci-lint run ./pkg/generator/...` clean.
- **Spectator plan Phase 2.1 — `loom agent event-emit` CLI for hook-driven telemetry** (`cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_agent_event_emit_test.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_agent.go`): closes Phase 2.1 of the agent telemetry event bus + live spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). Adds a CLI subcommand that reads a CLI hook payload from stdin and publishes the canonical telemetry event (`session.start`/`session.end`, `tool.call.start`/`tool.call.end`, `agent.status.change`) to the loom daemon's event bus via the existing `/events/publish` HTTP endpoint (the same endpoint Phase 1.3 wired for `mcp-agent-context`). Flags: `--hook <session-start|session-end|pre-tool-use|post-tool-use>`, `--platform <claude-code|generic>` (default claude-code), `--agent-id`, `--daemon-url`, `--quiet` for hook-context safety. The `claude-code` platform normalizes Claude Code's stdin schemas (`tool_name`/`tool_input`/`session_id`/`tool_response`/`exit_code`/`duration_ms`/`error`) into canonical event payloads; the `generic` platform passes through a pre-normalized `{type, payload}` envelope. Tool args go through `pkg/telemetry/redact.Redact(toolName, args, TierPublic)` before publication so secrets never reach the bus (regression test plants `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE` and asserts it does not survive). CLI-side allowlist restricts emission to the five canonical hook-emittable types so a typo'd hook script can't accidentally publish (for example) `server.health`. Best-effort fire-and-forget publish via `pkg/eventpub.HTTPPublisher` so a slow/down daemon never stalls a user's CLI hook. 17 unit tests cover Claude Code normalization for every hook variant, redaction enforcement, generic-platform passthrough + hook/type mismatch rejection, allowlist, env-precedence (`--daemon-url > $LOOM_DAEMON_HTTP_URL > 127.0.0.1:9876`), and `intField` numeric coercion. Unblocks Phase 2.2 (hook config generator updates wiring `loom agent event-emit` into Claude Code/Gemini/Codex `settings.json`) and Phase 3 (`LiveSessionsCard` HUD card).
- **Spectator plan Phase 1.3 — cross-process Publisher via HTTP POST endpoint** (`internal/daemon/api_events.go`, `pkg/eventpub/publisher.go`, `cmd/loomd/main.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/main.go`): closes Phase 1.3 of the agent telemetry event bus + live spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). Wires the gap so session lifecycle + agent.status.change events emitted by the Publisher in `mcp-agent-context` (slice 1.1) reach the daemon EventBus and the existing `/events` SSE fan-out — making them visible end-to-end at `curl http://localhost:9876/events`. Architecture: `pkg/agentcontext` cannot import `internal/daemon` (Go's `internal/` rule), so cross-process publish goes via a new `POST /events/publish` endpoint on the daemon. New `pkg/eventpub.HTTPPublisher` implements `agentcontext.Publisher` with best-effort 2s-timeout POSTs (failures log at debug — never blocks tool handlers). `internal/daemon/api_events.go` accepts JSON envelopes, requires Bearer auth when `embedded_hud.admin_token` / `LOOM_HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` / `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` is set, and falls back to loopback-only when no token is configured (1MB body limit). `cmd/loomd/main.go` registers the route on the metrics mux and exports `LOOM_DAEMON_HTTP_URL=http://defaultMetricsAddr` alongside the existing `LOOM_SOCKET` so child MCP servers discover the endpoint without flag plumbing. `cmd/mcp-agent-context/main.go` constructs the publisher when the env is set; no-op otherwise (preserves standalone behavior). 13 new test cases cover POST shape, Bearer auth matrix, loopback policy, malformed envelopes, concurrent publish (race-detector clean), and best-effort behavior on a closed port.
- **Spectator plan Phase 2.3 — per-tool-call events from spawn telemetry** (`internal/daemon/events.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/spawn_telemetry.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/spawn_telemetry_test.go`): closes Phase 2.3 of the agent telemetry event bus + live spectator plan (`.loom/97/98/99`). Replaces the post-hoc `agent.spawn.telemetry.delta` batch granularity with per-call `tool.call.start` and `tool.call.end` events fired within ~100ms of each tool invocation; the legacy delta event continues firing additively (no regression). Adds `EventToolCallStart` + `EventToolCallEnd` constants on the daemon EventBus alongside matching `ToolCallStartEvent` / `ToolCallEndEvent` payload structs in `spawn_telemetry.go`. The accumulator's `StartToolCall` / `CompleteToolCall` now publish to the bus when a publisher is wired (no-op when unset, so test callers don't need plumbing); args are redacted at `TierPublic` via the existing `pkg/telemetry/redact` package and `CallID` is generated via `crypto/rand` so start/end pairs correlate. Six new tests cover happy-path correlation, secret masking surviving truncation, no-publisher fallback, and concurrent-call ordering.
- **Mills v2 Phase 6 slice 6.2 — Subrun reconcile pickup + spawn-driver helper** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/recursion.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler_test.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_subrun_test.go`, `tools/spawn-driver/src/control.ts` (new)): closes Phase 6 slice 6.2 from `.loom/94-implementation-plan-mills-v2-hierarchical-swarm-2026-05-02.md`. Subruns created mid-run by `recursion.SubrunGuard` are now started by the next reconcile tick — `Reconciler.Tick` calls a new `pickupQueuedSubruns` helper after the main backlog loop that walks `pipeline_runs` rows in `state=queued AND parent_run_id IS NOT NULL AND attempts=0` (new `PipelineDAO.ListQueuedSubruns`) and hands each to the same `PipelineStarter` that drives fresh-from-backlog launches; per-row failures count into `TickResult.Errored` without blocking the rest. To prevent the main loop double-starting the same backlog, `SubrunGuard.SubrunCreate` now atomically claims the target backlog by transitioning it to `BacklogRunning` after the subrun row is inserted (best-effort: missing-backlog is logged but doesn't unwind the successful create). New `tools/spawn-driver/src/control.ts` ships a dependency-free TS helper (`createMillsSubrun`, `millsRecursionAvailable`, `SubrunGuardError` with stable `.code`) workers can `import` to call the operator's `/subrun` endpoint when `LOOM_MILLS_RUN_ID` + `LOOM_MILLS_OPERATOR_URL` + `LOOM_MILLS_ADMIN_TOKEN` are all set on their environment; the `SubrunGuardError.code` field round-trips the operator's stable guard codes (`recursion_depth_exceeded`, `budget_subrun_too_large`, `recursion_cycle_detected`, etc.) so worker code can branch on the rejection reason. Tests pin the reconciler pickup + starter-error-survival + the slice-6.1 happy-path's new claim-backlog invariant. MCP-tool surfacing (so an agent's tool list automatically includes `mills_pipeline_subrun_create`) is deferred to slice 6.3 alongside the recursion-depth histogram + HUD parent→child tree.
- **Mills v2 Phase 6 slice 6.1 — Bounded recursion guards + subrun endpoint** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/recursion.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{server,handlers_pipeline,handlers_subrun_test}.go`): closes Phase 6 slice 6.1 from `.loom/94-implementation-plan-mills-v2-hierarchical-swarm-2026-05-02.md`. Pipeline workers can now request a child run via `POST /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}/subrun` (admin-token gated); the new `pipeline.SubrunGuard` runs four guards in order — policy gate (V2-D6 default disabled), parent existence, depth ceiling (`policy.recursion.max_depth`, default 2), per-subrun budget cap (`policy.recursion.subrun_max_budget_share`, default 0.5 of `pipeline.max_usd_per_run`), and a cycle detector that walks `parent_run_id` looking for a backlog id reuse. Each rejection returns a typed `GuardError` whose stable `Code` string maps directly to the spec acceptance language: `recursion_depth_exceeded` → 400, `budget_subrun_too_large` → 400, `recursion_cycle_detected` → 400, `recursion_disabled` → 403, `recursion_parent_not_found` → 404. New `store.PipelineDAO.CreateSubrun` is the insert-only persistence helper that defensively checks the parent row exists and rejects subruns with `Depth <= 0`. Tests cover all six guard codes plus a happy-path depth-1 success that round-trips through the store. Slices 6.2 (worker MCP tool surface) + 6.3 (telemetry / KPI / HUD recursion histogram) come next.
- **Mills v2 Phase 5 slice 5.3 — HUD "Debate Rounds" expander + GET /api/mills/council/runs/{id}/debate** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_council,handlers_test,server}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{components/Mills/CouncilPanel.svelte,stores/mills.svelte.ts}`, regenerated `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`): closes Phase 5 slice 5.3 from `.loom/94-implementation-plan-mills-v2-hierarchical-swarm-2026-05-02.md`. Operator gains a new read-only endpoint `GET /api/mills/council/runs/{id}/debate` that returns the persisted `council_debate_rounds` rows for a council run (200 + array when debate ran, 200 + `[]` when the run was single-pass, 404 when the run id is unknown — so the UI distinguishes "no debate ran" from "no such run"). HUD `CouncilPanel.svelte` Council rows now expand on click into a "Debate Rounds" view that lazy-fetches the transcript via `millsStore.loadDebate(runID)`; results cache in a per-run `debateByRun` map so subsequent expansions are instant. Each transcript row renders with a round pill (`R0`, `R1`, ...), a friendly role label (`Editor proposes` / `Reviewer critiques` / `Moderator decision` / `Editor revises`), per-row USD cost, summary, and any `artifact_deltas` as `<code>` chips. New `TestHandleCouncilRunDebate` covers all three response shapes (with-debate, no-debate-empty, unknown-404). Slice 5.1 + 5.2 + 5.3 together complete Phase 5.
- **Mills v2 Phase 5 slice 5.2 — Council Debate Mode persistence + cost rollup** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_debate.go`, `pkg/mills/budget.go`, `pkg/mills/store_adapter.go`, `pkg/mills/runner/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/budget_test.go`, `pkg/mills/runner/runner_test.go`): closes Phase 5 slice 5.2 from `.loom/94-implementation-plan-mills-v2-hierarchical-swarm-2026-05-02.md`. The runner now persists every entry of `out.Sidecar.Debate.Rounds` into `council_debate_rounds` (FK on the just-committed `council_runs` row) after non-dryrun debate runs, with best-effort per-row error logging so a single insert failure can't unwind a successful council run. Dryrun runs still populate the in-memory transcript on `Sidecar.Debate` but skip the canonical writes (zero-side-effect invariant preserved). Full debate spend (editor.propose + every reviewer.critique + every moderator.assess + every editor.revise) now rolls into `council_runs.cost_frontier_usd` via the runner's pre-writer stamp on `out.Sidecar.CostUSD`, so `CouncilDAO.SumCostSince` (and therefore the daily council cap) sees the full debate cost — not just the editor's final-revise call. Local-cost split for debate is deferred to slice 5.3 alongside per-round backend tracking; the simplification is documented inline. Adds `DebateDAO.SumCostSince` (mirrors `CouncilDAO.SumCostSince` and `PipelineDAO.SumCostSince`), extends `BudgetReader` with `DebateCostSince`, and ships `Budget.DebateSpentSince(ctx)` as an informational helper (no enforcement) for HUD widgets and future per-debate-tier daily caps. New tests pin: 3-row transcript persistence on a 2-round-converge fixture; cost rollup equality between `DebateDAO.SumCostSince`, `Sidecar.Debate.TotalCostUSD`, and `council_runs.cost_frontier_usd`; dryrun zero-write invariant; `DebateSpentSince` passthrough + nil-reader error path.
- **Mills v2 Phase 5 slice 5.1 — Council Debate Mode runner + moderator** (`pkg/mills/council/{debate,moderator,sidecar,editor,reviewer}.go`, `pkg/mills/council/prompts/moderator.md`, `pkg/mills/council/debate_test.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/policy_test.go`, `pkg/mills/runner/runner.go`): closes Phase 5 slice 5.1 from `.loom/94-implementation-plan-mills-v2-hierarchical-swarm-2026-05-02.md`. Adds a multi-round editor↔reviewer debate loop per spec §"Council Debate Mode" (Round 0 propose → Round 1+ critique→assess→revise, max 3 rounds, mid-round budget gate at 80% of `policy.debate.max_usd`). New `Debate` type orchestrates an `Editor` + `Reviewer` `Dispatcher` + `Moderator` to convergence; new `Reviser` and `Refocuser` optional sub-interfaces let the existing `Editor` / `Reviewer` contracts gain `Revise(prior, critiques, focusAreas)` and `RefocusedReview(brief, lens, focusAreas)` without breaking v1 callers (when an editor/reviewer doesn't implement the v2 method, debate degrades to single-pass and emits an `early_exit_reason: editor_not_reviser` transcript). `FakeEditor` / `FakeReviewer` / new `FakeModerator` exercise every spec scenario (2-round converge, 3-round to cap, mid-round budget exit, moderator error surface, validation refusals). `Sidecar.Debate` is the on-disk transcript (`{enabled, rounds, early_exit_reason, total_cost_usd}`) populated only when debate ran; v1 single-pass runs leave it nil so existing artifact / judge / mutator stages and JSON consumers see no shape change. `pkg/mills/runner.Runner` gains an optional `Moderator` field and now branches on `policy.Debate.Enabled.AllowedFor(string(trigger))` (cron / roadmap / incident / manual) — when enabled-by-policy but no moderator is wired, the runner logs the degradation and falls back to single-pass rather than failing the run. Defaults remain v2-D5: debate off for cron + roadmap_change, on for incident + manual. Persistence of `council_debate_rounds` rows + per-trigger budget cap enforcement land in slices 5.2 + 5.3.
- **Mills v2 policy gating + cross-repo registry foundation (closes `.loom/95-implementation-status-mills-v2-2026-05-02.md` G-1..G-4)** (`pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/policy_test.go`, `pkg/mills/squads/router.go`, `pkg/mills/audit/triggers.go`, `pkg/mills/crossrepo/{types,registry,registry_test}.go`, `mcp/skills/mills-ops/SKILL.md`, services/loom-core!262 + platform/gitops!69): Phase 1–3 of the v2 hierarchical-swarm rollout shipped squads + audit code without policy gates, blocking the Phase 8 default-on flip. This change adds six v2 sections to the `Policy` struct (`Squads`, `Audit`, `CrossRepo`, `Debate`, `Recursion`, `AdaptivePolicy`) at safe defaults (squads off, audit advisory-on, others off), bumps schema to `version: 2` while keeping v1 forward-compat, and gates `squads.Router.Pick` and `audit.Triggers.OnArtifactsCommitted/OnPipelineMerged` behind a minimal `PolicyGate` interface that production wires to the live `PolicyManager` for hot-reload. Cross-repo Phase 4 slice 4.1 lands the `pkg/mills/crossrepo/` package: `Registry` accessor + fsnotify-watched loader mirroring `pkg/mills/squads/loader.go` semantics (strict initial parse, lenient reloads, last-good-config preserved on invalid YAML, `atomic.Pointer[Registry]` for lock-free reads). Companion platform/gitops!69 ships the v2 ConfigMap schema, seeds `repos.yaml` (loom-core/loom auto_merge=true, flexdeck auto_merge=false), and mounts `loom-mills-repos` at `/etc/loom-mills/repos` (inert until Phase 4 slice 4.2 wires the loader). Phase 0 slice 0.1 also closes the empty `mcp/skills/mills-ops/SKILL.md` operator skill (136 lines, frontmatter parses; covers pause/replay/audit/recover/rollout-staging scenarios with GitOps-first workflow). Implementation status + spec-driven next sequence captured in `.loom/95-implementation-status-mills-v2-2026-05-02.md`.

### Changed
- **GitLab pipeline source-fetch consolidated into `.clone_repo` anchor** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`, closes services/loom-core#80): backports the `.clone_repo: &clone_repo` anchor pattern that sister services (flexdeck, jobsearch-app) already use, so every job's source-fetch path is consistent. Replaces the inline `git init / fetch / checkout` block in `.go-template`'s before_script and the duplicated defensive copy inside `guardrails:docs-cli`'s script with a single `*clone_repo` reference. Also switches the "skip clone" guard from the brittle `[ ! -f go.mod ]` test to `git rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`, which correctly detects the runner-fetched-via-tarball case where `go.mod` exists but `.git` does not — the exact divergence that previously made `guardrails:docs-cli` need its own duplicated clone block.
- **`gitlab.flexinfer.ai/libs/mcp-go` pinned to current libs/mcp-go!main HEAD** (`go.mod`, `go.sum`, advances services/loom-core#82): bumps the pin from `d2e5fba8ab13` (2026-03-03) to `7ff2221818cd` (2026-03-17, current `main` HEAD of the upstream repo). Picks up `feat(transport): add SSE GET endpoint for Streamable HTTP server`, `fix(transport): add request body size limit to Streamable HTTP`, and `chore(ci): integrate tech-radar scanner` — all already on `libs/mcp-go!main` but absent here. The other two internal pins (`fi-accel/go/fiaccel` at `ce3294e404e0`, `fi-mcp-kit` at `1a41094d2496`) were already at their respective `main` HEADs (verified via `glab api projects/<lib>/repository/commits/<sha>/refs?type=branch`). Closes the mcp-go portion of the CI-stabilization "align internal module pins with mainline published revisions" item.

### Fixed
- **APNs token invalidation actually removes stale tokens (MBL-7)** (`internal/hud/apns_sender.go`, `internal/hud/apns_sender_test.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, `internal/hud/otel_metrics.go`, advances services/loom-core#36): `APNsSender.Send` previously logged "APNs token invalidated" on 404/410 responses but never actually called `DeviceTokenStore.Invalidate`, so expired iOS tokens kept generating noisy 410s on every subsequent push. The store wasn't even reachable from the sender. New `WithTokenStore(*DeviceTokenStore)` setter wires the registry through, and the 404/410 branch now removes the token, increments a `push_token_invalidated_total` OTel counter, sets a `push.token_removed` span attribute, and includes `removed_from_store` in the warn log. New `apns_sender_test.go` covers the 410, 404, success, transient-5xx, and nil-store paths via a `RoundTripper` injection on the existing `http.Client`. Operationalizes the "Token invalidation cleanup path" acceptance criterion from MBL-7's M4/M5 checklist.

### Changed
- **HUD `/api/otel` polling consolidated onto shared `otelStore`** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/otel.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{OverviewPanel,ServersPanel}.svelte`, closes services/loom-core#54): `OverviewPanel` and `ServersPanel` previously each kept their own `otelStatus`/`otelInfo` `$state`, fetcher, and `setInterval(..., 30_000)` against `/api/otel`. Both panels now read from the existing shared `otelStore` via `$derived`, and the store gained an owner-set polling pattern (refcounted) so multiple panels can independently start/stop without tearing down each other's feed — same shape as `fleetStore` / `tracesStore`. Net result: one OTel timer + one source of truth, even when both panels are mounted; consistent `OTel on/off` rendering across the daemon-tile and ServersPanel observability card.

### Fixed
- **Claude Code Monitor tool permission** (`mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `pkg/generator/configs_claude.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_test.go`): `Monitor` now ships in the Claude Code permission allowlist emitted by `loom sync claude --regen`. Previously every `Monitor` call (streaming events from a long-running script, or until-loop one-shot waits) hit a prompt; ad-hoc additions to `~/.claude/settings.json` were undone on the next sync because the registry + generator regex didn't know about the tool. Registry YAML `platform_permissions.claude.allow` gains the entry; `claudePermissionRuleRegexp` RE2 fallback lists `Monitor` so `filterClaudePermissionRules` keeps it when the embedded upstream schema (which uses lookaheads Go can't compile) isn't available; the "all known tool names" filter test is updated.

### Fixed
- **iOS Agents tab "Ungrouped runtime" regression** (`internal/hud/domain/mobile/{handler_agents,types}.go`, `internal/contracts/{golden_test.go,testdata/mobile_{agents,sessions}.golden}`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/Models/{SessionInfo,UnifiedAgent}.swift`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/ViewModels/AgentsViewModel.swift`): live Claude Code and Codex agents were all bucketing into "Ungrouped runtime" on the mobile Agents tab because the client grouped by project→namespace→branch only, and the server's `/mobile/agents` response didn't carry the session hierarchy fields (`parent_session_id`, `root_session_id`) that the web HUD's `PresenceAgentsTab.groupKeyFor` already uses. Server now populates both from the session lookup on the unified agent DTO; client decoders add backwards-compatible `decodeIfPresent` for both. iOS `AgentsViewModel.groupDescriptor` now groups by `session:<root>` first (with a codex-infrastructure override for keepalive-wrapper / heartbeat-bootstrap sessions), keeping the existing project/namespace/branch fallbacks for presence-only agents and replacing the terminal "Ungrouped runtime" bucket with per-`agent_id` rows. Contract goldens exercise a parent+root subagent pair so the wire shape is frozen.

### Added
- **`hud.workflow.waiting_approval` SSE emission for mobile push flow** (`internal/hud/monitor/workflows.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/workflows_test.go`): closes the last missing link in the iOS approvals push path. `WorkflowMonitor` now detects per-step transitions into `waiting_approval` and fires a new `OnNewApproval` callback (deduped via the existing `notifiedApprovals` map, re-armed once the workflow leaves the waiting state); `embed.go` wires the callback to broadcast a `hud.workflow.waiting_approval` SSE event carrying `workflow_id`, `name`, and `current_step`. The existing `PushEventBridge.classifyWorkflowApproval` already consumes this payload and delivers via APNs — combined with the iOS companion's `loom://workflow/{id}/approve` deep-link handler, the end-to-end approval gate → push → tap-to-approve loop now actually fires. Previously every piece of the stack was wired except the emitter. Tests cover initial transition, dedup on unchanged state, re-entry after resolution, and the no-workflows-waiting no-op case.

### Fixed
- **`mcp-hub-wrapper` teardown reconnect race** (`cmd/mcp-hub-wrapper/main.go`): `recvLoop` previously called `ensureHub()` every iteration, so when the upstream hub connection failed during wrapper shutdown (e.g. the server closing its side after a test's `releaseHub` unblocks, or any production teardown path) it would dial a fresh connection before the main bridge had a chance to observe stdin EOF. Under CPU-constrained runners (`GOMAXPROCS=1`-class) this beat the `TestRun_HubRecvCloseRetriesRequestWithoutClosingWrapper` / `TestRun_ReconnectsAndReplaysInitialization` expected-two-connections barrier, surfacing as a `test:race` flake. Reconnection is now owned by `sendHubMessage` (new client request) and `retryInFlight` (explicit in-flight retry); `recvLoop` waits on a `hubReady` signal channel or `ctx` cancellation instead of racing to dial. `bridge()` also scopes a child `bridgeCtx` that cancels on return, and `reconnect()` short-circuits on `ctx.Err()`. No behavior change on the happy path.

### Added
- **Vendor lifecycle contract pinned in a test + doc** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks_test.go`, `docs/AGENT_LIFECYCLE.md`): new `TestVendorLifecycleContract` asserts that Claude Code, Gemini CLI, and Codex CLI each emit the expected session-start / session-end / heartbeat invocations for their hook surface — a silent drop of any event during a future generator refactor will fail CI. For Claude and Gemini, the test pins the event names (`SessionStart` / `Stop` vs `SessionStart` / `SessionEnd`, `PostToolUse` vs `AfterTool`) and requires `--ensure-session` on the heartbeat. For Codex, it asserts the `notify = [...]` entry invokes `loom agent keepalive-wrap` with `--ensure-session --session-id --agent-type codex --infer-namespace` and does not invent fake event names (Codex has no native session-start/session-end — the orphan reaper from slice B compensates). New `docs/AGENT_LIFECYCLE.md` documents the per-vendor lifecycle model, the gap in Codex (no exit hook; `notify` fires on turn completion only), and the upcoming `codex_hooks` feature flag that will close the gap when it goes GA. No behavior change — this is the regression guard for slices A+B.

### Fixed
- **Orphan agent presence detection and auto-reap** (`internal/hud/fleetview/fleetview.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_{dto,session}.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/coordination/coordination.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mobile/{handler_agents,types}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{utils/agents.ts,components/FleetPanel.svelte}`): follow-up to the canonical session join. Agents heartbeating without an active session (the "9 live without session" screenshot) are now first-class in the model. `fleetview.Join` computes `IsOrphan` + `OrphanAgeSeconds` as derived fields — an orphan is a presence row with `HasPresence && !HasSession` that has persisted past `OrphanStaleAfter` (120s grace window, long enough to cover normal session-start bootstrap). The fleet monitor counts `snap.OrphanAgents` on every refresh and fires a fire-and-forget `reapOrphans` goroutine for rows past the reap threshold (10min) with a 2min per-agent cooldown; reaped presence rows drop out of the fleet view on the next refresh. Coordination attaches `"orphan without session"` to the agent's `AttentionReasons`, so the HUD attention list flags the condition. Frontend surfaces an ORPHAN chip (warning color) next to PRESENCE / SESSION pills and a `N orphans · M bootstrapping` sub-metric on the Sessions stat card. Mobile API emits `is_orphan` / `orphan_age_seconds` per agent and `summary.orphans`.

### Fixed
- **HUD Fleet session grouping divergence** (`internal/hud/fleetview/` new, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mobile/handler_agents.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{utils/agents.ts,components/FleetPanel.svelte}`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_presence.go`): the Fleet panel counter ("N sessions · M live without session") disagreed with per-row SESSION badges whenever a presence row carried a stale `has_session=true` from a prior snapshot — badges lit up while the counter showed 0. Root cause: `has_session` was a *stored* DTO field set in two independent correlators (fleet monitor + mobile handler) and never cleared; the frontend then fell back to `agent.has_session ?? !!session`, preserving the stale truth. This change introduces `internal/hud/fleetview` with one canonical `Join(presences, sessions, now)` function used by both the fleet monitor and the mobile `/agents` handler; `HasSession` / `SessionID` / `SessionStatus` / `SessionStartedAt` / `SessionAgeSeconds` are now *derived* at join time and reset on every call, never trusted across snapshots. Frontend `buildUnifiedAgents` stops trusting `agent.has_session` and computes purely from the live sessions array, so badge and counter read the same signal by construction. Heartbeat now overwrites `presence.SessionID` whenever a non-empty `session_id` is supplied (previously only on first write) so the binding follows the agent's current session after a re-bind. Drops ~170 lines of duplicated correlation logic.

### Added
- **Autonomous-refactor DAG template + schema validator (F7 v1)** (`cmd/mcp-mentatlab/templates/autonomous-refactor.yaml`, `pkg/mentatlab/autonomous_validator.go`, `internal/spawn/mentatlab_adapter.go`): in-repo DAG template for `plan → spawn → verify → review_gate → commit/push/open_pr`, plus a standalone Go validator that asserts every write-typed node (`shell`, `agent_spawn`, `git_*`) lies on a start-to-terminal path crossing a `human_gate` or `review_gate`. New `AgentTypeMentatLabNode` constant + `DispatchDAGNode` stub on the spawn orchestrator (logs "would spawn DAG node"; actual engine integration deferred to F7 v2 since `cmd/mcp-mentatlab/` is a thin MCP proxy, not the DAG engine).
- **Token-economics dashboard (F8)** (`internal/hud/domain/fleet/economics.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/EconomicsPanel.svelte`): new `GET /api/fleet/economics?window=7d` endpoint derives 6 ratios (token savings, tool-call reduction, cost ratio, context waste, compression ratio, local utilization) from existing `SpawnTelemetry.TotalCostUSD` + weaver `TokensTotal` counters. Pure derivation — no new recording. Svelte panel mounts on the Fleet page alongside the Slice E1 claim-conflict chip; renders 6 ratio cards + an inline-SVG stacked bar of frontier vs local tokens. Ratios that would divide by zero render as "—". Tests cover all-zero, mixed-inputs, and divide-by-zero cases.
- **MCP tool refresh on upstream reconnect (Slice G)** (`internal/daemon/tool_refresh_debounce.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon_dispatch.go`): Daemon now advertises `tools.listChanged: true` in the initialize response and schedules `refreshToolCache()` with a 3s debounce whenever an upstream MCP server's pool is cleared (pod restart, process crash, network blip). The existing `EventToolsChanged` → `notifications/tools/list_changed` path then fires automatically so spec-compliant clients can re-fetch `tools/list` without restarting. New JSON-RPC method `loom/tools/reload` provides a manual escape hatch. Tests cover debounce coalescing (20 rapid calls → 1 fire), Stop cancellation, and nil-safety. Follow-up: wrap `loom/tools/reload` as an MCP tool for clients that don't honor the notification.
- **Vendor spec drift check (Slice H)** (`cmd/loom/cmd_vendor_specs.go`, `pkg/generator/vendor_specs.{go,yaml,_test.go}`): new `loom vendor-specs check [--json] [--manifest PATH]` command reads a human-editable manifest of vendor doc URLs + expected keys per CLI platform, fetches each doc, and asserts (a) required substrings appear, (b) deprecated/invalid tokens do not, and (c) every emitted key appears in `pkg/generator/configs_test.go`. Catches the class of regression that caused the 2026-04-18 Codex fix. Seed manifest covers codex, claude_code, gemini_cli, kilocode; supports adding antigravity/zed/vscode. Follow-up H2 (scheduled CI job) deferred.

### Fixed
- **Codex Loom auto-approval regression** (`pkg/generator/configs_formats.go`): Regression from commit 848be7ef (2026-04-09) emitted `approval_mode = "always"` in Codex `mcp_servers.<name>` stanzas — not a valid Codex config key, so Codex fell back to prompt-every-tool behavior. Now emits the correct `default_tools_approval_mode = "approve"` per openai/codex#16501 and https://developers.openai.com/codex/mcp. Run `loom sync codex --regen` to refresh local `~/.codex/config.toml`.
- **`agent_worktree_allocate` repo resolution** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_worktree.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_worktree.go`, closes services/loom-core#83): the tool previously failed with an opaque `fatal: not a git repository (or any of the parent directories): .git` when `AGENT_CONTEXT_GIT_REPO_PATH` / `REPO_PATH` wasn't set on the deployed agent-context server. Allocate now resolves the repo in priority order — explicit `repo_path` arg > `session.WorkingDir` > `cfg.GitRepoPath` — and validates each candidate with `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` before calling `git worktree add`. On failure the error lists every source that was tried plus the three ways to fix it.
- **Docs guardrail false-positives (DEBT-065)** (`scripts/ci/check_docs_guardrails.sh`, `scripts/ci/check_docs_guardrails_test.sh`): the `guardrails:docs-cli` job now classifies Go/Python test files (`*_test.go`, `*_mock.go`, `/mocks/`, `test_*.py`, `*_test.py`) as non-documentation-significant, alongside the existing `/dist/`, `/testdata/`, `_golden.`, `.min.js/css`, `.snap` exclusions. True user-facing code still fails the check without a CHANGELOG/README/docs update. New `check_docs_guardrails_test.sh` exercises 14 cases (pure-test, generated, genuine code, mixed) against temp git repos and runs in `make ci-guardrails` and the `guardrails:docs-cli` CI job.

### Changed
- **`mcp-neo4j` migrated onto `mcpscaffold` (DEBT-066)** (`cmd/mcp-neo4j/main.go`): first server in the cycle-6 wave-2 "migrate the largest remaining MCP entrypoints" batch. Replaces hand-rolled `mcplog`/`mcpotel` setup with a single `mcpscaffold.NewServer()` call and folds each `server.AddTool(..., mcpotel.TracedToolHandler(tracer, name, fn))` into the scaffold's shorter `srv.AddTracedTool(tool, fn)` helper. Tool names, descriptions, input schemas, env-driven connection config, degraded-mode startup, and driver close defer are all preserved — no operator-visible change. 902 → 876 LOC.
- **`mcp-argocd` migrated onto `mcpscaffold` (DEBT-066)** (`cmd/mcp-argocd/main.go`): second server in the batch. Same scaffold migration as `mcp-neo4j`: folds `mcplog` + `mcpotel` startup into `mcpscaffold.NewServer()` and converts all 16 `server.AddTool(..., mcpotel.TracedToolHandler(...))` registrations to `srv.AddTracedTool(...)`. Preserves all tool names/schemas, `ARGOCD_SERVER`/`ARGOCD_AUTH_TOKEN`/`ARGOCD_INSECURE` env wiring, and the existing `httpclient.Client` + `argocdRequest` API path. 904 → 889 LOC.
- **`mcp-terraform` migrated onto `mcpscaffold` (DEBT-066)** (`cmd/mcp-terraform/main.go`): third server in the batch. Same scaffold migration: folds `mcplog` + `mcpotel` startup into `mcpscaffold.NewServer()` and converts all 14 tool registrations to `srv.AddTracedTool(...)`. Preserves all tool names/schemas, `TFC_HOST`/`TFC_TOKEN`/`TFC_ORGANIZATION`/`TFC_SKIP_VERIFY` env wiring, the 60s httpclient timeout, and the existing API paths. 945 → 924 LOC.

### Added
- **Live file-claim conflict overlay (F9)** (`pkg/agentcontext/file_claims_conflict_bus.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_claims_stream.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/ClaimConflictChip.svelte`): in-process `ConflictBus` (non-blocking fan-out, drop-on-full) + new `GET /api/fleet/claims/stream` SSE endpoint + Svelte chip mounted on the Fleet panel. `svc_claims.go` `Acquire` publishes a `ClaimConflictEvent` on collision. Target collision→UI latency <500ms. Tests cover subscribe/publish/unsubscribe/drop/race.

### Fixed
- **HUD readout correctness (U1/U2/U3/U10)** (`.loom/89-hud-ux-issues-from-screenshots-2026-04-17.md`): Now-view top instrument relabeled `Health` → `Running` to match underlying `healthyCount/serverCount` semantics (U1). Coordination `Attention` / `Risk` chips zero out when no hotspots present (U2). Servers panel latency column renders em-dash when `ms <= 0` instead of a misleading `<1ms` (U3). Fleet `.fleet-table-card` gets `max-height: 60vh; overflow-y: auto` so the existing `Showing X of Y` DataTable footer is no longer clipped (U10).

### Added
- **Auto-handoff triggers (F5)** (`pkg/agentcontext/handoff_triggers.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`): stateful `TriggerGate` fires on the SECOND consecutive breach of an input-token / cost / stalled-duration threshold within the same spawn session, with a debounce (default 10m) after each fire. `MaybeAutoHandoff` on the agentcontext service creates a handoff tagged `source="auto"` + `auto_reason`. Spawn budget watcher gained a nil-safe `AutoHandoffHook` interface so `internal/hud/spawn.go` stays dependency-light. v1 does NOT auto-accept. Env flags default to disabled (`AGENTCONTEXT_HANDOFF_ENABLED=false`). Metrics: `loom_handoff_trigger_{fired,suppressed}_total{reason}`.
- **Fleet task queue + capability-aware dispatch router (F6)** (`pkg/agentcontext/dispatch.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_tasks.go`, `mcp/context/agent-capabilities.yaml`): pure-function `ChooseAgent` scorer (capability-must-match, lowest-load wins, deterministic tie-break) plus new MCP tool `agent_task_dispatch` returning `{agent_id, reason, candidates_considered}` — v1 returns the decision only (no spawn). `Task` gains `CapabilityNeeded []string` + `Scope` fields. Capability seed YAML loaded from `mcp/context/agent-capabilities.yaml` with `LOOM_AGENT_CAPABILITIES_FILE` override; missing file degrades gracefully. Metrics: `loom_fleet_dispatch_{requests,mismatch}_total`.
- **LLM-backed auto-compaction (F2)** (`pkg/agentcontext/compaction_{llm,pinned,audit}.go`): optional synthesis path for compaction triggers. `CompactionConfig` gains `Mode` (`"extractive"` default, `"llm"` opt-in), `PinRawFor` (default 1h), and `MaxSynthesisTokens`. `CompactionSummarizer` interface + `NewCoordinatorSummarizer` adapter for `POST /api/coordinator/compress`; on error falls back to extractive and increments `loom_agentcontext_compaction_fallback_total`. `MemoryPinnedStore` keeps raw entries readable during the pin window. Follow-ups: confirm coordinator contract shape + persist audit entries through `ctxSvc.Add`.
- **Weaver auto-compose for unmatched queries (F10)** (`pkg/weaver/auto_compose.go`): when `weaver_query` hits a `no_match` intent AND `WEAVER_AUTO_COMPOSE_ENABLED=true`, pick up to `WEAVER_AUTO_COMPOSE_MAX_DOMAINS` (default 3) domains by keyword scoring against `SubAgent.Description` and dispatch in parallel through the existing router path. Domains flagged `write: true` on their `SubAgent` are never auto-selected. Metrics: `loom_weaver_auto_compose_total{outcome}` + `loom_weaver_auto_compose_domains_used`.
- **Recall reranker (F1)** (`pkg/agentcontext/reranker*.go`): pluggable `Reranker` interface with FlexInfer (`/v1/rerank` proxy), BGE cross-encoder, and Noop backends.
